# SPEC-002: Generators & registry

**Crate**: `crates/sciart-generators` · **Milestones**: M1 (registry, starfield), M4–M7 (batches), M10 (Batch E), M11 (titles, taxonomy) · **Status**: M1, M4–M7 (Batches A–D) and M10 (Batch E, `gray_scott` upgrade) implemented; GEN-R12..R15 specified for M11

Ports the Python stability contract (`docs/archive/issue-01-simulator-stability.md`, R1) to Rust
and extends it for real-time animation.

## Registry
- **GEN-R1** `registry() -> &'static [Entry]` lists every generator; `Entry { meta: &'static Meta, build: fn() -> Box<dyn Generator> }`. Names are unique, `snake_case`, and sorted.
- **GEN-R2** `find(name) -> Option<&'static Entry>` is exact-match on `meta.name`, or on a retired name (GEN-R14).
- **GEN-R3** Adding a generator requires exactly: one module file + one line in the registry macro. The contract suite picks it up automatically.

## Stability contract (every registered generator)
- **GEN-R4** Never panics for any `Size` with `w, h >= 1` across any sequence of `resize`, `update(dt)` (`0 <= dt <= 0.25`), and `render` calls — including resizes between any two calls.
- **GEN-R5** Dimension-dependent state is rebuilt in `resize`; `update`/`render` must tolerate the smallest size (1×1).
- **GEN-R6** Determinism: same seed + same call sequence ⇒ byte-identical `Canvas::to_lines()`.
- **GEN-R7** No wall-clock reads (`Instant`, `SystemTime`) and no global RNG (`rand::rng()`/`thread_rng`) inside generators; time comes from `dt`/`ctx.time`, randomness from `ctx.rng`. Enforced by `clippy.toml` `disallowed-methods`.
- **GEN-R8** Performance: `update(1/60)` + `render` < 5 ms at 200×50 in release (criterion bench per generator).
- **GEN-R9** Motion: over 1 simulated second at 200×50 the rendered frame changes (a scene is an animation, not a still). Verified by the contract suite.
- **GEN-R10** Unicode glyphs must have an ASCII fallback (`Glyph::new(u, a)`); ASCII mode output is pure ASCII.
- **GEN-R11** After warm-up (build, resize, ≥ 1 step and one render), `render` performs zero heap allocations, and `update(1/60)` performs zero allocations in steady state unless the scene is listed with a reason in `UPDATE_ALLOCATES` of `crates/sciart-generators/tests/alloc_free.rs` (empty today). Resizing may allocate. Verified at 200×50 and 80×24, in both glyph modes, by that test (ADR-0009).

## Titles, categories and names (M11)
Design: `docs/design/M11-titles-taxonomy.md` §1–§3 · ADR-0013 (taxonomy) · ADR-0014 (titles). Mechanism: CORE-R71..R77.
- **GEN-R12** Every registered scene has a valid science title (ADR-0014). The contract suite checks `meta.title` and every entry of `meta.captions`:
  - the title contains exactly one ` · `, with non-empty, trimmed text on both sides;
  - its width is ≤ 32 cells and the phenomenon's is ≤ 20 cells (`Title::width`, CORE-R72);
  - each caption is non-empty, trimmed and has no ` · `, and `phenomenon · caption` is ≤ 32 cells;
  - every char is in the title charset (`title::ascii_of(c).is_some()`), and none of `= ^ _ * / < >` appears (no formulas or markup);
  - titles are unique across the registry;
  - `caption()` sampled at t = 0, 1, 5 and 20 s (seed 42, 80×24) is `None` or an entry of `meta.captions`, and a scene with empty `captions` always returns `None`.

  A title never claims more than the model does; wording is signed off by the science review.
- **GEN-R13** The bottom-left belongs to the title. With the title hidden (`Stage::set_title_visible(false)`), at 80×24 and 40×12, seed 42, t = 0, 1 and 5 s, in Unicode glyph mode, no cell of row `h − 1` left of where the longest `title::readout_room` readout may start (the span of `Title::of(scene)` plus the 4-cell gap, CORE-R76) holds an alphanumeric char. Only text is excluded: half-block, braille and field art may fill the row. A scene that places a readout on row `h − 1` right-aligns it with `draw::label_right` (CORE-R76) and shortens or drops it so that at least 4 blank cells separate it from the title's text: forms are chosen against `title::readout_room` (CORE-R76) with the live caption, whether or not the title is drawn. Other scene text (names, epithets) stays off row `h − 1` while the title is shown, so it never reads as part of the title. The moves below supersede the bottom-left placements written in the scene sections.

  | Scene | M11 change |
  |-------|-----------|
  | action_potential | Readout (`HH squid …`) right-aligned on row `h − 1`; its form follows the room right of the title. |
  | belousov_zhabotinsky | The name `Belousov-Zhabotinsky` is dropped (the title names the phenomenon); the beat word and `b=…` are right-aligned, forms chosen by the room, capped at half the row. |
  | boids_flocking | `N 192  k=7` right-aligned. |
  | brownian_motion | MSD readout right-aligned, parts two spaces apart; when it would reach the title, trailing parts are dropped (`t = …s` first, then the true D); hidden when the estimator alone does not fit. |
  | cosmic_microwave_background | `CMB  T=2.725K  dT~1e-5` right-aligned instead of centred. |
  | double_pendulum | Divergence and energy readout right-aligned instead of centred. |
  | doppler_effect | The legend describes sound and is right-aligned, longest form that fits: `<- lower pitch  v = 0.5 c_s  higher pitch ->`, `<- lower  v = 0.5 c_s  higher ->`, `v = 0.5 c_s`. |
  | electron_orbitals | Orbital name and transition readouts right-aligned. |
  | fractal_growth | The phrase `diffusion-limited aggregation` is dropped (the title says it); `p_s …   N …   D_f …` right-aligned; whole parts are dropped from the left, D_f last. |
  | molecular_dynamics | `T = … heating  U/N …  LJ reduced units` right-aligned, shortened by dropping `LJ reduced units`, then `U/N … N …`, then the target temperature. |
  | physarum | `SA … RA … SO …` right-aligned. |
  | solar_surface | `day …  Teff 5772 K` right-aligned (Teff still dropped first when narrow). |
  | strange_attractors | The attractor name becomes `caption()`; `meta.captions` = `Lorenz`, `Aizawa`, `Thomas`, `Halvorsen`, `Rössler`, and `meta.title` is `Strange attractor · chaos`. The parameter set, when it fits, is right-aligned. |
  | constellation_map | The α-star label and the constellation name are clamped to rows ≤ `h − 2`; on row `h − 1` the epithet is nudged right past the title span, and dropped if it no longer fits. |
  | moon_phases | The phase name is clamped to rows ≤ `h − 2`. |
- **GEN-R14** Renamed scenes keep their old names as aliases: `ALIASES: &[(&str, &str)]` maps `chemical_reaction → gray_scott` and `crystal_growth → fractal_growth`. `find(name)` tries `meta.name` first, then an alias, so `sciart run <old name>` and `disabled = ["<old name>"]` (CFG-R7) keep working. Every alias target is registered, and no alias equals a registered name. `registry()` and `sciart list` show only current names. Acceptance IDs keep their prefixes (`CHEM-A*`, `CRY-A*`).
- **GEN-R15** Each scene section heading in this file reads `### <name>  (<category label>, <milestones>)`. Every registered scene has exactly one such section, and its label equals `meta.category.label()` (CORE-R77), which is how each scene's category (ADR-0013) is written down. Checked by a test that parses this file.

## Per-generator spec template
Each scene gets a short section appended below when its batch starts:

```
### <name>  (category label, batch)
Intent: what the viewer should see/feel.
Model: state, physics/maths, parameters.
Motion: what changes over time and at what rate.
Resize: what is rebuilt.
Inks: roles used.
Acceptance: scene-specific assertions beyond GEN-R4..R10.
```

### starfield  (Deep Cosmos, M1)
- **Intent**: drifting parallax starfield with twinkling.
- **Model**: `N = clamp(area * 0.08, 1, 4000)` stars with `x, y ∈ [0, size)`, `layer ∈ {1..4}` (4 = nearest), `phase ∈ [0, 2π)`, `rate ∈ [1.5, 4]` rad/s.
- **Motion**: `x += layer * SPEED * dt` wrapping at width (parallax: nearer = faster); brightness = layer base + twinkle `sin(phase + rate * t)`.
- **Resize**: star field regenerated for the new area.
- **Inks**: `Role::Star`, level from brightness.
- **Acceptance**: (`STAR-A1`) star count follows the formula; (`STAR-A2`) nearer layers move farther than farther layers over the same dt.

---
## Batch A — analytic / time-driven scenes (M4)
Shared helpers: `sciart_core::draw` (CORE-R22..R27). All lengths below are in **rows**; x is stretched by `ASPECT`. `R = draw::fit_radius(size, frac)`.

### solar_system  (Sun & Planets, M4)
- **Intent**: the eight planets circling the Sun at physically ordered speeds, with an asteroid belt.
- **Model**: orbit radii `R(0.95) · {.14,.22,.30,.40,.60,.74,.87,1.0}`; Kepler's third law `ω(a) = (2π/12 s) · (a/a⊕)^-1.5` (Earth year = 12 s); random initial phases; 60 belt particles at `0.48–0.54` of the outer radius.
- **Motion**: every body advances `ω·dt`. **Resize**: radii recomputed, phases kept.
- **Inks**: Sun `Sun`, planets `Planet`, orbits `Dim`, belt `Particle`.
- **Acceptance**: `SOL-A1` ω strictly decreases outward and Earth's period is 12 s; `SOL-A2` every planet stays on-canvas at any time (200×50); `SOL-A3` the Sun's core sits on the center cell.

### pulsar  (Deep Cosmos, M4)
- **Intent**: a lighthouse neutron star — two opposite radiation cones sweeping, dipole field loops, a flash each time a beam faces the viewer.
- **Model**: spin period 2.5 s, beams at `θ(t)` and `θ+π`; cone half-angle 0.12 rad; dipole field lines `r = L·sin²φ` about the beam (magnetic) axis for `L ∈ {0.35, 0.6, 0.85}·R(1)`; the viewer sits at angle `-π/2` (screen up) and a flash lasts while a beam is within 0.2 rad of it.
- **Inks**: beams `Wave` (level falls off with distance and off-axis angle), core `Hot`, field `Dim`, flash halo `Accent`.
- **Acceptance**: `PUL-A1` the beams are exactly π apart and advance by `2π/2.5` rad/s; `PUL-A2` exactly two flashes per rotation; `PUL-A3` field-line points satisfy `r = L sin²φ`.

### moon_phases  (Earth & Sky, M4)
- **Intent**: a lit sphere going through a full lunation, with craters and the phase name.
- **Model**: synodic cycle 30 s, phase `p ∈ [0,1)`; light direction `L = (sin 2πp, 0, −cos 2πp)`; surface normal `n = (nx, ny, √(1−nx²−ny²))`; illumination `n·L`; illuminated fraction `(1 − cos 2πp)/2`. Scene time starts at a seeded offset in `[0, 30)` s so runs open on different phases (still deterministic per seed).
- **Inks**: lit `Star` (level ∝ illumination), limb of the dark side `Dim`, label `Text`.
- **Acceptance**: `MOON-A1` new moon (p=0) has no lit point on the visible hemisphere, full moon (p=0.5) lights every interior point; `MOON-A2` names: 0 New Moon, .25 First Quarter, .5 Full Moon, .75 Last Quarter, .97 New Moon; `MOON-A3` illuminated fraction is 0, ½, 1, ½ at p = 0, ¼, ½, ¾.

### exoplanet_transit  (Sun & Planets, M4)
- **Intent**: a planet crossing a limb-darkened star while the light curve draws the dip.
- **Model**: star radius `Rs`, planet `rp = max(0.12 Rs, 0.5)`, impact parameter `b = 0.3 Rs`, period 10 s along `u ∈ [−2.2 Rs, 2.2 Rs]`; flux `1 − A(d; Rs, rp)/(π Rs²)` with `A` the exact circle–circle overlap area; limb darkening `I(μ) = 1 − 0.6(1 − μ)` for shading only. The light-curve panel (right 38 % of the screen, only if ≥ 12×6 cells) plots one full period with a marker at the current phase.
- **Inks**: star `Sun`, planet `Planet` (off-disk) / blank silhouette (on-disk), curve `Accent`, axes `Structure`.
- **Acceptance**: `TRAN-A1` flux = 1 when the disks don't touch; `TRAN-A2` flux = `1 − (rp/Rs)²` when the planet is fully inside; `TRAN-A3` overlap is symmetric in its radii and non-increasing in `d`.

### satellite_orbit  (Voyages, M4)
- **Intent**: a rotating Earth (day/night, oceans, continents, ice caps) with satellites on inclined orbits passing in front of and behind it.
- **Model**: Earth radius `Re = R(0.45)`, spin period 20 s, procedural continents from seeded sinusoids on (lon, lat); 5 satellites with `a ∈ [1.25, 2.2]·Re`, inclination `∈ [0.2, 1.2]` rad from the screen plane (radii and inclinations drawn stratified so orbits spread out), node angle random, `ω = (2π/6 s)·(a/Re)^-1.5`. 3-D position `(a cosθ, a sinθ cos i, a sinθ sin i)` rotated by the node angle in the screen plane; `z < 0` is behind.
- **Inks**: ocean `Cold`, land `Particle`, ice `Text`, night side dimmed, satellites `Accent`, orbit paths `Dim`; extras: sun-side atmosphere glow `Cold`, sparse background stars `Dim`.
- **Acceptance**: `SAT-A1` a satellite is hidden iff `z < 0` and its screen distance from Earth's center is `< Re`; `SAT-A2` ω decreases with `a`; `SAT-A3` `|position| = a`.

### doppler_effect  (Forces & Waves, M4)
- **Intent**: a moving source compressing wavefronts ahead (higher pitch) and stretching them behind (lower pitch).
- **Model**: wave speed `c = 10` rows/s, source speed `v = 0.5c` left→right (wraps with margin), one wavefront every `T = 0.35 s`, each front a circle of radius `c·(t − t₀)` around its emission point; fronts are dropped once larger than the screen diagonal.
- **Inks**: each front point is coloured by its direction θ from its own emission point — `Cold` (higher pitch, `cos θ > 0`) else `Hot`; on the axis this is exactly "ahead of the source ⇒ `Cold`", and it stays correct off-axis and after the source wraps; source `Accent`.
- **Acceptance**: `DOP-A1` in steady state the spacing of fronts along the axis is `(c − v)T` ahead and `(c + v)T` behind; `DOP-A2` the number of live fronts is bounded by `diag/(cT) + 2`.

### wave_interference  (Forces & Waves, M4)
- **Intent**: two coherent point sources whose ripples add up into bright and dark bands.
- **Model**: sources at `(0.25 w, h/2 ± s/2)`, `s = 0.35 h` rows; wavelength `λ = max(3, h/6)` rows; `ω = 3` rad/s; amplitude `a = sin(k d₁ − ωt) + sin(k d₂ − ωt)`, glyph from `(a + 2)/4`.
- **Inks**: `Wave`, level ∝ `(a + 2)/4`; sources `Accent`; title `Text` (only if `h > 8`).
- **Acceptance**: `WAVE-A1` on the perpendicular bisector the amplitude is `2 sin(kd − ωt)`; `WAVE-A2` where the path difference is `λ/2` the amplitude is ≈ 0 at all times.

### cosmic_microwave_background  (Deep Cosmos, M4)
- **Intent**: the Planck-style oval sky map: smooth hot/cold anisotropies that slowly shimmer (not per-frame noise).
- **Model**: an isotropic Gaussian random field by spectral synthesis, `f = (1/N) Σ sin(kᵢ·(x/ASPECT, y) + φᵢ + ωᵢ t)` with N = 96 equal-power modes; `|kᵢ|` stratified-sampled so their density in `ln ℓ` follows `D_ℓ·B_ℓ²` — `D_ℓ` an approximate Planck 2018 ΛCDM TT spectrum (Sachs–Wolfe plateau, first acoustic peak at ℓ = 220 ≈ 5× plateau, trough, 2nd/3rd peaks, damping), `B_ℓ² = exp(−(ℓ/400)²)` a "cell beam", ℓ ∈ [10, 760] — mapped to the screen as `|k| = (2π/6)·ℓ/220` rad/row (first peak = 6-row wavelength); directions from a jittered golden-ratio sequence over [0, π) along the ℓ-sorted modes; phases uniform; `|ωᵢ| ∈ [0.08, 0.35]` rad/s with random sign (slow shimmer, no drift); display saturates at `|f| = 2.7σ`, `σ = 1/√(2N)` (Planck colour bar ±300 μK ≈ ±2.7 σ_CMB); drawn only inside an ellipse covering 94 % of the screen (Mollweide-like); caption `CMB  T=2.725K  dT~1e-5` on the last row when `h > 6`.
- **Inks**: `f > 0` ⇒ `Hot`, else `Cold`, level ∝ `min(|f|/2.7σ, 1)`.
- **Acceptance**: `CMB-A1` `|f| ≤ 1`; `CMB-A2` smooth — horizontally adjacent cells differ by at most `Σ|kₓᵢ| / (ASPECT·N)`, and adjacent-cell correlation is ≥ 0.9 horizontally and ≥ 0.7 vertically; `CMB-A3` a 200×50 frame contains both hot and cold cells; `CMB-A4` isotropic, not stripy — the direction tensor `Σ k̂k̂ᵀ` has anisotropy ≤ 0.05 and the two-point correlation at equal aspect-corrected lags agrees across directions within 0.1 (mode ensemble at 0°/90°/±45°, 1–6 rows; and sampled on a 200×50 screen, horizontal vs vertical, 1–3 rows); `CMB-A5` CMB-like spectrum — `D_ℓ` peaks at ℓ ∈ [200, 240] with `D_220/D_10 ∈ [4.5, 6]`, a trough then a second peak, and damping; the most populated Δln ℓ = 0.5 band of modes is centred at ℓ ∈ [140, 300].

### gravitational_lensing  (Deep Cosmos, M4)
- **Intent**: background stars drifting behind a massive lens, each seen as two images, and a galaxy that periodically lines up into an Einstein ring.
- **Model**: point lens with Einstein radius `θE = R(0.35)`; lens equation `β = θ − θE² θ/|θ|²`; for a source at `β` the images are `θ± = β̂ (|β| ± √(|β|² + 4θE²))/2`; 160 seeded stars drifting at 0.6 rows/s (wrapping); an extended source (radius 1.5 rows) crossing near the lens every 12 s, rendered by inverse ray-shooting within `2.5 θE`. Images with `|μ| < 0.05` are not drawn (in practice only far minor images, which otherwise clutter the Einstein disc; real ones are ≈ `u⁻⁴` faint).
- **Inks**: images `Star` (level by magnification), ring/arcs `Accent`, lens `Structure`.
- **Acceptance**: `LENS-A1` both images map back to `β` through the lens equation; `LENS-A2` as `β → 0` both images approach radius `θE`; `LENS-A3` for `|β| ≫ θE` the primary image ≈ `β` and the secondary → 0.

### constellation_map  (Earth & Sky, M4)
- **Intent**: a star chart that traces one constellation at a time, then names it, over a twinkling sky.
- **Model**: 6 constellations (Orion, Ursa Major, Cassiopeia, Cygnus, Crux, Scorpius) as normalized star coordinates + edges, fitted to 80 % of the screen preserving shape; each is shown for 12 s: lines trace in over the first 4 s, the name appears after tracing; background stars `0.02·area` twinkle.
- **Data**: star positions from real J2000 RA/Dec and visual magnitudes, gnomonic projection about the figure centre (east left, north up); only stars on the conventional stick figure are included (e.g. ε Cru omitted).
- **Inks**: constellation stars `Star`, lines `Structure`, tracing pen tip `Accent`, name `Text`, background `Dim`.
- **Acceptance**: `CON-A1` every edge references valid stars and every star is on at least one edge; `CON-A2` tracing progress 0 draws no segment, 1 draws all, and drawn length is monotone in progress; `CON-A3` the constellation index advances every 12 s and wraps.

---
## Batch B — particle systems (M5)
Shared helpers: `sciart_core::particles` (CORE-R28..R32). Pools are sized in `resize` from the area and are
bounded (GEN-R8); particles are respawned rather than reallocated. Positions are in **rows** unless stated
(x on screen = `cx + x·ASPECT`). Physics uses `dt` from `update` only.

### black_hole  (Deep Cosmos, M5)
- **Intent**: an Interstellar-style black hole: a dark shadow, a thin bright photon ring, and a tilted accretion disk whose far side is lensed up over the shadow; the approaching side glows brighter.
- **Model**: shadow radius `rs = R(0.14)`; ISCO `r_in = 2.2 rs`; disk out to `R(0.95)`; inclination 78° (disk ellipse flattening `cos i`); particles on circular orbits `ω(r) = kepler_omega(gm, r)` with the ISCO period fixed at 2.5 s; relativistic beaming for shading only: level ∝ `(1 + 0.55·β_los)³` with `β_los` the line-of-sight velocity fraction. Particles slowly drift inward (`dr/dt = −0.02 r`); inside `r_in` they plunge and are respawned at the outer edge. The far half of the disk is drawn at its weak-field lensed image positions (`θE² = 2·R_s·depth`): the bright primary is lifted into an arc over the shadow, a faint secondary appears below; there is no separate unlensed copy. Pool `clamp(area/6, 24, 1600)`, initially uniform in `ln r` (the steady state of the drift).
- **Inks**: disk `Hot` (level from radius and beaming), photon ring `Accent`, lensed arc `Hot` dimmer, shadow = blank.
- **Acceptance**: `BH-A1` ω strictly decreases with r and the ISCO period is 2.5 s; `BH-A2` no disk particle is drawn inside the shadow when it is behind the hole (far side, projected distance < rs); `BH-A3` the approaching half is brighter on average than the receding half; `BH-A4` the pool size stays constant after warm-up (respawn, no growth).

### galaxy_collision  (Deep Cosmos, M5)
- **Intent**: two spiral galaxies passing each other and pulling out tidal tails and bridges (Toomre & Toomre 1972).
- **Model**: restricted three-body: two cores (masses `M1 = 1`, `M2 = 0.8`) on a bound, eccentric, softened (`ε = 0.3` of disk radius) orbit integrated with kick-drift-kick leapfrog at `dt` sub-stepped to ≤ 1/120 s; massless test stars (`≈ 0.04·area`, max 900, split ∝ mass) start on circular orbits `v = √(G M r² / (r² + ε²)^{3/2})` in disks of radius `Rd`, one prograde and one inclined/retrograde, projected to the screen. Cores start at apocentre of an orbit with `a = 3.5 Rd`, `e = 0.63` (Kepler period mapped to 24 s). A cycle is 40 s (approach, pericentre ≈ 12 s, tails, second close passage ≈ 36 s — a conservative restricted three-body model cannot merge) then restarts from a re-seeded configuration (galaxy 2 spin 40–140°); the camera eases to frame both cores.
- **Inks**: galaxy 1 stars `Cold`, galaxy 2 `Hot`, cores `Accent`; star level by local density or distance to core.
- **Acceptance**: `GAL-A1` the core pair conserves energy within 1 % over a full cycle (leapfrog); `GAL-A2` initial star speeds equal the softened circular speed; `GAL-A3` after pericentre some stars are farther than `2·Rd` from both cores (tails form); `GAL-A4` the cycle restarts at 40 s.

### stellar_nursery  (Deep Cosmos, M5)
- **Intent**: a glowing emission nebula with dark dust pillars where gas streams onto protostars that ignite, flare bipolar jets, then shine.
- **Model**: nebula brightness = slowly drifting domain-warped sum of sines (≤ 6 terms, drawn only where above a threshold); 2–3 dust pillars as tapered vertical shapes that occlude; 3–5 protostars at seeded positions; a gas-particle pool (`≈ 0.03·area`, max 600) spawned in the nebula, accelerating toward the nearest protostar (`a = gm / (d² + ε²)`), absorbed within 0.8 rows, adding mass; a protostar ignites when mass ≥ `M_ign` (brightness ramps over 1 s) and then emits short-lived jet particles along a seeded axis; after 25 s of shining it fades (2.5 s) and is replaced by a new protostar elsewhere. Gas is stored in polar coordinates about its target: only the radial motion follows the softened gravity, the swirl is kinematic (pure rotation), which keeps NUR-A1 exact; new gas targets the nearest *accreting* protostar. Ignited stars ionise a growing bubble of nebula; pillar rims glow `Hot` as ionisation fronts; jets are pulsed (Herbig–Haro knots); jet pool `clamp(area/60, 4, 240)`.
- **Inks**: nebula `Wave`/`Hot` blend by level, pillars `Structure` (dark), gas `Particle`, protostars `Star`, jets `Accent`.
- **Acceptance**: `NUR-A1` a gas particle's distance to its target protostar is non-increasing until absorption (for dt ≤ 1/60); `NUR-A2` protostar mass is non-decreasing while it accretes and ignition happens exactly when mass first reaches `M_ign`; `NUR-A3` particle pools never exceed capacity.

### brownian_motion  (Forces & Waves, M5)
- **Intent**: a large pollen grain jittering among fast solvent molecules, leaving a fading random-walk trail, with a live mean-squared-displacement readout.
- **Model**: grain obeys the Langevin/Ornstein–Uhlenbeck equation `dv = −γ v dt + σ √dt ξ` (γ = 2 s⁻¹, σ chosen so the diffusion constant `D = σ²/(2γ²)` gives ~3 rows/√s), exact OU update for the velocity; reflecting walls; trail of the last 180 positions (`Trail`); `≈ 0.02·area` (max 400) visible solvent molecules moving ballistically with Maxwell–Boltzmann velocities and reflecting walls, occasionally flashing where they "hit" the grain; readout `D^ (MSD@2s) = …  D = … rows^2/s  t = …s` (ASCII, right-aligned, GEN-R13): `D̂` from the time-averaged MSD at a 2 s lag with the exact OU inertial correction (a single grain's `r²/4t` from the start never converges); when it would reach the title trailing parts (t, then the true D) are dropped, never clipped, and the readout is hidden when even `D^ (MSD@2s) = …` does not fit. The joint position–velocity OU transition is sampled exactly; walls are exact specular reflections via folding (method of images).
- **Inks**: grain `Accent`, trail `Structure` fading by age, molecules `Particle`, readout `Text`.
- **Acceptance**: `BRO-A1` the stationary velocity variance per axis converges to `σ²/(2γ)` (seeded statistical test, 5 %); `BRO-A2` the ensemble MSD at time t is `4Dt` within 10 % for t ≫ 1/γ (seeded, many walkers); `BRO-A3` the grain and molecules always stay inside the box; the trail length is ≤ 180.

### molecular_dynamics  (States of Matter, M5)
- **Intent**: a Lennard-Jones fluid in a box that is slowly heated and cooled so it melts into a gas and condenses back into droplets/crystals.
- **Model**: reduced LJ units, `U(r) = 4(r⁻¹² − r⁻⁶)` shifted at cutoff 2.5, in **2-D**; constant reduced density ρ = 0.25, inside the 2-D LJ vapour–liquid dome (r_c = 2.5 truncated-shifted: T_c ≈ 0.46, ρ_c ≈ 0.33–0.35, triple point ≈ 0.40); per-terminal layout: rows per σ `s = clamp(√(ρ·A₁/240), 1.1, 2.5)` and `N = clamp(round(ρ·A₁/s²), 8, 240)` with `A₁` the interior area in σ² at one row per σ (neighbouring crystal rows stay ≥ 1 row apart; 80×24 gets N ≈ 159; above ≈ 250×50 the scale clamps and ρ falls); one simulated second = 6 τ; velocity-Verlet with `dt_md ≤ 0.004` sub-steps per frame; cell list for O(N) forces; reflecting walls; atoms start on a triangular crystal; below `r = 0.8` the potential continues linearly so forces stay finite; a 30 s temperature cycle `T = 0.25 + 0.75·u²`, `u = (1 − cos 2πt/30)/2` (below T_c for about half the cycle, ≈ 2 T_c at the peak), by gentle velocity rescaling (Berendsen, τ = 0.5 s).
- **Inks**: atoms `Cold`→`Hot` by kinetic energy; box `Structure`; readout `T = …` `Text`.
- **Acceptance**: `MD-A1` the force equals `−dU/dr` (finite-difference check) and vanishes beyond the cutoff; `MD-A2` with the thermostat off, total energy drifts < 1 % over 5 simulated seconds; `MD-A3` atoms always stay inside the box; `MD-A4` the thermostat moves temperature toward its target; `MD-A5` the layout keeps ρ within 5 % of 0.25 wherever `s` is not clamped, and at 80×24 (seeded) the mean coordination within 1.5 σ in the cold phase (28–33 s) is > 2× that around the peak (14–19 s) and > 3.5.

### particle_accelerator  (Forces & Waves, M5)
- **Intent**: a collider ring where two counter-rotating bunches accelerate, meet at the detector, and burst into a shower of curling tracks.
- **Model**: ring = ellipse `R(0.8)`; bunches at angles `−π/2 + φ(t)` and `−π/2 − φ(t)` (mirror images about the vertical axis), so they meet only when `φ ≡ 0` (the detector, top) or `φ ≡ π` (a second, quieter interaction point, bottom); `dφ/dt` ramps linearly from 0.5 to 6 rad/s over 10 s; after the ramp the bunches collide at the detector each time they meet; each collision spawns 20–40 shower tracks with random momentum `p`, charge ±1, moving in a uniform magnetic field so each follows a circle of radius `r = p/B` (curling clockwise for +, counter-clockwise for −), losing momentum as `dp/dt = −0.8 p` (closed-form inward spiral, exact for any dt; non-relativistic: speed = p, radius = p/B) and fading after ≤ 2 s. Pool capped at 128. Collisions flash; the bottom interaction point only glints. Cycle 20 s; the first shower comes after the 10 s ramp.
- **Inks**: ring `Structure`, bunches `Accent` with short trails, tracks `Hot` (+) / `Cold` (−), detector `Text` label.
- **Acceptance**: `ACC-A1` the two bunches are always mirror images about the vertical axis and meet only at the two interaction points; `ACC-A2` a shower track's curvature radius equals `p/B` and its turning sign equals its charge; `ACC-A3` showers only spawn at the detector after the ramp and the pool never exceeds capacity.

### rocket_launch  (Voyages, M5)
- **Intent**: a launch from countdown to orbit: vapour venting, ignition, a rising rocket with a flickering flame, billowing exhaust that cools into smoke, and stage separation.
- **Model**: 22 s cycle — countdown 4 s (vapour puffs, "T−3 … 1" text), ignition 1 s (hold-down, exhaust spreads along the pad), flight: first stage burns 8 s with thrust `F`, exhaust speed `ve`, mass flow `ṁ = F/ve`, `a = F/m − g`; stage separation (the spent stage tumbles and falls away), second stage burns until the rocket exits the top; the view is fixed on the pad until altitude exceeds 60 % of the height, then the camera follows (ground scrolls away). Exhaust particles (`≈ 0.08·area`, max 1500) emitted backward with spread, slowed by drag, rising buoyantly as they cool from `Hot` to grey smoke. Rocket drawn from a small ASCII sprite (≥ 5 rows tall at h ≥ 20, a 1-cell glyph when tiny). Units are scaled for the screen (toy vehicle, `ve` ≈ 110–165 m/s; rows-per-metre chosen so 60 % height is reached 5 s after liftoff); the rocket equation is exact with `t` and `m0` measured from liftoff. Countdown vapour and the separation puff use a separate vapour pool, so "exhaust only while burning" holds strictly. Captions `LIFTOFF`, `STAGE SEPARATION`, `STAGE 2 IGNITION` and a telemetry line (T+, ALT, VEL) are shown.
- **Inks**: rocket body `Text`, flame `Hot`/`Accent`, smoke `Dim`, ground/tower `Structure`, countdown `Text`.
- **Acceptance**: `ROC-A1` during first-stage burn the velocity matches the rocket equation `v = ve ln(m0/m) − g t` within 1 %; `ROC-A2` exhaust spawns only while an engine burns, and the pool never exceeds capacity; `ROC-A3` altitude is non-decreasing from liftoff until stage separation and the cycle restarts at 22 s.

---
## Batch C — grids, automata & stochastic processes (M6)
Shared helpers: `sciart_core::grid` (CORE-R33..R36) plus `particles` and `draw`. Grids are rebuilt (or `resize`d) in `resize`.

### fractal_growth  (States of Matter, M6; renamed M11 from `crystal_growth`)
- **Intent**: a frost/mineral dendrite growing from a seed by diffusion-limited aggregation (Witten–Sander), glowing at its growing tips, then melting away and regrowing.
- **Model**: DLA on the square **braille sub-cell lattice** (2×4 dots per cell = 1/4 row per side with `ASPECT = 2`), so walks, contact distances and particle cost are all isotropic (weighting steps on the 2:1 cell grid only fixes diffusion and grows vertical needles, D_f ≈ 1.44); 8-neighbour sticking; far-field and near-field jump accelerations (Meakin / Ball–Brady; no measurable effect on D_f); walkers launch on a circle just outside the cluster radius and are discarded beyond `2×` it; sticking probability `p_s ∈ [0.3, 1]` (seeded per run: lower = denser); a bounded walker budget per update (`≤ 4000` walker steps per frame; paced so growth takes ~13–22 s at any size). Melting removes sites by graph distance from the seed (tips first, remainder stays connected). A live `D_f` readout is shown. Each crystal cell stores its attach time. When the cluster reaches `R(0.85)` (or after 60 s) it holds 3 s, then melts outside-in over 3 s and a new seed starts (optionally 2–3 seeds competing).
- **Inks**: fresh cells `Hot`/`Accent` fading by age to `Cold`; walkers faint `Dim` (optional); melting `Dim`.
- **Acceptance**: `CRY-A1` the cluster is always 8-connected to a seed; `CRY-A2` during growth cells are only added (count non-decreasing, attach times never change); `CRY-A3` walkers stay within the kill radius and the grid; `CRY-A4` the mean mass–radius fractal dimension of a small seeded ensemble is in `[1.45, 1.95]` (single clusters scatter ±0.08; finite-size fits read ≈ 1.63 vs the asymptotic 1.71).

### radioactive_decay  (Forces & Waves, M6)
- **Intent**: a lattice of unstable nuclei flickering through a decay chain while live population curves trace the exponential law.
- **Model**: chain `A → B → C` (C stable) with half-lives `T_A = 8 s`, `T_B = 3 s`; every nucleus decays within a step with probability `1 − 2^{−dt/T}` (exact for any dt); each decay emits a short-lived α/β track particle (`ParticlePool`) and a flash. Lattice on the left (≈ 62 % width, one nucleus per cell or per 2 cells for readability), plot panel on the right (only if ≥ 24×10 cells) with measured `N_A, N_B, N_C` and the analytic Bateman curves `N_A = N₀ 2^{−t/T_A}`, `N_B = N₀ λ_A/(λ_B − λ_A)(e^{−λ_A t} − e^{−λ_B t})`. Reset when `N_A + N_B < 2 %` of N₀ (≈ 50.6 s). A→B→C within one step is sampled exactly from the 3×3 transition matrix. The panel itself must be ≥ 24×10 (≈ terminals ≥ 63 columns); a resize starts a fresh run.
- **Inks**: A `Hot`, B `Accent`, C `Dim`, tracks `Particle`, curves: measured points in their species ink (A `Hot`, B `Accent`, C `Dim`, legend likewise), analytic Bateman curves `Structure`, axes/labels `Text`.
- **Acceptance**: `RAD-A1` per-step decay probability is `1 − 2^{−dt/T}` and composes exactly (`p(dt₁) ∘ p(dt₂) = p(dt₁ + dt₂)`); `RAD-A2` a seeded 20 000-nucleus run keeps `N_A(t)` within 4σ of `N₀ 2^{−t/T_A}` and `N_B(t)` within 4σ of Bateman; `RAD-A3` `N_A + N_B + N_C = N₀` at all times.

### gray_scott  (Reactions, M6; upgraded M10; renamed M11 from `chemical_reaction`)
- **Intent**: upgrade of the M6 scene; this section replaces the M6 one, and the scene moves to `Category::Chemistry` (CORE-R77). It shows living Turing patterns from the Gray–Scott system `U + 2V → 3V, V → P` (Gray & Scott 1983; Pearson 1993). One culture walks through five regimes of Pearson's (F, k) plane: spots that bud and divide, a short beat of branching coral, then three regimes that never settle (loops, a swarm of spots and short worms, and moving spots). Each regime melts into the next, so the pattern reorganises instead of being replaced. The field is drawn in half-block pixels on the sequential colormap: pale-yellow cores in orange bodies inside faint violet depletion halos, floating on the terminal's own black, which stays the majority of the screen in every regime a visit opens in. Nothing else is drawn except one corner label. At 80×24 there are about forty mitosis spots, each ≈ 5 columns × 2½ rows. At 200×50 there are about sixty, each ≈ 10 columns × 5 rows, round with graded edges. The scene stays distinct from `belousov_zhabotinsky` (travelling spiral fronts) and `liesegang_rings` (fixed concentric bands) because its forms are near-stationary blobs and stripes that divide, branch and reorganise.
- **Model**: `∂u/∂t = D_u∇²u − uv² + F(1 − u)` and `∂v/∂t = D_v∇²v + uv² − (F + k)v`, with `D_u = 2 D_v` and `D_v = 0.03` nodes² per reaction time unit. Kept from M6: the isotropic 9-point Laplacian (Patra–Karttunen), a periodic lattice, and an explicit Euler sub-step τ = 0.5 inside the positivity bounds `τ(10/3·D_u + 1 + F) ≤ 1` and `τ(10/3·D_v + F + k) ≤ 1`. The bounds are const-asserted for every preset and checked along the whole walk (CHEM-A5); the step is a convex combination plus non-negative sources, so u, v ≥ 0 and u ≤ 1, and decaying diffusive modes stay decaying (τ·D·16/3 ≪ 2). Every preset has its own **reaction rate** (reaction time units per simulated second, see the walk): 400 for mitosis and coral (800 sub-steps/s, ≈ 13.3 per frame), 70 for loops, 110 for swarm and 150 for spots. A morph lerps the rate by the same weight m as F and k, and τ stays 0.5, so the rate only sets how many sub-steps a frame runs (at most 200 per `update`, the 400 rate at dt = 0.25) and the positivity bounds are unaffected. At a single rate of 400 the moving regimes, which evolve far faster in reaction time than mitosis divides, read as a boiling field: loops renewed ≈ 60 % of the ASCII cells in 0.25 s (≈ 13 % per frame, swarm ≈ 11 %). **Lattice (ADR-0012)**: square nodes decoupled from the display, one node per half-block pixel below the cap: `lattice_size(display) = grid::lattice_for(Size(max(w, 32), max(2h, 16)), MAX_NODES = 5 120, BUCKET = 1.12)`, with the 32×16 floor restored inside the cap when the aspect snap undercuts it on extreme displays. Below the cap `lattice_for` returns its input (80×24 → 80×48, 40×12 → 40×24). Above the cap the lattice stops growing (200×50 → 100×50, two pixels per node), its aspect snaps to a power of 1.12 (stretch ≤ ±6 %), and the pattern zooms instead of getting denser. A pixel is square, so the lattice stays isotropic; the pixel scale holds 7–8 pattern wavelengths across an 80×24 screen, which keeps small displays from locking into a torus mode (the M10 draft's 40×24 lattice held 3–4 and settled into diagonal plane-wave stripes; a 20×12 lattice visibly tiled). The 32×16 floor keeps a culture alive through tiny transient sizes. **Preset walk**: a looping 10-beat `Timeline` of five holds, each followed by a smoothstep morph to the next, 119 s in all. Name, (F, k), rate, hold, morph that follows: mitosis (.0367, .0649; Sims), 400, 8 s, 6 s; coral (.0545, .062; Sims), 400, 3 s, 6 s; loops (.018, .051; Márquez-Neila's "spots and loops"), 70, 24 s, 8 s; swarm (.016, .052), 110, 24 s, 8 s; spots (.014, .054; Márquez-Neila's "moving spots"), 150, 24 s, 8 s; then back to mitosis. The morphs into and out of coral take 6 s rather than 8 s so the blown-out labyrinth is brief: at 80×24 the share of time with ≥ 50 % `#`/`@` cells fell from 7.25–8 s to 6.25–6.5 s, and with ≥ 30 % of the half-block pixels at Seq ≥ 192 from ≈ 8 s to 6.5 s (seeds 1, 3, 7, 42). The coral hold is short because coral branches only during the morph into it and the first seconds of the hold; a 12 s hold spent ≈ 10 s on a frozen, blown-out labyrinth (2–8 % of cells changing per 4 s, 57 % `#`/`@`), which the rejection criteria below exclude. From seed bumps, mitosis spots divide for ≈ 12 s, through the 8 s hold and into the morph, then jam once they fill the torus (a 16 s hold was still for its last 4 s, 3 % change). **Passaging**: without it, the return to mitosis inherits a full field of moving spots, which divide until they tile the torus and then jam: the returning hold was still for ≈ 8–10 s (4 s change 0.00–0.02). So the return is passaged, like a culture split into fresh medium. The first `resize` draws a seed from `ctx.rng` and builds a mask on the lattice of blocks of ≈ 6×6 nodes (`round(W/6) × round(H/6)` blocks tiling the torus exactly), each active when a SplitMix64 draw keyed by the seed and the block index is below 0.8; it is rebuilt from the same seed on every lattice change. During the spots → mitosis morph, each sub-step is followed by the sink `v ← v(1 − τκ m² mask)` (Lie splitting, κ = 0.02 per reaction unit, m the morph's smoothstep), a shrinking factor, so positivity holds. About four in five moving spots fade out over the morph's second half, and the survivors divide into the freed room through the mitosis hold. Measured at 80×24: masking half the blocks left ≈ 20 survivors that refilled the torus within ≈ 6 s, and the hold still froze for its last 2–4 s (4 s change 0.07–0.11); with κ·m instead of κ·m² the spots died by mid-morph and its last 4 s were still (0.06–0.12). With 0.8 and m², every 4 s window over 160 s from the mitosis and the spots slot (12 seeds each) changes ≥ 15 %; the returning hold changes 0.20–0.24 per 2 s, as the first one does. Each preset sits within 0.005 of the saddle-node curve `F = 4(F + k)²` (F − 4(F + k)² ∈ [−0.0046, +0.0002]). **Morphs follow the saddle-node curve**: F is interpolated, and k keeps the interpolated signed offset from the curve, `k = k_sn(F) + lerp(k_a − k_sn(F_a), k_b − k_sn(F_b), m)` with `k_sn(F) = √F/2 − F`. A straight chord can bulge across the curve into the region where the uniform v₊ sheet is stable (the M10 draft's worms → holes chord reached F − 4(F + k)² = +0.0028 and reseeded every loop), so following the curve is what lets the culture carry over. Rejected regimes, measured on this lattice: worms (.078, .061) never grows from seed patches (it reseeds for its whole hold); holes, maze and chaos cover 98–100 % of the screen and freeze, and maze and chaos lock into diagonal stripes at 80×24; chaos at Márquez-Neila's .026 is a near-homogeneous Hopf oscillation; solitons (.030, .062) and pulsating solitons (.025, .060) freeze within ≈ 12 s; waves (.014, .045) becomes one pulsing blob. The seeded start is a whole start slot, the start of the mitosis, loops, swarm or spots hold (never coral, whose labyrinth fills the torus). A `sim` visit of 15 s shows one regime develop and keep moving (from mitosis: spots divide, then elongate toward coral), and a long `run` shows the walk. Seeds are Gaussian bumps `v = ¼·exp(−(r/r₀)²)`, `u = 1 − 2v` (Pearson's u = ½, v = ¼ at the centre), r₀ ∈ [1.5, 3] nodes, ±5 % noise per node, 3 + nodes/300 of them; a bump only raises v and lowers u. The first `resize` pre-rolls 200 sub-steps (100 reaction units, ¼ s at the mitosis rate), so the first frame already shows graded bodies. A once-per-second featureless check (max v < 0.02, or max v − min v < 0.05) reseeds and counts it; the spread, unlike σ(v), does not mistake a young sparse culture for a dead one. It never fires in the walk (CHEM-A4, CHEM-A5).
- **Motion**: sub-steps advance the fronts every frame. Mitosis spots swell and divide for ≈ 12 s; loops, swarm and spots jostle, merge and split without ever settling, as organisms rather than a boiling field: at 80×24 and 200×50 about 2–4 % of the ASCII cells change per frame and ≈ 20 % per 0.25 s (loops 2.1–2.8 % and 0.18–0.22, swarm 3.0–3.6 % and 0.19–0.23, measured over seeds 1, 2, 3, 6, 7, 11), while 50–80 % still change per 4 s at the end of each hold. The 6–8 s morphs reorganise the whole field: mitosis spots elongate into coral branches, the coral breaks into loops, loops shrink into a swarm and then into moving spots, and most moving spots fade (passaging) while the rest slow into dividing mitosis spots. The walk repeats every 119 s, but frames never repeat because the culture carries over.
- **Resize**: if `lattice_size` is unchanged (every display at or above the cap in the same bucket), the simulation is untouched. Otherwise u and v are resampled onto the new lattice with `grid::resample_bilinear`, so the pattern is stretched to the new domain rather than cropped. The bilinear edge clamp leaves a seam that diffusion heals within ≈ 1 s. If the node scale changed, spots divide or merge back toward the intrinsic wavelength, which is physically faithful. Nothing is reseeded unless the field is featureless. `Pixels` and the `grid::SampleMap` (lattice → pixels) are rebuilt here. The view (u, v → Pixels) is refreshed at the end of `resize` and of `update`, so `render` only blits and writes the label.
- **Inks**: `Role::Seq` only (ADR-0011); the M6 `Hot`, `Cold` and `Accent` inks are retired. u and v are sampled bilinearly into the `Pixels` through a `grid::SampleMap`; v first, and u only where the pixel is not pattern (the halo needs it). With `q = clamp(v/0.40, 0, 1)`, a pixel with `q ≥ 0.3` (v = 0.12) is pattern and gets `ink::seq(0.12 + 0.88 p^0.8)`, `p = (q − 0.3)/0.7`, from a 256-entry table built once. The edge was 0.125 (v = 0.05) in round 2: the v ≈ 0.05–0.12 tails between loops then read as a wall of `:` and `+` (loops at 80×24 kept ≈ 35 % near-black half-block pixels, i.e. Background or Seq below 32, and 26–35 % blank or `.` ASCII cells; swarm ≈ 50 %). At 0.25 loops averaged ≈ 53 % near-black but single frames fell to 47 % and to 40 % blank or `.`; at 0.3 loops average 57–61 % near-black (frames ≥ 48 %) and 51–56 % blank or `.`, swarm 67–71 % and 63–67 %. Mitosis spots lose only their outermost ring, which the halo still grades, so the ramp's exponent stays 0.8. Full scale 0.40 leaves headroom above the broad v ≈ 0.3–0.37 plateaus (coral, the tops of large spots), which read yellow-orange (level ≈ 200–225), so only spot cores reach the pale-yellow top: with 0.34, 36 % of the coral beat's pixels sat at the top level with no internal grading; now 5–11 %, and mitosis cores (v99 ≈ 0.38) still reach level ≈ 245. The ASCII `#`/`@` share barely depends on the exposure (coral's plateau is far above the `#` threshold at any full scale from 0.34 to 0.48). The ramp starts just above the halo, so edges stay graded, and the thin tails between neighbouring spots fall to the halo or to black, so even dense regimes keep dark gaps. Otherwise the pixel gets the halo, which fades in with substrate depletion from zero at `1 − u = 0.2` to its peak at 0.6: `ink::seq(0.1·clamp((1 − u − 0.2)/0.4, 0, 1))`, and `Ink::BLANK` (the terminal background) where that level rounds to 0. There are no hard black notches in the violet valleys. The label is `Role::Text` level 195: it sits on the brightest backgrounds of any scene.
- **ASCII**: the `Pixels::blit` closure uses the brighter half, so thin features survive. Seq levels 13–31 give `.` (the stronger half of the halo); fainter halo, which Unicode shows as a fade to black, is left blank. Above that, `:` `+` `#` `@` by quartile of `(level − 32)/223`, keeping that pixel's `Seq` ink. A cell with both halves empty is left untouched. The look matches M6: `.:+#@` blobs on blank.
- **Label**: row 0. The text starts at column 1 inside a one-cell blank gutter on each side (column 0 and the cell after the text are `Ink::BLANK` spaces), so it never touches the pattern. At width ≥ 56 it reads `mitosis  F=.037 k=.065`: the current preset name, switching at mid-morph, then F and k rounded to thousandths with no leading zero. They are written from integers into a stack buffer and track the morph continuously. At 18–55 columns it reads `F=.037 k=.065` (with the name, the label and its gutter took 24 of 40 columns and cut a notch into the pattern). Below 18 columns or 6 rows there is no label. The M6 bottom caption is dropped, which also keeps the last row free for the HUD.
- **Perf**: at the 400 rate (mitosis, coral), 80×24 runs 3 840 nodes × 13.3 sub-steps ≈ 51 k cell updates per frame and 200×50 (and above) 5 000 nodes ≈ 67 k (~3 ns each); loops, swarm and spots run 2.3, 3.7 and 5 sub-steps per frame. The passaging sink adds one multiply per node and sub-step during one morph. The view samples v for every pixel and u only off the pattern, plus the ink mapping and the blit. Measured (`cargo bench`, frame = update + render, averaged over the walk the benchmark steps through from seed 42): 0.26 ms at 200×50 and 0.90 ms at 400×100 (M10 round 3; at a constant 400 rate round 2 measured 0.29 ms and 0.84 ms). The simulation is capped at any size; the view grows with the display (every pixel samples v bilinearly), so 400×100 costs ≈ 3× 200×50 (informational, GEN-R8 is measured at 200×50). `render` and steady `update` stay allocation-free.
- **Acceptance**: `CHEM-A1` u, v stay finite and in `[0, 1]` for any dt ≤ 0.25, and the kernel's clamp is only a guard: at every 4 s frame of the whole-walk runs of CHEM-A8 (seeds 1 and 7), the unclamped reference Euler update of the live fields (core `laplacian9`) is already in `[0, 1]` and its v stays below 0.7 (measured ≤ 0.63, loops colliding); `CHEM-A2` the uniform state `(u, v) = (1, 0)` is a fixed point; `CHEM-A3` with the reaction terms off, diffusion conserves `Σu` and `Σv` (periodic); `CHEM-A4` from each start slot (mitosis, loops, swarm, spots) and seeds 1, 2, 3 and 7, an 80×24 run develops structure within 10 s: var(v) > 3e-3 and at most 90 % of the ASCII cells below the label row are blank or `.`; from the mitosis slot the number of nodes with v > 0.1 at least doubles (the moving regimes are already developed by the pre-roll); and no reseed happens in the first 20 s; `CHEM-A5` the preset table is exactly the five presets above (names, F, k, rates, holds, morphs); `preset_at(t)` visits them in order, equals each preset exactly during its hold (its F, k and rate), is between preset i and i + 1 at mid-morph with the rate at the midpoint of the two rates, changes F and k by ≤ 1e-4 and the rate by ≤ 1 per 10 ms across the whole walk, keeps the rate within [70, 400], and wraps at 119 s; every seeded visit opens at the start of a start slot, all four occur over 40 seeds, and coral never does; at every 10 ms of the walk both Euler positivity bounds hold, F, k > 0, and F − 4(F + k)² ∈ [−0.005, +0.0015]; at mid-morph F is the midpoint and k − k_sn(F) is the midpoint of the endpoints' offsets; `k_sn` solves the curve to 1e-12; `uniform_states(F, k)` returns (1, 0) plus, iff F > 4(F + k)², the two non-trivial homogeneous states `v± = [F ± √(F² − 4F(F + k)²)]/(2(F + k))`, `u = (F + k)/v`, each with ODE residual < 1e-9 in f64; one full walk plus 2 s from the mitosis slot at 80×24 for seeds 1, 5, 7 and 11 never reseeds; `CHEM-A6` for displays 1×1 … 1000×300 `lattice_size` gives ≤ 5 120 nodes and at least 32×16, equals `(max(w, 32), max(2h, 16))` whenever that has ≤ 4 500 nodes, gives 80×48 at 80×24, 40×24 at 40×12, 100×50 at 200×50 and 80×63 at 120×50, and gives 200×50, 300×75 and 400×100 one shared lattice (100×50); a resize that keeps the lattice leaves u and v bit-identical; in the mitosis hold, 200×50 → 120×50 → 200×50 resamples with the reseed counter unchanged, keeping mean v within 5 % of before, and 2 s later std(v) is within 30 % of before with still no reseed; `CHEM-A7` `v_ink(u, v)` is `None` iff q < 0.3 and the halo level rounds to 0, and is otherwise `Role::Seq` with level non-decreasing in v for fixed u; the halo level rises by at most 1 per 0.01 of depletion from 0 to `ink::seq(0.1)`; a pattern pixel never reads u; `ascii_cell` leaves Seq levels < 13 blank, gives `.` for 13–31 and uses the brighter half; an 80×24 Unicode frame after 3 s uses only `Seq`, `Text` and `Background` inks, and row 0 starts ` mitosis  F=.037 k=.065 ` (blank gutter, label at level ≥ 190); the 80×24 ASCII frame is pure ASCII from `.:+#@` plus the label; the label is exactly `<name>  F=.fff k=.kkk` at width ≥ 56 (thousandths, name switching at mid-morph), exactly `F=.fff k=.kkk` at 18–55 columns, and absent below 18 columns or 6 rows; `CHEM-A8` from each start slot at 80×24 and 200×50 (seeds 1 and 7), 10 s into the hold (3.9 s into the 8 s mitosis hold) at most 50 % of the ASCII cells below the label are `#` or `@` and at least 20 % are blank or `.`; at least 5 % of cells change in the next 4 s; and in the loops, swarm and spots holds at least 30 % change between 20 s and 24 s. In the loops and swarm holds at 80×24 and 200×50 (seeds 1 and 7), sampled at 2, 5, 8, 15 and 18 s, each sample with the frame 1/30 s later and the frame 0.25 s later: on average over the samples at most 4 % of the ASCII cells change per frame and at most 35 % per 0.25 s, at least 50 % of the half-block pixels below the label row are near-black (`Ink::BLANK` or `Seq` below level 32) and at least 45 % of the ASCII cells are blank or `.`, and no single sample has under 45 % near-black or under 40 % blank or `.`. Over the whole walk and on through the passaged return to mitosis and its hold (to 136 s), from the mitosis slot at 80×24, and from the spots slot through the first return and its hold (to 48 s), seeds 1 and 7: each frame at 4i s has at most 50 % `#`/`@` cells (60 % in the coral beat, from mid-morph into coral to the end of the morph out of it: a labyrinth covers ≈ 55 % of the cells at any exposure, measured 0.51–0.56), and in every 4 s window [4i, 4i + 4] at least 10 % of the cells change, with no exemption. `passage_mask` is a pure function of lattice and seed (different seeds give different masks), holds only 0 and 1, is constant on blocks (at most one change of value per 5 nodes along a row), and marks 55–85 % of the nodes on average over 8 seeds at 80×48, 100×50, 32×16 and 40×24.

### spacetime_grid  (Deep Cosmos, M6)
- **Intent**: the rubber-sheet picture of general relativity: a perspective grid dimpled by two orbiting masses, with gravitational-wave ripples spreading outward.
- **Model**: a `(nx × ny)` vertex mesh (`Grid<[f64; 3]>` or similar) on the plane, depth `z = −Σ mᵢ / √(rᵢ² + ε²)` plus a quadrupole wave term `A·cos(2(φ − Ω(t − r/c)))·f(r)` with retarded time (`c` = wave speed in grid units, amplitude decaying ~ `1/√r` and tapered near the sources); binary on a circular orbit (angular speed Ω, masses `m₁ ≥ m₂` about the centre of mass), inspiralling on a Newtonian (Peters) chirp `a = a₀(1−τ/τc)^{1/4}` with Kepler Ω and closed-form phase, reset at `a = 0.55 a₀` with continuous phase (a circular mode is kept for SPT-A2); wave amplitude grows ∝ `1/a`; `c` is artistic (λ comparable to the orbit so the spiral fits the sheet); the mesh is viewed from a tilted camera (pitch ≈ 55°) and projected with perspective, drawn as polylines with slope-aware glyphs, drawn far-band to near (painter's order) so near lines win; crossings merge into `┼`/`╳`; masses drawn as bright spheres above their wells.
- **Inks**: grid lines `Structure` (level by depth/nearness), wells `Cold`, masses `Sun`/`Star`, wave crests `Wave`.
- **Acceptance**: `SPT-A1` for a single mass the depth is radially symmetric and strictly increases in magnitude toward the mass; `SPT-A2` the binary keeps its centre of mass fixed and its separation constant (non-inspiral mode); `SPT-A3` the wave term has period π in the azimuth and its phase at radius r equals the source phase at `t − r/c`.

---
## Batch D — physics sims & narrative scenes (M7)
Shared helpers: `ode` (RK4), `timeline` + `ease` (CORE-R37..R39) plus `particles`, `grid`, `draw`. Narrative scenes loop forever: every phase has a fixed duration in simulated seconds, so a run is deterministic per seed and resizes never desynchronise the story.

### double_pendulum  (Forces & Waves, M7)
- **Intent**: chaos made visible — two double pendulums released from almost identical angles swing together, then diverge wildly, each tracing a glowing path.
- **Model**: exact Lagrangian equations for point masses `m₁, m₂` on massless rods `L₁, L₂` (rows, aspect-correct on screen), `g` scaled so the upper arm alone has a small-angle period of 2.2 s (the coupled slow mode is ≈ 2.78 s); physics runs in model units (`L₁ = 1`) with a separate screen scale, so motion is identical at any terminal size; both twins share one pivot; state `(θ₁, θ₂, ω₁, ω₂)` advanced with `ode::integrate` at `h ≤ 1/240 s`; the twin starts with `Δθ₂ = 10⁻³` rad; high-energy seeded initial angles (both arms above horizontal ± variety); trails of the lower bobs (`Trail`, ~2 s); a divergence readout (`|Δθ| e-folding`) and energy drift; restart every 45 s with new seeded angles.
- **Inks**: pendulum A `Cold`, B `Hot`, rods `Structure`, pivots `Text`, trails fading by age.
- **Acceptance**: `DP-A1` total energy drifts < 0.5 % over 20 s; `DP-A2` for small angles the two normal-mode frequencies match the linearised analytic values within 1 %; `DP-A3` from a seeded chaotic start the twins' separation (max so far — it saturates near π) grows ≥ 100× within 30 s; `DP-A4` rods keep their lengths exactly (bob positions derived from angles).

### mars_rover  (Voyages, M7)
- **Intent**: a Perseverance-style rover crossing rust-red dunes under a butterscotch sky: parallax ridges, dust devils, a stop to drill a sample and beam data to an orbiter, as the sol passes from day to dusk (blue Martian sunset).
- **Model**: world-x terrain from seeded fractal value noise (≥ 3 octaves) that is a pure function of world x (deterministic, continuous); 3 parallax layers; rocker–bogie suspension: 6 wheels each placed on the terrain surface, body height/pitch from the rocker and bogie pivots; a `Timeline` sol: drive (18 s) → stop & deploy arm (3 s) → drill with dust (4 s) → stow (2 s) → transmit to an orbiter crossing the sky (4 s) → drive … ; dust particles from wheels and drill (`ParticlePool`); 0–2 dust devils drifting; Phobos crossing fast; sky/sun tint changes over a slower 90 s sol cycle (solar elevation at Jezero's latitude; Phobos on its real synodic period, scaled). Pose is solved by Newton iteration on the three wheel-contact residuals (rigid links, body pitch = rocker angle); far-side wheels coincide with the near ones in the side view. Dust over solid fills lightens rather than replaces the cell (a "veil" compositor), since cells have no background colour.
- **Inks**: terrain `Hot` (near) → `Dim` (far layers), day haze `Sun` (low levels), blue sunset glow `Cold`, dusk band/orbiter `Accent`, dust devils `Hot`, rover body `Text`, wheels/arm `Structure`, dust `Particle`, signal `Wave`.
- **Acceptance**: `MR-A1` every wheel contact point lies on the terrain surface (within 0.5 row); `MR-A2` terrain height is a pure deterministic function of world x — identical across scrolls, resizes and chunk boundaries, and continuous (adjacent columns differ by ≤ a bound); `MR-A3` the sol timeline visits its phases in order with their durations and loops.

### voyager_journey  (Voyages, M7)
- **Intent**: Voyager 2's Grand Tour, 1977–1989 in 48 s: launch from Earth, gravity assists at Jupiter, Saturn, Uranus and Neptune with flyby flashes and captions, then out past the heliopause to a closing "Pale Blue Dot" moment.
- **Model**: heliocentric top-down map with a monotone radial compression (e.g. `ρ = R·log(1 + r/r₀)/log(1 + r_max/r₀)`, AU→rows) so Earth…Neptune all fit; planets on circular orbits with real mean motions and J2000-ish mean longitudes; real dates: launch 1977-08-20, Jupiter 1979-07-09, Saturn 1981-08-26, Uranus 1986-01-24, Neptune 1989-08-25; the spacecraft path is a smooth curve through the planets' positions at those dates (e.g. Hermite/polar interpolation in (r, φ) between encounter points with monotone r), time compression with slow-downs near encounters (via `ease`), a date/distance readout; 48 s mission + 8 s epilogue, then loop. Planet elements from JPL/Standish "Keplerian Elements for Approximate Positions" (J2000); the path is a monotone cubic Hermite (PCHIP) curve through each planet's model position on its encounter date (a shape, not a propagated trajectory). Captions sit at the top (Uranus/Neptune are near the bottom in the real geometry). The epilogue credits the Pale Blue Dot to Voyager 1 (which took it); the sight line from Voyager 2 is artistic licence. **Stylisation**: the path is interpolated through the real encounter geometry; the gravity assists the title names ("planets fling it on") are depicted, not integrated.
- **Inks**: Sun `Sun`, orbits `Dim`, planets `Planet`, spacecraft `Accent` with `Structure` trail, captions `Text`, flyby flash `Hot`.
- **Acceptance**: `VOY-A1` at each encounter date the spacecraft coincides with the planet (same model position, and within 0.5 cell on screen); `VOY-A2` planet angular speeds follow Kepler (period ratios `(a₁/a₂)^{3/2}`) and match real periods within 1 %; `VOY-A3` mission time → calendar date is monotone and prints the exact encounter dates at encounters; `VOY-A4` the radial mapping is strictly monotone and fits Neptune's orbit on screen.

### moon_landing  (Voyages, M7)
- **Intent**: Apollo 11's powered descent: the LM pitching over, throttling its descent engine above a cratered Mare Tranquillitatis, kicking up dust, "CONTACT LIGHT", "The Eagle has landed", then the flag and an earthrise.
- **Model**: 2-D point-mass LM with thrust `T ≤ T_max` along its attitude, `ṁ = −T/(I_sp g₀)` (I_sp 311 s), lunar `g = 1.62 m/s²`, scaled distances; a guidance law (e.g. gravity-turn then a constant-deceleration / polynomial guidance to a target point with touchdown speed ≤ 1 m/s) that always lands for the seeded start set; cratered terrain (seeded crater field on a gently rolling surface, pure function of x); dust `ParticlePool` when altitude < ~30 m (scaled) blown outward from beneath the engine; `Timeline`: descent (≈ 22 s) → touchdown & dust settles (4 s) → flag & caption (5 s) → earthrise hold (4 s) → loop with a new seeded approach; readout of altitude, vertical speed and fuel %, "CONTACT LIGHT" at probe contact. Guidance: P64 closed-form E-guidance to a gate 8 m above the site, then P66 sink-rate profile with position hold. "Touchdown" is footpad contact; the engine runs until then (a historical cut at CONTACT LIGHT would free-fall 1.7 m to ≈ 2.4 m/s and break ML-A1). Earth appears only in the earthrise phase.
- **Inks**: LM `Text`/`Structure`, flame `Hot`, dust `Particle`, terrain `Dim`/`Structure`, Earth `Cold`, captions `Text`.
- **Acceptance**: `ML-A1` for a seeded set of start conditions the guidance lands with vertical speed ≤ 1 m/s, horizontal ≤ 0.5 m/s and fuel ≥ 0; `ML-A2` with constant thrust the integrated velocity matches the rocket equation `Δv = I_sp g₀ ln(m₀/m) − g t` (vertical burn) within 0.5 %; `ML-A3` the LM never penetrates the terrain; `ML-A4` the timeline phases occur in order and loop.

## Batch E — scientific art: fields, gravity, chemistry & biology (M10)
Design: `docs/design/M10-scientific-art-batch.md`; ADR-0010 (layered raster: `raster::Dots` braille, `raster::Pixels` half-block with `Cell.bg`), ADR-0011 (colormap roles `Seq`/`Div`), ADR-0012 (simulation lattice decoupled from the display, `grid::lattice_for`).
New core helpers (CORE-R40..R59, RND-R6/R7): `raster::{Dots, Pixels, Density}`, `grid::{resample_bilinear, resample_nearest, lattice_for, SampleMap, laplacian9}`, `ode::{verlet_step, verlet_integrate, tridiag_solve}`, `geom::{Camera, Projector}`, `ink::{seq, div, soft_clip, seq_value, div_value}`, `text::StackText`, `draw::text_mode`, `particles::{SpatialHash, InverseCdf}`, `rng::SplitMix64`, `Category::{Biology, Chemistry}`; the post-M10 audit lifted more shared code into core (CORE-R60..R70); plus the existing `draw`, `particles`, `grid`, `ode`, `timeline`/`ease` and `geom` helpers.
The `gray_scott` upgrade is specified in place in Batch C above (`CHEM-A5`..`CHEM-A8`).

### action_potential  (Physics of Life, M10)
- **Intent**: a nerve impulse made visible. A stylised neuron rests in the dark: soma, branching dendrites, a long unmyelinated axon and a fan of synaptic terminals, all drawn as dim membrane outlines. A stimulus fires the soma, and a bright pulse of depolarisation runs down the axon with a cool after-hyperpolarisation wake behind it. It lights the terminals, which release a small puff of transmitter. An oscilloscope inset, wired to the recording electrode by a faint dotted lead, draws the classic spike waveform at the moment the pulse passes the electrode. A looping protocol shows four things in turn: a single spike, a train that slows as it runs into its own refractory wake, two spikes annihilating in collision, and the all-or-none threshold.
- **Model**: Hodgkin & Huxley (1952) squid giant axon, in the modern rest = −65 mV convention (Dayan & Abbott 2001).
  - **Cable equation**: `C_m ∂V/∂t = (a / 2R_i) ∂²V/∂x² − ḡ_Na m³h (V − E_Na) − ḡ_K n⁴ (V − E_K) − ḡ_L (V − E_L) + I_stim`, with `C_m = 1 μF/cm²`, `ḡ_Na = 120`, `ḡ_K = 36`, `ḡ_L = 0.3 mS/cm²`, `E_Na = +50`, `E_K = −77` and `E_L = −54.387 mV`.
  - **Rates** (ms⁻¹, V in mV): `α_m = 0.1(V + 40)/(1 − e^{−(V+40)/10})`, `β_m = 4 e^{−(V+65)/18}`, `α_h = 0.07 e^{−(V+65)/20}`, `β_h = 1/(1 + e^{−(V+35)/10})`, `α_n = 0.01(V + 55)/(1 − e^{−(V+55)/10})`, `β_n = 0.125 e^{−(V+65)/80}`. All are scaled by `φ = 3^{(T − 6.3)/10}` with `T = 6.3 °C`, so `φ = 1`. The removable singularities at −40 and −55 mV go through an `x/(1 − e^{−x})` helper with a series branch.
  - **Geometry**: axon radius `a = 238 μm` and `R_i = 35.4 Ω·cm` (HH 1952) give `D = a/(2 R_i C_m) = 0.336 cm²/ms`. The cable is `L = 10 cm` in `N = 200` compartments (`Δx = 0.5 mm`, ≈ 1/14 of the 7 mm resting length constant), with sealed (no-flux) ends.
  - **Sub-step (10 μs)**: first a Rush–Larsen (1978) exponential update of m, h and n, which is exact for frozen V and stays in `[0, 1]`. Then backward-Euler V with the ionic conductances frozen (Hines 1984), solved as a tridiagonal M-matrix by the Thomas algorithm into preallocated scratch. This is unconditionally stable; explicit Euler would need a step ≤ 3.7 μs and RK4 ≤ 5 μs. Against a converged fine-grid run (Crank–Nicolson, Δx = 0.05 mm, 0.5 μs: 12.31 and 18.72 m/s) the velocity error is 0.4 % at 6.3 °C and 0.9 % at 18.5 °C.
  - **Clock**: it counts integer sub-steps (`h = 0.01 ms`, through an accumulator with 1e-9 slack), so how frames chop time cannot change results. Slow motion is 4 ms of axon time per simulated second (250×).
  - **Measured physiology** (converged): propagated peak +37.9 mV; after-hyperpolarisation minimum −75.9 mV; V > −40 mV for 2.3 ms at a point (a ≈ 2.9 cm footprint); conduction velocity 12.3 m/s at 6.3 °C and 18.7 m/s at 18.5 °C (HH computed 18.8 m/s and measured 21.2 m/s); threshold ≈ 90 μA/cm² for a 0.3 ms pulse over the first 0.25 cm.
  - **Protocol**: a looping `Timeline` of 18 s (= 72 ms). `single` 3 s: a soma pulse at 0.2 s. `train` 8 s: pulses at 3.0, 5.5 and 8.0 s, 10 ms apart. `collision` 4.5 s: soma and terminal end stimulated together at 13.0 s, the neurophysiologist's collision test. `threshold` 2.5 s: a 0.5× pulse at 16.0 s. Strong pulses are 270 μA/cm² (3× threshold) for 0.3 ms over the first (or last) 0.25 cm, and the weak pulse is 45 μA/cm².
  - **Refractory timing**: every strong pulse starts ≥ 9.5 ms after the last spike passed its site. That is beyond the ≈ 6–8 ms refractory limit, so every strong pulse fires, but inside the relative refractory period, so each train spike runs slower than the last (measured 10.9, 10.1 and 9.8 m/s, against 12.3 isolated). The model state runs continuously across loops, and the second loop repeats the first to within 0.01 s. The first loop starts from rest; every later one starts 20 ms after the collision, in its supernormal phase (at 3–7 cm `h` is ≈ 0.01 above and `n` ≈ 0.006 below their resting values), so its single spike runs 0.8 % faster: 12.36 against 12.26 m/s. Moving the weak pulse (15.2–17.0 s) does not change this. Later loops repeat one another to 1e-6 m/s.
  - **Weak pulse**: it lands on the after-hyperpolarisation left by the collision and lifts the soma to ≈ −61 mV (≈ −57 mV from rest). It spreads passively ≈ 1 cm and fades.
  - **Stimulus markers**: each pulse lights a stimulus marker for 0.25 s, at a level proportional to its amplitude, so the weak kick visibly happens. The marker is `>` left of the soma, and `<` just before the terminals for the far-end pulse.
  - **Stylisation**: the soma, dendrites and terminal arbor are drawn, not simulated; the real giant axon is a syncytium grown from the stellate ganglion. The soma shows V of compartment 0, and the terminal arbor fans out the last 0.8 cm of the cable.
  - **Vesicles**: at each 0 mV up-crossing at the cable end, every bouton emits 4 vesicle dots (`ParticlePool`, capacity 20 = 5 boutons × 4). They drift 1–2 cells across the cleft and fade within 0.4 s.
- **Motion**: at t = 0 the neuron rests, showing only its outline. The stimulus marker and soma flash at 0.2 s. By 1 s the pulse is a third of the way along the axon (GEN-R9). It reaches the terminals at ≈ 2.3 s, with a bouton flash and a vesicle puff. The trace scrolls continuously (a 20 ms = 5 s window) and draws each spike as the pulse passes the electrode (compartment 100, 5 cm, ≈ 1.1 s after the stimulus). In the train, each spike leaves the soma as the previous one reaches the terminals, and each is visibly slower. In the collision beat, two pulses converge and vanish near mid-axon at ≈ 14.1 s. In the threshold beat the marker blinks and a faint soma glow spreads about a centimetre and fades within ≈ 1 s. Nothing travels, and the trace stays flat. The weak kick lands 12 ms after the collision spike left the soma, on its after-hyperpolarisation (≈ −69 mV), so its half-threshold amplitude and the residual refractoriness both hold it below threshold; AP-A5 checks that the same pulse also fails from rest. The loop is 18 s.
- **Resize**: the simulation does not depend on size, so no model state is touched. Geometry is rebuilt from normalised parameters:
  - the soma centre and radius;
  - the axon path, stored as a per-pixel table of arclength `s`, with V linearly interpolated between compartments;
  - the dendrite tree. Its topology is drawn from `ctx.rng` on the first resize only and stored in a fixed array of ≤ 40 segments, each ending at a node `(angle, ρ)`: polar about the soma centre, ρ soma radii beyond the rim, drawn at `r + k·ρ`. The tree is radial and planar. Three primaries leave the rim radially within a ±1.45 rad cone pointing left: one up (−1.15 rad), one down (+1.15) and one at ±0.55 on a random side, each jittered by ±0.08, so the stimulus marker straight left stays clear. Each primary owns the wedge of the cone between the midpoints to its neighbours. Every fork splits its wedge at the parent's angle and turns each child 22–38° off the parent's radial line (measured at the child's end), clamped inside its half-wedge. Since subtrees keep to disjoint convex wedges about the soma centre, no two branches ever cross. Radial reach by depth is 0.9, 0.7, 0.55 and 0.45 soma radii (±20 %). Primaries and their children always fork and about half the depth-2 twigs fork again. On resize each primary's subtree gets its own scale k, the largest that fits left of, above or below the soma (at most 1.3 r and 1.6× the tightest subtree's k: the room is mostly above and below, and terminal rows are coarse). The drawn depth follows the soma radius r: depth 1 below r = 4 cells, depth 2 below 8, depth 3 from 8 up, so a small neuron draws a few long branches instead of a scribble. A twig spanning under 2 cells (`max(|Δx|/2, |Δy|/4)` in dots) is dropped with its subtree; a short primary is stretched to 2 cells instead;
  - the terminal branches, the electrode and stimulus markers, and the trace panel rectangle.

  Tiers: the full neuron when w ≥ 24 and h ≥ 8; otherwise only the axon, as one Pixels row spanning the width. The tube holds 4 pixel rows of axoplasm (a 3-cell tube) when h ≥ 40, else 2. The far-end marker `<` sits just right of the boutons, clamped on screen. The trace panel appears only when w ≥ 56 and h ≥ 16, starting at row 1 and column 0.625 w, and ideally ending at row 1 + 0.27 h. The trace's after-hyperpolarisation dip lies on or just above the panel's bottom row, so that row ends at least two rows above the top bouton, with one blank row between, and the panel shrinks to fit (at least 4 rows). If 4 rows do not fit, the panel keeps its height and ends three columns left of the arbor's first column (the trunk end), provided it keeps at least 12 columns; otherwise there is no panel. It always ends at least two columns from the right edge (x1 ≤ w − 3), the boutons' margin. A dotted lead joins the electrode to the scope. It climbs from the cell above the electrode to the trace's resting baseline and then runs along the baseline's dot row to one blank cell before the `-65` tick. Where the electrode sits under the tick or the panel, the lead stops one row below them. Drawing happens in `update` and `resize` (no `RefCell`): the axon, soma and boutons go into a `raster::Pixels`, the dendrites, arbor and trace into one `raster::Dots` and the vesicles into another; `render` only blits them and writes markers and labels.
- **Inks**: axoplasm, soma fill and boutons use `Role::Div`. For V ≥ rest the value is `x = √((V − V_rest)/105 mV)`, peaking at ≈ +0.99; the square root keeps millivolt-scale subthreshold responses visible. Below rest it is `x = −0.55·((V_rest − V)/12 mV)^1.5` (≈ −0.48 at the −75.9 mV minimum): the shallow tail of the after-hyperpolarisation stays near the background and only the deep, recent wake reads cool, quieter than the spike. Filled regions (axoplasm, soma fill) are left blank within 6 Div levels above rest (≈ +0.2 mV) and 12 below (≈ −3.7 mV), so a resting axon reads as its two membrane lines and the half-block glyphs change as a spike passes. Rest (level 128) sits near the background, so outlines carry the form: `Structure` levels 60–110 for the membrane lines above and below the axon, the soma rim, dendrites and terminal branches. The recording electrode, stimulus markers and trace are `Accent`; the electrode's lead is `Dim` 60 and dotted (every other dot); the trace baseline and the `+40`/`-65` ticks are `Dim`, and `+40` shows only while the window holds a sample above −20 mV (it labels a spike's scale and would float alone over a flat baseline); vesicles are `Particle`; the label is `Text`.
- **ASCII**: in the `Pixels::blit` closure, Div pixels map by level: `≥ 242` → `@` (V ≳ +20 mV), `≥ 211` → `#` (V ≳ −20 mV), `≥ 145` → `+` (V ≳ −63 mV), `< 84` → `~` (V ≲ −74 mV, the deep, recent after-hyperpolarisation only), else rest. At rest the axon is `=`. Structure-only cells are `=` on the axon membrane and `o` on the boutons. The soma is drawn at cell resolution: its rim is the ellipse of radii r × r/2 cells (cells are two pixels tall), rim cells being those inside with a 4-neighbour outside, drawn `o` whatever the voltage; cells inside show the fill's voltage glyph. Dendrites, the terminal arbor and the trace are line art, one glyph per cell. Dendrites and arbor use slope glyphs (`-` `/` `\` `|`). Dendrites are drawn shallowest first, each child skipping its first cell (its parent's last), and never overwrite a drawn cell, so the parent's stroke wins every junction. An arbor cell above rest shows its voltage glyph (`+` `#` `@`), except at the junction with the trunk. Below rest an arbor cell keeps its stroke and a bouton keeps its `o`, in the `Div` ink, so the cool colour carries the after-hyperpolarisation and the fan never dissolves into `~`. The `~` of the deep wake is kept for the axon and soma fill, where the pulse pattern below depends on it. The trace uses `|` where it crosses a cell top to bottom, `/` or `\` where it rises or falls within one, and `_` or `-` for a flat run in a cell's lower or upper half; the baseline shows as `.` ticks only where the trace has left it. Vesicles use the Dots density fallback. The electrode is `v` and the stimulus markers are `>` and `<`. The lead is `:` in every row of its climb and `.` in every other column along the baseline, drawn first so that everything else overwrites it. A spike on the axon reads `==~~~~~~~~===++++#####@@@@#+++===` moving right: `~` marks only the deep, recent wake, so the resting `=` frames each pulse.
- **Label**: right-aligned on the last row with `draw::label_right` (GEN-R13), drawn only when h ≥ 3. The form depends on the room right of the title (`title::readout_room`); each threshold is the form's length with the longest beat, so the form never flickers with the beat:
  - room ≥ 62: `Hodgkin-Huxley 1952 | squid axon 6.3C | v=12.3 m/s | collision`;
  - room ≥ 38: `HH squid 6.3C | v=12.3 m/s | collision`;
  - room ≥ 32: `HH 6.3C | v=12.3 m/s | collision`;
  - room ≥ 7: `HH 6.3C`;
  - none below.

  Every form with a velocity keeps the temperature: 12.3 m/s is right for 6.3 °C, not the textbook 21 m/s at 18.5 °C. `v` is measured live from the latest orthodromic spike's 0 mV crossings at 3 cm and 7 cm. The 3 cm crossing must come first, with the 7 cm crossing 1.5–6 ms later, so antidromic and colliding spikes are ignored. The readout shows `v=--.- m/s` before the first spike, then 12.3 after the first loop's single spike (12.4 in every later loop, in the collision's supernormal wake; see Refractory timing) and 10.9 → 9.8 along the train, and holds through the collision and threshold beats. It returns to `--.-` when the loop wraps, so the slowest train spike never sits beside `single`. The last field is the current beat name. Everything is ASCII, formatted in a stack buffer.
- **Perf**: 6.67 sub-steps × 200 compartments ≈ 1.3k compartment updates per frame (9 `exp`, ~40 flops and a Thomas sweep each). Rasterising covers ≈ 800 axon pixels, ≈ 150 soma pixels, ≤ 40 dendrite segments and ≤ 400 trace columns, then the blits. Measured ≈ 0.09 ms per `update(1/60)` + `render` at 200×50 (criterion, 2026-09-27: 89.8 μs). `dt = 0.25` costs 100 sub-steps, ≈ 1.5 ms. If the bench ever shows > 0.4 ms, tabulate the six rates at build time (0.1 mV steps on `[−100, +60]` mV, with linear interpolation).
- **Acceptance**: `AP-A1` with gates at steady state, `I_ion(V) = 0` has its root at `−65.0 ± 0.1 mV` (bisection on `[−80, −50]`), an unstimulated cable stays within ±0.05 mV of rest for 50 ms, and the α helpers are finite and continuous at −40 and −55 mV (`|α(V₀ ± 1e-7) − α(V₀)| < 1e-6`); `AP-A2` the propagated spike at 5 cm (strong pulse) peaks in `[+34, +42]` mV (reference +37.9), dips to a minimum in `[−77.0, −74.5]` mV (−75.9) and is back within 0.5 mV of rest 25 ms after the peak, and over one full 18 s protocol every gate stays in `[0, 1]` and every compartment's V in `[E_K − 0.5, E_Na]` (prototype −76.2 to +42.2 mV; ≈ 1 s in a debug build); `AP-A3` the conduction velocity between the 0 mV crossings at 3 cm and 7 cm is `12.3 m/s ± 3 %` at 6.3 °C with the scene discretisation (fine-grid reference 12.32, scene scheme 12.26); `AP-A4` with `φ = 3^{(18.5 − 6.3)/10}` the same solver gives `18.8 m/s ± 3 %`, HH 1952's computed velocity (scene scheme 18.55); `AP-A5` all-or-none: the scene's weak pulse (45 μA/cm²) from rest keeps V at 3 cm below −60 mV, while strong pulses at 1.5×, 3× and 6× the 90 μA/cm² threshold give peaks at 5 cm within 0.3 mV of one another; `AP-A6` refractoriness and collision: strong pulses 5 ms apart give exactly one 0 mV crossing at 7 cm, pulses 10 ms apart give two (the second slower), and simultaneous pulses at both ends give exactly one crossing at each end and at mid-axon, with max V < −60 mV 10 ms after the pulses meet (≈ 1 s in a debug build); `AP-A7` `60 × update(1/60)` and `4 × update(0.25)` leave bitwise-identical V and gate arrays (both runs execute the same 400 sub-steps), and each 18 s loop issues exactly the scripted pulses (0.2, 3.0, 5.5, 8.0 s; 13.0 s at both ends; 16.0 s weak) in order, repeating identically in the next loop; `AP-A8` over one protocol loop the live velocity readout prints exactly `12.3` after the first loop's single spike, then 10.9, 10.1 and 9.8 m/s after the three train spikes (each ± 0.3 m/s), and the collision and threshold beats leave it unchanged; the readout returns to `--.-` when the loop wraps, and the second and third loops' single spikes both print exactly `12.4`; `AP-A9` the drawn dendrites, for seeds 1–24 at 24×8, 40×12, 56×16, 80×24, 120×40 and 200×50: exactly three primaries are drawn, every drawn segment spans at least 2 cells, and no two drawn segments cross (a shared endpoint is not a crossing).

### aurora  (Earth & Sky, M10)
- **Intent**: a subauroral night, looking north over a still lake. A quiet arc low in the north brightens suddenly and breaks into folding, rayed curtains that climb and fill the sky, green below and capped with crimson above, with a pink hem along the lower border at the substorm's peak. It then fades into softly pulsating patches. Between the curtains the sky stays black: light is drawn as coherent arcs, rayed curtains and patches, never as scattered speckle. The focal point is the lower border of the brightest curtain, which is the sharpest and brightest edge in the frame. A black spruce ridge and its rippling reflection anchor the bottom, and the sparse stars never compete. At 200×50 the shore is row 40. At scene start the arch's crest stands ≈ 18° up (≈ 12 rows above the shore) with both ends sagging into the horizon, and at the expansion peak the curtains fill rows ≈ 2–38. At 80×24 the shore is row 19 and the crest starts ≈ 6 rows above it.
- **Model**: *View*: every object lives in sky angles (azimuth α, east positive; elevation e) or in world km, projected equirectangularly onto a full-screen `Pixels` lattice (P = 2h pixel rows). The horizon row is `y_h = round(0.80·P)` with a lake below it, or `round(0.92·P)` with no lake when h < 14. The scale is `f = max(y_h/62°, W/π)` px/rad, with `x = W/2 + f·α` and `y = y_h − f·e`, so the frame is ≈ 155° × 62° at 200×50 and ≈ 130° × 62° at 80×24. *Curtains*: 1–3 thin vertical sheets aligned east–west. Sheet c lies at northward ground distance `D_c(s,t) = D0_c(t) + ξ_c(s,t)` of the along-arc coordinate s (east positive). It is sampled at M = 480 fixed points `s_j = 300 km·tan α_j`, with α_j uniform in ±85°. The displacement is `ξ = Σ_{k=1..4} w_kj·a_k sin(κ_k s + θ_k)` with λ = 900/240/70/18 km (bend/surge/fold/curl), `a_1 = 40 km` and `a_k = F(t)·σ_k/κ_k` for slope amplitudes σ = 0.5/1.3/0.8. The weight `w_kj = clamp(λ_k/(2Δs_j) − 1, 0, 1)` fades a mode out wherever it has fewer than 4 samples per wavelength, so flank folds never alias.
