Phases integrate as `θ_k −= κ_k c_k dt`, so mode k drifts at velocity c_k, with c = +0.3, −v_s(t), +1.2, −0.8 km/s (negative is westward; v_s is the westward-travelling surge). Speed changes therefore never make the pattern jump. Each sample lies at ground distance `d = hypot(s, D)` and azimuth `α = atan2(s, D)`. An altitude z maps to `e = atan((z − d²/2R⊕)/d)` (flat earth plus curvature drop, R⊕ = 6371 km). A straight east–west arc is therefore an arch that sags into both horizons. The sheet doubles back on screen wherever `ξ' > cot|α|`; for the fold band's slope of 1.3 that happens only beyond |α| ≈ 38°, which is how real folds read in the oblique sky. *Precipitation*: `Φ = A_c(t)·B·((1 − r) + r·ρ/ρ̄)`. B is a westward-drifting bulge `1 + b(t)·exp(−((α − α_b)/w_b)²)`. The ray field is `ρ = ⅓Σ_m max(0, cos(κ_m(s − v_m t) + φ_m))⁶` with λ_m = 23/37/61 km and `v_m = v_r(t)·{1, −0.6, 0.8}`. Its mean is ρ̄ = 5/32, so switching rays on moves precipitation into the rays without changing its average; the ray-averaged flux is `Φ̄ = A·B`. The mean energy E0(t) sets the lower border `z_b = 131 − 25·log10(E0/1 keV)` km, clamped to [85, 150]. This is a fit to the peak energy-deposition altitudes of Rees (1963). *Emission* at altitude z has three components (rays also raise the green column by up to 1.8×, since H grows inside a ray and the profile is not renormalised). Green OI 557.7 nm is `G = Φ·S·g(z − z_b)` with `g(u) = (1 − e^{−(u+2)/2})·e^{−u/H}` for u ≥ −2 km (else 0). It peaks ≈ 3 km above z_b, and `H = 22 km·(1 + 0.8 r ρ)` so that rays reach higher. Green is prompt (the O(¹S) lifetime is 0.74 s). Red OI 630.0 nm is `R = R_j·min(S, 2)·0.4·exp(1 − y − e^{−y})/(1 + e^{−(z−190)/12})` with y = (z − 240)/50 km: a Chapman production layer times collisional quenching of O(¹D) below ~200 km. O(¹D) atoms diffuse tens of km during their 110 s lifetime, so the red layer is thick: its slant gain is capped at 2, and the red is smoothed along the arc by a ≈ 4° Gaussian (three running-sum box passes per pixel row) before it is thresholded, so where folds double back the red ramps instead of standing as a wall. It is displayed at 0.27× (the photopic ratio V(630)/V(557.7)), so the diffuse fringe stays a fringe. The red source relaxes exactly as `R_j ← Φ + (R_j − Φ)·e^{−dt/τ_R}` with τ_R = 3 s. That is the 110 s radiative lifetime compressed only enough to keep the lag visible (on the 144× substorm clock it would be 0.76 s), so the red is smooth, rayless and lingers. The pink N₂ 1PG hem marks type-B aurora, where fast electrons reach heights at which O(¹S) is quenched. It is `P = Φ·S·clamp((E0 − 8)/12, 0, 1)` for z_b − 4 ≤ z ≤ z_b + 1 km, splatted for the brightest curtain only, and it always includes the layer's lowest lit pixel, so it never vanishes at small sizes. A pixel is pink only where P ≥ 0.25 G and the pink's own tone `1 − e^{−P/E_sat}` (after extinction) is ≥ 0.6; isolated hem pixels with no hem neighbour in the adjacent columns are demoted to green (keeping their level), so the hem reads as a run. The slant factor `S = min(1/|n̂·l̂|, 6)` uses the sheet normal n ∝ (−ξ', 1, 0) (east, north, up) and the line of sight l̂ to the sample's lower border. It is the path length through a thin sheet, so folds seen edge-on become bright caustic "rays". *Simplifications*: sheets and rays are vertical (field-aligned; valid for the northern sky seen from a subauroral site) and arcs are strictly east–west. The red source ignores E0 (real 630 nm weakens as precipitation hardens), and its 0.4 normalisation makes the red column ≈ 2.2× the green one, 10–45× the photometric I630/I557.7 of keV aurora, for a vivid fringe; the photopic weight 0.27 brings the displayed ratio to ≈ 0.6. *Raster*: consecutive samples are splatted as column spans into per-column layers {d, z_b, H, G/R/P amplitudes}, at most 6 per column (one crossing per curtain plus room for three more where folds double back; a seventh merges into the nearest d). A fold that doubles back gives a column several layers, and they add. Pixel row y samples each layer at `z = d·tan e_y + d²/2R⊕`. This uses a per-resize tan e row table, a 0.5-km green LUT in u with H quantised to 16 steps, and a 1-km red LUT in z. The sum is multiplied by the extinction `exp(−0.12(1/sin max(e, 2°) − 1))` and tone-mapped as `1 − e^{−E/E_sat}` (E_sat = 0.9) through a LUT. Emission fades in under the top edge over 20 % of the pixel rows (at least 3 px) by smoothstep, so a crown overhead thins into dark sky and the label stays in clear air. *Thresholds, not dither*: the theme floors lit roles at 30 % brightness, so faint light cannot be shown by level; it is left dark rather than scattered as a dither. The pulsating-patch green Q (see *Recovery*) adds to the tone but is kept apart from the curtains' green G. Where the curtains' own green tone is < 0.03 the red is dropped (below notice), so patch light never opens the crown's gate. A pixel whose red share R/(G+Q+R) is ≤ ½ is green if its tone is ≥ 0.14, at level `12 + 243·(tone − 0.14)/0.86`. A red-dominant pixel is the crown if its tone is ≥ 0.2 *and* the curtains' green tone there is ≥ 0.03, at level `12 + 98·(tone − 0.2)/0.8` in `Accent`, so every lit level starts from the core floor `ink::MIN_VISIBLE_LEVEL` = 12 (CORE-R66): the crown caps each ray and fold with crimson and so follows the curtains' envelope; diffuse red far above the green would be cut flat by the top fade (a lid) and stand as a slab over the sky. A hem pixel is `Accent` at `190 + 65·(tone − 0.14)/0.86`. Every lit run thus starts from the theme's dimmest shade of its role, each column lights contiguous runs where its emission profile crosses the threshold, and rays that reach higher end higher, so a curtain's top edge is combed into vertical streaks. Finally specks are dropped: a lit sky pixel with no lit neighbour (of 8), and a pair whose two pixels neighbour only each other, so a fading arc end or a distant arc on the horizon never crumbles into grit. *Setting*: a far-shore ridge (3-octave value noise in α, 0.8–4° high) is topped by seeded spruce spires (1.5–4° apart, 1.2–3° tall, half-width 0.28× height); above f = 60 px/rad a second, nearer forest of fewer, taller spires (2.5–6° apart, 2.5–4° tall, half-width 0.35×) replaces them so each shows triangular flanks. Below it the far spires are stretched upward (widths unchanged, so they get spikier) until a 3° spire (the height cap) stands 3 px: at 80×24 a 3° spruce is under 2 px and the comb would read as a ruled line. Silhouette pixels are never lit. A `Seq` skyglow lights every sky pixel below the glow height `max(f·8°, tallest silhouette + 1.5 px, 3 px)`, from 0.2 at the horizon falling quadratically to 0.02 at its top, solid throughout: the ramp's black end is a dark navy, so the glow stops just above it rather than being carried into it, and the ridge and spires cut a crisp black edge into it. The lake mirrors the sky about y_h at 0.45× level, with their roles: the skyglow, and each column's lit sky runs whole wherever a run's brightest pixel has level ≥ 40 (a fainter run's copy could not be dimmer than the theme's floor, while cutting the dim ends off brighter runs would leave shards), emission copies never below level 12; the ridge and spires mirror as a black band cut out of the reflected glow. Its ripple is `δx = round(0.04 d·sin(0.9d − 1.7t + 0.13x))`, where d is the number of pixel rows below the shore, so the wavefronts travel toward the viewer while the water stays still. Whole rows drop out where `sin(0.9d − 1.7t) > 0.9`, so mirrored rays read as vertical streaks cut by horizontal ripple gaps. Stars come from a seeded 1200-star catalogue in (α, e) with magnitudes. They are shown brightest-first until 2 % of sky cells hold one, so the limiting magnitude adapts to resolution. They twinkle more toward the horizon and are drawn only in cells whose two pixels are empty.
- **Motion**: a `Timeline` runs growth 12 s → onset 3 s → expansion 15 s → recovery 20 s. The 50 s loop compresses a ~2 h substorm ≈ 144× (Akasofu 1964), while folds and rays move at real km/s speeds. Keyframes are smoothstep-interpolated, except that every segment starting at the onset uses `ease::out_quad` (the sudden breakup), and every one returns to its growth-start value by the end of recovery. The scene clock starts at t = 8 s, so breakup lands 4 s into a default 15 s visit. *Growth*: two faint homogeneous arcs drift equatorward, toward the viewer (D0₁ 520 → 260 km, A₁ 0.35 → 0.4, A₂ 0.3, E0 2 → 4 keV, F 0.15, r 0.04, v_r 1 km/s), each a clear band above its threshold rather than a broken line hovering at it. *Onset*: the equatorward arc brightens 2.5× within 1.5 s (`ease::out_quad`). E0 rises to 20 keV, so the lower border drops ≈ 18 km and the pink hem appears. Rays switch on (r → 0.8, v_r → 12 km/s). *Expansion*: the bulge swells both ways. Arc 1 moves toward the zenith (D0₁ → 170 km, base ≈ 31°) while arcs 2–3 light up poleward, arc 2 at D0₁ + 150 km closing to + 110 km within 9 s, arc 3 at D0₁ + 330 km. F rises to 1.0, so folds double back in the oblique sky. The bulge surges west (α_b +10° → −40°, v_s → 6 km/s), and E0 settles to 10 keV. *Recovery*: the arcs dim and retreat poleward (D0 → 520 km; E0, F, r, v_r back to their growth values), and the red fringe outlives the green by τ_R. Six pulsating patches fade in and out: seeded one per 15° stratum across α ±45° (±3° jitter, so neighbours drifting together keep apart instead of fusing into one blob) and at e 25–45° (mid-sky, clear of the top fade), soft Gaussian 6–12° × 3–6° ellipses cut at 1.6 half-extents, with half-extents floored at min(5 px, 0.07 W) × min(3 px, P/12) (at 80×24 a 6° patch is only 4 columns, a smudge; the floor is 5 × 3 px there and at 200×50 and 2.8 × 2 px at 40×12, so a small frame's patches never swell into clouds half the sky wide), period 4–12 s, on-fraction 0.4 with 0.4 s edges, drifting east 0.5°/s, green at 0.45× the arc peak, so a patch at full pulse stays dimmer than the recovery arc. Pulsating aurora has no red cap: patch light is kept out of the crown's gate (*Thresholds*), so a patch in the lingering red dome raises no crimson hood. Activity a(t) runs 0.02 → 0.15 (end of growth) → 0.55 (end of onset) → 1.0 (t = 22 s) → 0.75 (end of expansion) → 0.02 and sets `AL = −(30 + 800a)` nT. In any 1 s window the lake ripple alone changes pixel occupancy (GEN-R9 compares glyphs), and rays and folds add more.
- **Resize**: there is no simulation lattice, so ADR-0012 bucketing does not apply. All state lives in world or sky coordinates and survives any resize untouched: fold and ray phases, red sources, clock, star catalogue, trees and patches. Resize rebuilds only the view: f, y_h, the tan e and extinction row tables, the per-column silhouette, the visible-star list, the layer scratch and `Pixels`. A resize re-frames the same sky. At 1×1 the view is sky-only and nothing panics.
- **Inks**: green `Particle` 12–255; red crown `Accent` 12–110 (a deep magenta-crimson: dark `Hot` is a rust brown and bright `Hot` an orange, while the 630 nm crown reads crimson over green in photographs); pink hem `Accent` ≥ 190, well clear of the crown; skyglow `Seq` 0.02–0.2; stars `Star`; ASCII ridge `Dim` 70; label `Text`. Reflections reuse the source role at 0.45× level (emission copies at least 12). Silhouettes stay empty (black).
- **ASCII**: every cell takes the brighter emission pixel of its pair (a hem pixel wins, so the hem stays a line); skyglow returns `None`. Sky: green is `|` (≥ 200), `!` (≥ 130), `:` (≥ 70), else `,` (so a quiet arc never reads as the dim stars `.`); the crown is `"` (≥ 60), else `'`; the hem is `=`. Lake cells are `~` (≥ 60), else `-`. After the blit, in ASCII mode only, the ridge top is drawn as one continuous `_` line (the cell holding each column's top ridge pixel), with a spire tip `^` in the cell just above it wherever a spire rises above the line (one comb however tall the stretched spires), in still-blank cells, `Dim` 70. Stars are `.`, with `*` for the brightest 10 %, and also need a still-blank cell. At 80×24 this reads as `,:!|` columns capped with `'"`, arching over a `^^_` shore and `~` water.
- **Label**: top-left `AL -620 nT  expansion` (`Text` 150). AL is rounded to 10 nT and written digit by digit into a stack buffer, and the phase word is the `Timeline` beat label. The phase word is dropped when w < 60, and the whole label when w < 24 or h < 10.
- **Perf** at 200×50: update ≈ 0.35 ms. Sampling costs ≈ 0.09 ms (3 × 480 samples × 4 fold sines, 3 ray cos⁶ and a red relaxation, with one exp per call), independent of terminal size; the rest is the rasterisation, which runs in `update`: projection and spans ≈ 0.08 ms; sky shading ≈ 16k px × ≤ 2 live layers of LUT reads ≈ 0.15 ms, skipping rows above the emission top; lake via per-row ripple tables ≈ 0.02 ms; silhouette. Render (blit, stars, the ASCII ridge and the label) ≈ 0.05 ms. Total ≈ 0.45 ms (measured 0.47 ms in criterion at load ≈ 14, 2026-09-27, after the patch and whole-run lake rework), with no allocation after resize. Thresholding and the speck pass cost ≈ 0.02 ms, the lake's per-column run-peak scan ≈ 0.01 ms; nothing is precomputed per process.
- **Acceptance**: `AUR-A1` `lower_border_km(e0)` is strictly decreasing on [1, 60] keV, stays within [85, 150] km for any input, and gives 113.5, 106.0, 98.5 and 88.5 km (±0.1) at 5, 10, 20 and 50 keV; `AUR-A2` with H = 22 km (ρ = 0) and E0 ∈ {3, 10, 20} keV, the green profile peaks within [z_b, z_b + 6] km, is 0 below z_b − 2 km and is < 5 % of its peak above z_b + 80 km; the red profile peaks in [220, 260] km and is < 5 % of its peak below 170 km; with equal sources, red > green for all z ∈ [200, 450] km and red < 0.05·green for all z ∈ [z_b, 150] km; the pink profile is 0 outside [z_b − 4, z_b + 1] km and 0 for E0 ≤ 8 keV; `AUR-A3` from a unit step, `red_relax(r, target, dt)` reaches 1 − e⁻¹ (±1e-12) after τ_R = 3 s, composes exactly (0.1 s then 0.2 s equals 0.3 s within 1e-12) and never overshoots; along the onset keyframes A(t) at least doubles within 1.5 s of the onset start, while a red source relaxed toward A(t) in 1/60 s steps covers < 45 % of the jump at that moment; the mean of ρ over s ∈ [0, 10⁴] km at fixed t is 5/32 ± 0.005; `AUR-A4` `elevation_deg(105, 600)` is 7.29 ± 0.05°, `elevation_deg(105, 180)` is 29.65 ± 0.05°, and `altitude_km(elevation_deg(z, d), d)` returns z within 1e-9 km; with F = 0 and a_1 = 0, a curtain at D0 = 300 km projects to an arch whose lower-border elevation is maximal at α = 0 and strictly decreasing in |α|; `AUR-A5` (200×50 view, curtain at D0 = 300 km): with F = 0 and a_1 = 0, every column where the lower border is above the horizon gets exactly one layer; with only the fold band (σ = 1.3, F = 1), no column with |α| < 37° gets 2 layers from that curtain and at least one column with |α| > 38° does; S is ≥ 1, never exceeds 6, and equals 1/cos e_b (1e-9) for an unfolded arc at α = 0; `AUR-A6` the beats are growth 12 s → onset 3 s → expansion 15 s → recovery 20 s in order, the loop is 50 s, and a fresh scene starts at t = 8 s; every keyframed parameter is continuous across the wrap (|Δ| < 1e-9); `al_nt(t)` stays in [−150, −30] nT throughout growth and ≤ −450 nT throughout expansion; `patch_pulse(t, P)` is P-periodic (1e-12) with on-fraction (value > 0.5) 0.40 ± 0.02, and patches are 0 outside recovery; `AUR-A7` at 200×50, after 56 × `update(0.25)` (scene t = 22 s) and one render (< 1 s in debug): no silhouette pixel is lit, lit pixels are 15–70 % of non-silhouette sky pixels, every lit lake pixel has level ≤ 0.5× the brightest sky level, every star cell has both pixels empty, and at least one hem pixel (`Accent` ≥ 190) exists, while after 4 updates (t = 9 s) there is none; at t = 19, 21 and 22 s a hem exists, every hem pixel (`Accent` ≥ 190) lies within 4 px above its column's lowest pink-layer border (never inside a higher layer) and has a hem neighbour in an adjacent column; at t = 20, 22, 28 and 36 s every lake row where `sin(0.9d − 1.7t) > 0.9` is blank, every other lit lake pixel is a same-role copy at 0.45× level (emission copies at least 12) of a mirrored sky pixel (skyglow, or any pixel of a column's lit run whose brightest pixel has level ≥ 40) within 3 columns of its mirror, and the lake is 0.9–1.1× as densely lit as its mirrored source rows; every hem pixel has level ≥ 190; `AUR-A8` (rendering): below its threshold (0.14 green, 0.2 red-dominant) a pixel is dark, at it the level is 12 (`ink::MIN_VISIBLE_LEVEL`), never lower above it, at full tone 255 (green) or 110 (crown), monotone between; a red-dominant pixel over green tone < 0.03 is dark; a hem pixel is `Accent` ≥ 190; at 80×24 and 200×50, at visit t = 0, 5, 12, 20, 26, 34 and 42 s, pixels in specks (8-connected groups of ≤ 3) are ≤ 3 % of the lit sky; at scene start every column over the frame's middle third is lit and its lower border steps by ≤ 2 px between neighbouring columns; at visit t = 14, 18 and 22 s at 200×50 the crown has ≥ 100 pixels, ≥ 95 % of them with green lit within 12 px below in the same column, and its top edge spans ≥ 6 rows; the patch floor is 5 × 3 px at 80×24 and 200×50 and 2.8 × 2 px at 40×12, and over seeds 0–15 every patch is seeded within 3° of its stratum's centre; during recovery at 80×24 and 40×12 every live patch's half-extents are ≥ the floor and ≤ max(6° × 3°, the floor), and at full pulse it has a solid core (5 × 3 px at 80×24, 3 × 1 px at 40×12); at visit t = 32, 34 and 36 s at 80×24 and 200×50 the centre of every patch at full pulse is dimmer than the brightest curtain pixel outside every patch's support; at 200×50 at visit t = 26, 27 and 28 s (seeds 7 and 42) no crown pixel lies within 2 px of a live patch's ellipse unless curtain green is lit within 12 px below it in the same column; at 40×12, 80×24 and 200×50 some spire rises ≥ 2 px over the bare ridge, the glow height is ≥ the tallest silhouette + 1.5 px, the sky pixel above every silhouette column is lit (glow or aurora), and no silhouette pixel's mirror in the lake holds skyglow.

### belousov_zhabotinsky  (Reactions, M10)
- **Intent**: a Belousov–Zhabotinsky dish seen from above. Thin, pale oxidation fronts sweep a dark medium. Each trails a tail that cools through orange, red and violet to black as the medium recovers. Broken wavefronts curl at their free ends into spirals: counter-rotating pairs, or a single spiral where one end is pinned to the dish wall. A pacemaker nucleus pulses slower target rings, which the spiral wave trains gradually push back. Every 62 s a light quench, as in the photosensitive Ru-catalysed BZ (Kuhnert, Agladze & Krinsky 1989), puts every wave out, and fresh broken fronts restart the dish. `gray_scott` shows Gray–Scott blobs that grow in place and `liesegang_rings` shows static bands; here every feature travels and the resting medium is black.
- **Model**: Barkley (1991) excitable medium on a no-flux (Neumann) rectangle: `∂u/∂t = ∇²u + ε⁻¹ u(1 − u)(u − u_th)`, `u_th = (v + b)/a`, `∂v/∂t = u − v`, with `a = 0.75`, `b = 0.06`, `ε = 0.02` (rigidly rotating spirals; the EZ-Spiral default). `u` stands for the autocatalyst HBrO₂. `v` stands for the oxidised catalyst (ferriin / Ru(bpy)₃³⁺), whose reduction releases the Br⁻ inhibitor; this is the two-variable Oregonator picture. The node spacing is fixed at `h = 0.5` space units (su), so the pattern scale is fixed in nodes. Each sub-step `τ = 0.0125` time units (tu) is Lie-split into three stages. (1) Explicit diffusion, using the isotropic 9-point Laplacian (Patra–Karttunen, as in `gray_scott`) with mirrored ghost nodes. The stencil sums mirror pairs first, `(W+E)`, `(N+S)`, `(NW+NE)` and `(SW+SE)`, so the update is bitwise left–right symmetric. (2) The reaction, with Barkley's (1991) implicit update on the decaying branch, `u ← u / (1 − (τ/ε)(1 − u)(u − u_th))` if `u < u_th`, and an explicit growth branch, `u ← u + (τ/ε) u(1 − u)(u − u_th)` otherwise; the growth branch stays bounded because `τ/ε ≤ 1` (Barkley and EZ-Spiral take it implicitly too). (3) `v ← v + τ(u − v)`. Because `(10/3)·τ/h² = 0.17 ≤ 1`, `τ/ε = 0.625 ≤ 1` and `τ ≤ 1`, every stage is a convex combination or a shrinking factor. So `u, v ∈ [0, 1]` holds exactly for any `b ≥ 0`, with no clamps. Measured on this exact scheme (NumPy prototype):
  - planar pulse speed `c = 3.58` su/tu, which is 0.85 `c_N`, where `c_N = √(2/ε)(½ − b/a) = 4.2` is the Nagumo limit at `v = 0`; the continuum value at `h = 0.1` is ≈ 3.9, and the O(ε) recovery effect accounts for the rest of the gap;
  - spiral period 3.95–4.2 tu;
  - spiral wavelength λ ≈ 25 nodes;
  - excited plateau ≈ 8 nodes long.

  Pacing is `RATE = 1.5` tu per simulated second, exactly 2 sub-steps per 1/60 s, through an accumulator that handles any `dt` (30 sub-steps at `dt = 0.25`). Seeding draws from `ctx.rng` once per cycle and places up to `k = clamp(round(nodes / 2000), 1, 4)` broken fronts. Each front is a straight segment:
  - its length is `L ∈ [1.2, 2.0]·λ` at a uniform random angle;
  - its midpoint is uniform in the central 60 % of each axis;
  - if a free end falls within 4 nodes of a wall, or the segment comes within λ/2 of an earlier segment, `L` shrinks in 10 % steps down to 0.8 λ; if it still does not fit, the draw is rejected. The distance between segments is 0 if they cross, else the nearest endpoint-to-segment distance. Each painted band reaches at most 6 nodes from its segment, so bands never touch or cross. A front still rejected after 32 tries is dropped, and a crowded dish gets fewer fronts rather than two tangled ones (≤ 1 in 10 on the tested lattices). The first front has no earlier segments to avoid; if even it fails the walls, the last draw is shrunk as far as needed, but not below 3 nodes (a shorter front cannot launch a wave, BZ-A2), and otherwise clipped to the lattice;
  - with probability 0.3 the segment is instead extended to a wall from its end nearer one, which leaves one free end and gives a single spiral. Extending the far end would draw a front across the whole dish and leave no room for the others;
  - it is a 2-node strip of `u = 1` backed by a 6-node strip whose `v` ramps 0.69 → 0.36 away from the front. Every `v ≥ 0.315` gives `u_th > ½`, so the strip blocks backward propagation, and the ramp already shades as a tail.

  There is 1 pacemaker (2 when nodes ≥ 8000), at a normalised position in `[0.15, 0.85]²`. It first fires 1 tu into each cycle and then every `T_pm = 6` tu (≈ 1.5 × the spiral period), setting `u = 1` where `v < 0.1` in a disc of radius 2.5 nodes. The higher-frequency source wins (Zaikin & Zhabotinsky 1970; Winfree 1972), so the spiral domain grows into the target pattern. The story is a looping `Timeline [("waves", 60 s), ("quench", 2 s)]`. During the quench, `b` ramps 0.06 → 0.45 with `ease::smoothstep` over 0.5 s, so `u_th ≥ 0.6 > ½`. Fronts retreat, excited regions collapse once `v > a − b = 0.3`, pacemakers stay off, and `v` decays as `e^{−t}`. There is no excited node 1 s into the quench, and max `v` is ≈ 0.047 at its end (3 tu of `e^{−t}` decay), which shades nothing (`s = 0.55·(v/0.69)² < 0.06`). At the cycle boundary `b` resets and a new seed set is painted over the dark field.
- **Motion**: fronts advance ≈ 11 nodes/s, and a lattice node is ≈ 1 pixel column. Spirals turn every ≈ 2.8 s. At t = 0 the seeded fronts already read as short comets. By 1 s their free ends visibly curl, and by 5 s the spirals have formed. Over the minute, spiral wave trains push the target rings back toward their nucleus. On small dishes the takeover is quick: the 80×48 lattice at 80×24 is ≈ 3 × 2 wavelengths and fronts cross it in ≈ 7 s, so the spirals reach the pacemaker within seconds, after which it only fires into gaps between their fronts (5–9 of its 15 beats per cycle find recovered medium in its disc, mean 7.6 over seeds 1–15 and 42); the rings play out longer on large dishes. The quench fades the dish to black within ≈ 1 s and it stays dark for ≈ 1 s, then new fronts appear, brightening from dark over 0.5 s (display only: the model paints them at full strength). The very first seeding shows at once. The loop is 62 s.
- **Resize**: the lattice is `grid::lattice_for(Size(w, 2h), 20_000, 1.25)`, since Pixels are square, `w × 2h`. That gives 197×100 at 200×50 and exactly 80×48 at 80×24: below the cap the per-axis clamp makes the lattice the pixel grid itself. Above the cap, nodes are stretched relative to pixels by at most √1.25 (≤ 12 %). An unchanged lattice leaves the state untouched. Otherwise `u` and `v` are `resample_bilinear`ed into the new lattice (its convex weights keep `[0, 1]`), and the pattern relaxes to its natural λ within a few rotations. Pacemaker and seed positions are stored normalised in `[0, 1]²`. The first resize seeds. Resize also rebuilds per-axis sampling tables (source index and weight per pixel column and per pixel row). Shading happens in `update` and `resize`, with no `RefCell`: `u` and `v` are sampled bilinearly per pixel (the identity when the lattice equals the pixel grid) and shaded into a `raster::Pixels` without allocating; `render` only blits it and writes the label.
- **Inks**: `Role::Seq` encodes the *phase of the excitation cycle*, not a concentration. Let `r = clamp(v / (a − b₀), 0, 1)` with the unquenched `b₀ = 0.06`, so the quench ramp never brightens the tails. Excited cells (`u ≥ ½`) get `s = 1 − 0.45·r`, which is palest at the fresh front and orange at the back of the plateau. Refractory cells get `s = 0.55·r²`, going red → violet → dark as the medium recovers. `s < 0.06` gives `Ink::BLANK`, so resting medium is the terminal background. Every other pixel is `ink::seq(s)`. The prototype measured ≈ 3–6 % of pixels pale (`s ≥ 0.85`) and ≈ 40–52 % lit, so there are thin bright edges, dim tails and real dark gaps. The label is `Text`.
- **ASCII**: the `Pixels::blit` closure takes the larger Seq level of the two pixels: `≥ 217` → `@` (`s ≥ 0.85`), `≥ 179` → `#` (0.70), `≥ 102` → `+` (0.40), `≥ 51` → `:` (0.20), any other non-blank pixel → `.`, and two blank pixels → nothing. Fronts read as `@@##++::..` comets curling on white space.
- **Label**: right-aligned on row `h − 1` with `draw::label_right` (a blank cell each side, so it never touches the field; GEN-R13); there is no label when h < 4 or w < 14. The room is `min(title::readout_room, w/2)`, so the label never takes more than half the row. `{word}` is the beat: `waves`, or during the quench `light quench` (room ≥ 12) or `quench`. The name is not repeated: the title names the reaction.
  - room ≥ 44: `{word}  Barkley a=0.75 b=0.06 eps=0.02`;
  - room ≥ 20: `{word}  b=0.06`;
  - room ≥ 6: `{word}`.

  `b` is printed live to 2 decimals, so it reads 0.45 during the quench: the on-screen sign that the quench is a loss of excitability. The label is ASCII and written into a stack buffer without allocation.
- **Perf**: at 200×50 the lattice has 19.7k nodes × 2 sub-steps of ~30 f32 flops each, plus 20k bilinear pixel samples, the shading and the blit. Measured ≈ 0.31 ms (criterion, 2026-09-27: 310 μs) per `update(1/60)` + `render` at 200×50 (aim < 1 ms). `dt = 0.25` costs ≈ 2 ms (30 sub-steps). Reseeding paints ≤ 4 segments (≈ 2k nodes). There are no allocations after resize.
- **Acceptance**: `BZ-A1` `bz_step` maps any field with `u, v ∈ [0, 1]` back into `[0, 1]` with no clamping in the kernel (64 seeded random 24×16 fields, 50 sub-steps each, at `b = 0.06` and at `b = 0.45`), and the rest state `(0, 0)` is an exact fixed point; `BZ-A2` excitability is all-or-none on a 64×64 lattice: a disc of radius 4 nodes at `u = 0.9·b/a` (`v = 0`) decays to `max u < 1e-3` within 2 tu, while `u = 1` discs of radius 1.5 and 4 nodes each launch a ring whose outermost excited node (`u ≥ ½`) lies ≥ 12 nodes from the centre at 2 tu (prototype 13.0 and 17.0); `BZ-A3` a planar pulse on a 3×160 strip (scene `h` and `τ`; the `u = ½` front is fitted over nodes 40–120) travels at `c ∈ [0.75, 0.92]·c_N` with `c_N = 4.2` (prototype 0.85), and a pacemaker of period 6 tu at the strip's end emits fronts spaced `c·T_pm/h ± 10 %` apart, excluding the leading front (prototype 42–44 nodes against 43); `BZ-A4` a single spiral grown from the scene's wall-anchored broken front (from the top wall to the centre of a 64×64 lattice) rotates rigidly: after a 20 tu transient its period (`u = ½` up-crossings at a probe ≥ λ from the core) lies in `[3.7, 4.5]` tu (prototype 3.95–4.21), and in every 4 tu window up to 50 tu, the trailing 48–50 tu one included, its tip (`u = ½` ∩ `v = a/2 − b`, the EZ-Spiral definition, located per 2×2 block) stays inside a 6×6-node box (prototype ≤ 5×5; the core drifts ≈ 1 node per turn away from the walls, hence the window), with the probe ≥ λ from the window's mean tip (≈ 1–1.5 s in a debug build); `BZ-A5` a horizontal broken front with two free ends, centred on a 72×48 lattice, becomes a counter-rotating pair: at every sub-step up to 12 tu, `u` and `v` are bitwise mirror-symmetric about the line between columns 35 and 36, and at 12 tu the half-plane behind the seed holds excited nodes (`u ≥ ½`) on both sides of that line; `BZ-A6` the lattice never exceeds 20 000 nodes or the pixel grid in either axis (displays 1×1 to 400×120), a resize between two displays in the same aspect bucket that are both at or above the cap (240×60 → 250×62) leaves `u` and `v` bitwise unchanged, and any seeded resize sequence keeps `u, v ∈ [0, 1]`; `BZ-A7` at the end of the 2 s quench beat `max u < 1e-3` and `max v < 0.06` (prototype ≈ 0.047; every pixel is then blank, since `s = 0.55·(v/0.69)² < 0.06`), no pacemaker fires during the quench, each cycle re-seeds deterministically from `ctx.rng` (same seed ⇒ same fronts and pacemakers; consecutive cycles differ), on lattices of at least 64×40 (50 seed sets each at 64×40, 80×48, 197×100 and 100×64) every segment that is not wall-anchored keeps both ends ≥ 4 nodes from the walls, no two segments come within λ/2 of each other, and at most 1 in 10 fronts is dropped for want of room, while a 20×8 lattice still gets its one front; `segment_gap` is 0 for crossing segments and the nearest endpoint-to-segment distance otherwise; and a reseed after the first shows dark on screen one sub-step in (brightest level < 51) while the model holds it at full strength (`max u > 0.9`), then brightens to full brightness (level ≥ 217) within 0.5 s, its brightest level never falling from one frame to the next; `BZ-A8` composition at 80×24 with seed 42: at t = 10, 20 and 30 s the share of non-blank pixels lies in `[0.20, 0.70]` and the share of pale pixels (`s ≥ 0.85`) in `[0.01, 0.12]` (prototype 0.41–0.52 and 0.03–0.06).

### boids_flocking  (Living Systems, M10)
- **Intent**: a starling murmuration at dusk. About a thousand specks pour and fold like smoke above the roost, and the flock's texture shifts as it banks. Every 14 s a falcon, the only warm glyph on screen, stoops into it. A bright agitation wave then runs through the flock faster than any bird flies, and the murmuration bulges away from the attack and closes behind it. Birds copy a neighbour's escape manoeuvre (Hemelrijk et al. 2015), so the wave outruns any bird, as Potts (1984) observed in dunlin flocks when he found manoeuvres passing between neighbours faster than a bird's startle reaction (his chorus-line hypothesis).
- **Model**: Reynolds (1987) boids in 2-D (the sky plane) on the square braille dot lattice `W × H = 2w × 4h` dots (CORE-R41; 1 dot ≈ 0.3 m at scale σ = 1). The open sky is `W × H_s`, with `H_s = H − 4` (the label row) when the label is drawn and `H_s = H` otherwise. Population `N = clamp(round(0.1·w·h), 24, 1000)` (80×24 ⇒ 192, 200×50 ⇒ 1000). Length scale `σ = clamp(√(8wh / (80 N)), 0.5, 4)`, which is 1 whenever N is uncapped, so the flock always covers the same share of the sky. **Small sky**: `ρ = clamp((min(W, H_s) − 44)/16, 0, 1)` is 0 up to a 44-dot shorter side (a 12-row terminal with its label) and 1 from 60 dots (16 rows) up. At full size and speed a flock crosses a small sky in about a second and is pressed against its walls, so there the scene uses `σ·lerp(0.7, 1, ρ)` (clamped to [0.5, 4] again), roost stiffness `lerp(2.0, κ, ρ)` s⁻², margin fraction `lerp(0.2, 0.25, ρ)`, and roost amplitudes multiplied by ρ. At 80×24 and larger ρ = 1, and nothing changes. Every length, speed and acceleration below is multiplied by σ, except the wall margin `m`. **Neighbourhood**: the topological `k = 7` nearest within a metric horizon `R = 12` dots (3.6 m), after Ballerini et al. (2008, `n_c ≈ 6.5`) and Hemelrijk & Hildenbrandt (2011, StarDisplay). The core `SpatialHash` (CORE-R54) with cell size `R/4` is rebuilt every substep. Each query first tries the disc reaching the farthest of the bird's previous k neighbours at their current positions (its radius padded by 1e-9 relative, so the square root and the re-squaring never drop that farthest neighbour), and falls back to the full horizon R when that disc holds fewer than k birds. Once a disc holds k birds everything outside it is strictly farther, so the answer is exact for any hint. It keeps the k best in a fixed `[(d², idx); 7]` insertion array, with ties broken by index. The lists `nbr[N][7]` are built once per substep and shared by forces, contagion and rendering. **Acceleration of bird i** is the sum of five terms. (1) Separation `A_s Σ_{j∈N_i, d<r_s} (1 − d/r_s)(x_i − x_j)/d` with `A_s = 150` dots/s² and `r_s = 4` dots (1.2 m). A pair with `d < 1e-6` pushes along ±x instead, and the lower index goes −x. (2) Alignment `β(v̄_N − v_i)` with `β = 5` s⁻¹. (3) Cohesion `γ(x̄_N − x_i)` with `γ = 1` s⁻². (4) Speed relaxation `(v₀ − |v|)/τ_v · v̂` with `v₀ = 30` dots/s (9 m/s, starling cruise) and `τ_v = 0.5` s. (5) Roost attraction `κ(x_roost − x̄) + κ_i(x̄ − x_i)`, where `x̄` is the flock's centroid. The first part, with `κ = 0.45` s⁻², is the same for every bird, so it steers the whole flock around the roost (period `2π/√κ ≈ 9.4` s) without squeezing it. A polarised flock cruising at v₀ orbits the roost at radius `v₀/√κ ≈ 45` dots, which fits the 96-dot height of an 80×24 sky (κ = 0.3 would give 55). The second part keeps strays with the flock: `κ_i = κ` for N ≤ 300, falling log-linearly in N to 0.03 s⁻² at N = 1000, because a trap as stiff as κ on every bird of a large flock squeezes it into a round, evenly grainy ball (BOID-A7). The roost moves on a Lissajous path `(W/2 + a_x sin 2πt/23, H_s/2 + a_y sin 2πt/37)` with `a_x = min(0.25 W, max(0, W/2 − R_f − m))` and `a_y = min(0.2 H_s, max(0, H_s/2 − R_f − m))`, where `R_f = √(12N/π)·σ` is the flock's radius, so a flock at the roost clears the soft-bound margin. Soft bounds add `A_b·min(((m − d_edge)/m)², 4)` inward (`A_b = 200` dots/s²) within `m = 0.25·min(W, H_s)` dots of an edge of the sky; this wide, gentle cushion turns the flock back through the middle of the sky instead of piling it against the frame. The total is capped at `|a| ≤ 250` dots/s². After the velocity update, the velocity is turned by heading noise `θ ~ gaussian(0, √(2 D_θ h))` with `D_θ = 0.1` rad²/s, applied as the small-angle rotation `(v + θ v⊥)/√(1 + θ²)`, and `|v|` is clamped to `[0.5, 2]·v₀`. **Integration**: synchronous semi-implicit Euler at `h = SIM_DT = 1/60` s (all accelerations come from the old state and go into `vel_next`, then the buffers swap), with the substep count from the core `FixedStep(SIM_DT, 15)` (CORE-R9..R11). A specular wall at the edges of the sky `[0, W] × [0, H_s]` (the label row is not sky) reflects the position and negates the normal velocity. It is only a safety net; the soft bounds do the work (BOID-A4). **Falcon**: a looping 14 s `Timeline`. Patrol 7 s: slow 4 s circles of radius `r_c = 0.8 v₀ · 4 s / 2π` (≈ 15 dots) around an anchor over whichever upper corner is farther from the flock, inset `max(0.12 W, r_c + 4σ)` from the side wall and `max(0.2 H_s, r_c + 4σ)` from the top (never past the middle of the sky), at 0.8 v₀ and turning ≤ 2 rad/s. Stoop 2.5 s: speed eased from 0.8 to 3 v₀ with `ease::in_out_cubic`, steering toward the flock centroid predicted 0.5 s ahead, turning ≤ 3 rad/s. Pursue 2 s: 2 v₀, turning ≤ 1.5 rad/s, so the birds can out-turn it. Climb 2.5 s: 1.5 v₀ back onto the patrol circle, which it joins tangentially instead of overshooting the anchor. In every beat it heeds the walls: within `r_c` of an edge of the sky, `3·min(((r_c − d)/r_c)², 4)` along the inward normal (the birds' soft-bound profile with margin `r_c`) is added to its unit desired heading, and while heading into a wall its speed is divided by `1 + 2·max(0, −v̂·push)` (not below 0.8 v₀). Should it still reach an edge of the sky, it reflects specularly, so it never slides along the frame (BOID-A4). It never removes birds. **Escape wave**: Hemelrijk et al. (2015) showed that agitation waves arise only when birds repeat an escape manoeuvre, and that the dark band comes from orientation, not density. Each stoop is an attack with an id, and alarms can fire only inside that attack's stoop and pursue windows. A calm bird fires at most once per attack, in one of two ways. Directly: the falcon is within `r_flee = 12` dots, and the turn sign points away from it. By contagion: a topological neighbour fired at least `L = 2` substeps (33 ms) earlier and is still alarmed; the sign is copied from the lowest-index such neighbour. Fire steps are stored, so the outcome does not depend on update order. An alarmed bird (age < `T_a = 0.35` s = 21 substeps) adds a lateral `160` dots/s² with its sign and relaxes toward `1.3 v₀`. While the falcon is inside `r_flee` it also flees at `200(1 − d/r_flee)` dots/s². The front advances about one neighbour spacing every L substeps, ≈ 100–150 dots/s (30–45 m/s, 3–5 v₀), which is faster than any bird flies; Procaccini et al. (2011) measured wave speeds often above the flock's own speed. **Banking**: the coordinated-turn roll `φ = atan(a_⊥ / (gσ))` is signed (+ = right wing down), with `g = 32.7` dots/s² (9.81 m/s²), and is smoothed as such by an EMA (τ = 0.1 s), so the flickering pushes of separation average out and only a sustained turn banks a bird; the ink and the wings use `|φ|`. An alarmed bird's 160 dots/s² turn banks it past 75°. **Start**: a disc of radius `min(R_f, 0.3·min(W, H_s))` at the roost with uniformly random headings at v₀, so each visit opens with order emerging from chaos. All randomness comes from `ctx.rng` in index order.
- **Motion**: birds cross ≈ 15 cells/s at 200×50, so every step changes the frame. Within 1 s the random disc visibly breaks into aligned streams, and by ≈ 5 s it is one flock (BOID-A3). The flock swings around the wandering roost with a period of ≈ 9 s; at 200×50 it stretches into sheets and folds with dense edge ridges rather than a round ball (BOID-A7). Stoops begin at t ≡ 7 s (mod 14). The wave crosses the flock in under 1 s, and the flock re-closes within ≈ 2 s. The scene never resets, and its state is bounded.
- **Resize**: positions and the falcon are scaled by `(W'/W, H'/H)` and velocities by `σ'/σ`. When N shrinks, the first N' birds are kept. When it grows, new birds are cloned from bird `j mod N` with the same velocity and a `gaussian2` jitter of `r_s·σ'` truncated at `2.5·r_s·σ'` (clamped into the box), and they start calm. σ, `κ_i`, the sky height, the small-sky factor ρ and the margin `m`, the hash and `Dots` are rebuilt, and any bird scaled below the new sky's bottom edge is reflected back by the wall. Bird arrays are allocated at capacity 1000 in `build`, so only the hash buckets and `Dots` reallocate. Timeline time is untouched.
- **Inks**: calm birds are `Role::Structure` (grey-blue dusk silhouettes) at level `80 + 105·min(|φ|/60°, 1)`, which spans 80–185. Alarmed birds are `Role::Star` at 200–255, fading with alarm age. Because `Dots` keeps the brightest dot per cell, the wave always wins over calm neighbours. The falcon is `Role::Hot` 240, the only warm object, with a 0.3 s dot streak in `Role::Hot` 90. Label `Role::Text` 110.
- **ASCII**: each bird is a heading-aware stroke on `Dots`. A level bird lights 2 dots: the head at x and the tail at `x − 1.5σ v̂`. A bird banked past 35° adds its two wing dots at ±1 dot perpendicular to the heading, for 4 dots. Dots that round into the label row are not drawn, so a tail or wing never reaches it. Alarmed birds are banked, so the wave is literally denser, which matches Hemelrijk's orientational bands. The `Dots` fallback (1 `.`, 2 `:`, 3–4 `+`, ≥ 5 `#`) turns the flock into a `.`/`:` haze with `+`/`#` ridges (about a quarter of the lit cells at 200×50) and a `+`/`#` band where the wave passes. The falcon is an 8-way heading glyph `→ ↘ ↓ ↙ ← ↖ ↑ ↗` chosen by `round(atan2(vy, vx)/(π/4))`, with ASCII `> \ v / < \ ^ /`. It is drawn with `Canvas::put` over the blit, in the sky's last row at most.
- **Label**: `N 192  k=7` (ASCII, preformatted in `resize`) right-aligned on the last row (GEN-R13), drawn only when `w ≥ 32` and `h ≥ 10` and it fits right of the title, with a blank cell either side.
- **Perf**: at 200×50 (N = 1000, hash of 134×67 buckets of 3 dots), `update(1/60)` is one substep, dominated by the hinted kNN; with the padded hint no bird falls back to the full horizon. Rasterising ≤ 4000 dots into `Dots` happens inside `update`, and `render` only blits. Criterion on a loaded machine (load average ≈ 30–45, where molecular_dynamics measured 0.73 ms against its 0.55 ms quiet figure): 0.40–0.64 ms at 200×50 and 0.40–0.61 ms at 400×100 (0.40 ms at load ≈ 18; 0.68 ms at load ≈ 80–99 after the margin and ρ were cached in `resize`, with 0.37 ms measured at M10 integration under load ≈ 6–25), against the < 1 ms aim. At dt = 0.25 it takes 15 substeps, but GEN-R8 is measured at dt = 1/60. Steady state is allocation-free.
- **Acceptance**: `BOID-A1` for seeded clouds (N ∈ {1, 7, 8, 500}, including coincident points and points outside the box), the spatial hash's `k_nearest(i, 7, R)` returns exactly the brute-force set of ≤ 7 nearest within R, excluding i, sorted by (d², index), for any hint radius; and for a seeded 1000-bird cloud, the hint disc `reach` built from each bird's exact 7-neighbour list holds all 7 (no hinted query falls back to R); `BOID-A2` `N`/`σ` follow the formulas (80×24 ⇒ 192/1.0, 200×50 ⇒ 1000/1.0, 400×100 ⇒ 1000/2.0, 1×1 ⇒ 24/0.5), N is constant across updates, a resize from 200×50 to 80×24 keeps the first 192 birds with positions scaled by (W'/W, H'/H), and a resize from 80×24 to 200×50 yields 1000 birds with every clone within `3·r_s·σ'` of its scaled source; `BOID-A3` order emerges: in the seeded 100×30 scene (N = 300; seeds 0, 1 and 2) with the falcon disabled, global polarisation `Φ = |Σ v̂|/N` is < 0.2 at t = 0, and over 1 s samples in t ∈ [8, 20] s the median local alignment `⟨v̂_i · (1/k)Σ_{j∈N_i} v̂_j⟩` (the mean of the unit headings, not renormalised) is ≥ 0.9 and the median order parameter `max(Φ, M)` is ≥ 0.75, where `M = |Σ r̂_i × v̂_i|/N` about the centroid is the milling parameter, since a flock circling its roost is ordered although its Φ is low (wild flocks reach Φ ≈ 0.96, Cavagna et al. 2010); the global bound depends on the seed (a flock bent part-way into a mill, Φ ≈ 0.5 and M ≈ 0.6, misses it on about 2 of seeds 0–11), while median local alignment is ≥ 0.95 on all of them (~3 s in debug); `BOID-A4` over 20 s at 80×24 with attacks (seed 42), every bird is finite and inside the sky `[0, W] × [0, H_s]` after every substep, speed stays in `[0.5, 2]·v₀σ`, the specular wall acts on < 2 % of bird-substeps after t = 5 s, at every 1 s sample after 5 s the median distance to each bird's true nearest neighbour (brute force, at the current positions) is in `[0.35, 1.0]·r_s` (1.4–4 dots ≈ 0.4–1.2 m; Ballerini et al. 2008 report ≈ 0.7–1.5 m), at ≥ 90 % of the 0.5 s samples after 5 s the flock's 2–98 % extent stays at least one cell (2 dots across, 4 down) inside the sky, and the falcon is within 1 dot of an edge of the sky in < 3 % of substeps (~1.2 s in debug); and in a small sky (40×12 and 32×10, seeds 0–2, 60 s each at dt = 1/30, 0.5 s samples) σ is 0.7, the bottom row shows exactly the label with a blank cell either side, and the mean share of birds within 4 dots of a wall of the sky is ≤ 7 % at 40×12 and ≤ 16 % at 32×10 (with ρ forced to 1: ≈ 12 % and ≈ 21 %) (~2 s in debug); `BOID-A5` the alarm is an excitable wave: in the pure `alarm_step` on a chain graph (bird j's only neighbour is j − 1, T_a = 21 substeps), firing bird 0 at step 0 fires bird n at exactly step `n·L` for n ≤ 10, the sign propagates unchanged, and re-exposure within the same attack never refires a bird; in the seeded 100×30 scene, ≥ 80 % of birds have fired within 1.0 s of the first stoop's first alarm and ≤ 20 % of those fired directly, and the front outruns any bird: the least-squares slope of each firing's distance from where the first alarm fired against its time since then exceeds `V_MAX·v₀σ = 2 v₀σ` (≈ 132 dots/s ≈ 4.4 v₀ for seed 21); `BOID-A6` in the seeded 100×30 scene the falcon's beats are patrol [0, 7) / stoop [7, 9.5) / pursue [9.5, 11.5) / climb [11.5, 14) s and repeat at 14 s, every recorded fire step over 30 s lies inside a stoop or pursue window, and in each of the first two attacks the falcon's minimum distance to the flock centroid during stoop plus pursue is < 0.25 of that distance at stoop start; `BOID-A7` a large flock is not a round ball: `κ_i(N)` equals κ for N ≤ 300, never increases with N and equals 0.03 s⁻² at N = 1000; and in the seeded 200×50 scene (seed 42, with attacks), over 0.5 s samples in t ∈ [8, 40] s, the median principal-axis ratio `√(λ_max/λ_min)` of the positions' covariance is ≥ 1.5 and `+`/`#` make up ≤ 40 % of the lit cells of the ASCII frames (a per-bird trap of κ on every bird gives ≈ 55 %).

### dna_helix  (Molecules of Life, M10)
- **Intent**: the double helix of Watson and Crick's 1953 figure: two backbones winding right-handed around a ladder of coloured base pairs and turning in space. Then a replication fork runs through it. A helicase ring unzips the ladder, and each separated strand is copied into a daughter helix that is half old (grey) and half new (magenta): the leading strand continuously, the lagging strand in Okazaki fragments.
- **Model**: B-DNA in Å, with world +y up (CORE-R45 maps it to screen up) and the axis along +x.
  - **Helix geometry**:
    - Twist is `Ω = 2π/10.5` per bp, i.e. 34.29° (Wang 1979), with a rise of 3.4 Å, so the pitch is 35.7 Å.
    - The phosphorus backbone radius is `R = 8.9 Å` (Arnott & Hukins 1972).
    - The Watson angle is `θ_W(x, t) = φ(t) + 2πx/35.7 Å`. Crick is `θ_C = θ_W + Δ`, with `Δ = 2.2 rad` from C1′–C1′ = 10.5 Å at r = 5.9 Å (Saenger 1984). That gives axial groove widths of 12.5 Å (minor) and 23.2 Å (major).
    - A backbone point is `(x, y₀ + R cos θ, R sin θ)` about its duplex axis at height `y₀`. θ increases with x, so the helix is right-handed. Watson runs 5′→3′ along +x.
  - **Layout**: base pair `i` sits at `xᵢ = x_L + (i − 4.5)·3.4 Å`, where `x_L` is the world x at column 0, so column 0 falls midway between bp 4 and bp 5. Four pairs lie beyond each screen edge, so the camera sway never exposes a blunt end.
  - **Rungs**: a chord at `xᵢ` between the two backbones (the phosphorus positions), split at its midpoint so each half shows that strand's base. Rungs are drawn only every `stride`-th pair, so at least 2 columns separate them, and a rung seen end-on at a backbone crossing (a slanted chord under 2 rows, over 4 rows, or flatter than 1 column per 2 rows) is culled rather than drawn as a diagonal along a row. *Simplifications*: joining phosphorus positions puts each chord ≈ 4 Å off the axis on the minor-groove side (real B-DNA base pairs sit on it), and the helicase is a symbolic collar, not to scale.
  - **Sequence**: `N_MAX = 512` Watson bases in a stack array (enough to span the width at any aspect ratio up to w/h ≈ 50; beyond that the height fit wins and the duplex ends short of the right edge). The GC count is a seeded uniform integer in [⌈0.35·N_MAX⌉, ⌊0.65·N_MAX⌋] (each G or C at random), placed by a Fisher–Yates shuffle from `ctx.rng`. Crick is the complement.
  - **Spin**: `φ(t) = φ₀ − ω·t` on loop-local time, which wraps at the `reset` midpoint (where the fade is 0), so the apex angle is identical in every loop, with `ω = 2π·v_f/10.5 = 1.7952 rad/s`. That is the unwinding a fork at `v_f = 3 bp/s` needs, so the apex angle stays fixed. The unreplicated duplex spins as if its far end were free; in vivo it cannot rotate, and topoisomerases instead relax the positive supercoils that build ahead of the fork.
  - **Fork**: the apex is at `f(τ) = v_f·τ` bp, where τ is the time since `unwind` began. It keeps advancing through `hold` and is frozen during `reset`. There is no fork during `helix`. `v_f` is ≈ 6–17× slower than human forks (1–3 kb/min) and ≈ 300× slower than *E. coli* (≈ 1 kb/s).
  - **Arms**: behind the fork (`s = f − i > 0`) the parental strands separate into two arms, displaced by `±Y(s) = ±1.7R·smoothstep(s/6)` in world y.
    - The upper arm (+y) is the Watson template, which is the lagging-strand template. A DnaB-style helicase ring encircles it at the apex, translocating 5′→3′.
    - The lower arm is the Crick template (the leading strand).
    - Each new strand takes its partner's angular slot (Watson + Δ, or Crick − Δ), so each daughter is a B duplex like the parent. Keeping B geometry in the arms is artistic licence; real single-stranded DNA at the fork is unwound.
  - **Synthesis** is 5′→3′ only:
    - Leading: new base `j` is made at `f(τ) − 2 = j`, i.e. `τ = (j + 2)/3` (the polymerase trails the helicase by 2 bp).
    - Lagging: Okazaki fragments of `L_ok = 8` bp (scaled down from 100–200 nt in eukaryotes and 1–2 kb in bacteria) cover `[8k, 8k + 8)`. Fragment `k` is primed at its high-x end when `f ≥ 8k + 9` (`τ_k = (8k + 9)/3`) and extended toward decreasing x at `v_pol = 8 bp/s`, so base `j` is made at `τ_k + (8k + 7 − j)/8`. The fragment is complete 7/8 s after priming, and ligase seals the nick 0.5 s after completion.
    - Template bases that are not yet paired show as half-rungs.
  - Every quantity is a closed-form function of scene time; nothing is integrated.
- **Motion**: a `Timeline` of `helix` 4 s → `unwind` 14 s → `hold` 4 s → `reset` 2 s, looping every 24 s.
  - **Spin**: from the first frame the spin reads as a barber-pole drift along +x at 10.2 Å/s. That equals the fork speed, so the fork travels with the pattern. It is ≈ 7 columns/s at 80×24 (≈ 11 while zoomed) and ≈ 16 at 200×50.
  - **Camera**:
    - Yaw is `0.2·sin(2πt/19 s)`, capped at `asin(13 Å / visible half-width)` so the edges swing ≤ 13 Å in depth; pitch is 0.18 rad, and distance is `max(40R, 6·visible half-width)` (gentle perspective).
    - A zoom `z(t)` is 2.2 during `helix`. It eases to 1 over the first 3 s of `unwind` (`in_out_cubic`) and stays 1 until the `reset` midpoint, where it steps back to 2.2 while every level is 0.
    - The zoom is anchored at the left edge, so column 0 stays between bp 4 and bp 5, and the dolly-out opens room for the arms.
  - The fork enters at τ ≈ 1.3 s and leaves the screen at τ ≈ 12.4 s at 80×24 (≈ 14.1 s at 200×50). Every visible fragment is sealed before `reset` at both sizes.
  - Fresh nucleotides are drawn at level 255 and fade linearly to their normal level over 0.4 s.
  - `reset` fades the daughters out (1 s). At its midpoint, while the level is zero, it draws a new sequence, and it then fades the parental helix back in (1 s).
- **Resize**: view only; there is no lattice (ADR-0012).
  - The zoom-1 scale is `min(max(h − 2, 1)/(6.6·R), w/(2·35.7))` rows/Å, floored so N_MAX pairs still span the width at extreme aspect ratios. The 6.6 covers the open fork's ±2.7R plus pitch, sway and perspective, as DNA-A4 checks. The width term keeps at least one turn visible on narrow, tall terminals.
  - The pair count is `n = min(⌈w/cols_per_bp + 0.5⌉ + 8, 512)`: ≈ 42 at 80×24 (2.4 columns/bp, R ≈ 3.1 rows) and ≈ 47 at 200×50 (5.2 columns/bp, R ≈ 6.9 rows).
  - The two `Dots` layers are resized.
  - Scene time and the sequence are kept, and column 0 stays between bp 4 and bp 5.
  - Small views: the fork runs only when the zoom-1 backbone radius `R·scale` is ≥ 1.75 rows (h ≳ 14; 50×14 has it, 40×12 does not). Below that the arms, daughters and helicase share too few rows and knot into braille, so the view shows the intact helix at zoom 2.2 all cycle: no fork, arms or helicase, while the `reset` fade and the new sequence at its midpoint still run. The `Story` itself is unchanged; only what the view shows is.
  - Large views: from `R·scale` ≥ 4.5 rows (h ≳ 32, e.g. 120×36 and 200×50) near-layer backbone lines get a twin one dot along the screen normal, so the near strands are 2 dots wide. A 1-dot thread would otherwise be outweighed by the full-cell `│` rungs, and the near/far depth cue needs the area. Far strands stay 1 dot, so they recede.
- **Inks**:
  - Backbones: parental `Structure` and new `Accent`, so semiconservative replication (Meselson & Stahl 1958) shows as two colours.
  - Bases follow the four-dye sequencing convention: A `Particle` (green), T `Hot` (red), G `Sun` (yellow), C `Cold` (blue).
  - Helicase `Wave`.
  - The backbone depth cue is `level = 70 + 160·(z_local + R)/(2R)`, where `z_local` is the camera-space depth of the point minus that of its duplex axis at the same x. Rungs are drawn at `max(0.9 × backbone level, 110)`.
  - Every level is multiplied by the `reset` fade.
  - Label `Text`, with the bases in their base inks.
- **ASCII**: backbones are two `Dots` layers (far `z_local < 0`, near `≥ 0`), which fall back to `. : + #` by dot count. Rungs are `|`, or `/` and `\` when the sway slants them. The helicase is a 0.35R braille collar split across the far and near layers, marked in ASCII by two `o` cells at its top and bottom.
  - Draw order: far dots → rungs (painter-sorted by depth) → helicase → near dots → label.
  - At 80×24 the frame shows dotted strands braiding across the screen over a comb of `|` rungs; in the fork phase they split into a sideways Y.
- **Label**: top-left (row 0, col 1), e.g. `5'-GATTACAGCCTAGGTC-3'`, in ASCII. It shows 16 Watson bases when the width is ≥ 60, or 8 when it is ≥ 30.
  - The window starts at the first on-screen pair (bp 5), 5′ first, and is fixed for the cycle: it changes only at the `reset` midpoint, with the new sequence.
  - It is dropped below 30 columns or 6 rows and is never clipped.
- **Perf**: at 200×50 the backbones are sampled about one dot of arc apart: ≈ 800 samples per strand × 4 strands in the fork ≈ 3.2k projections and plots ≈ 70 µs, plus a twin line for each near segment on bold views (≈ 1.6k more lines, no extra projections).
  - Rungs: up to ≈ 94 (two daughters) × ≈ 12 cells, plus a sort of the visible rungs in a `Vec` reserved in `resize` (allocation-free after it).
  - Two layer blits ≈ 20 µs.
  - The `Dots` are rasterised at the end of `update` and `resize`, because `render` takes `&self`.
  - Total ≈ 0.2 ms per frame at 30 FPS, and ≈ 1.5 ms per frame in the 15-step low-power worst case. There is no heap use after resize.
- **Acceptance**: `DNA-A1` B-DNA geometry: the twist is `2π/10.5` per bp (34.2857°), the rise 3.4 Å and the pitch 35.7 Å; with `R = 8.9 Å` and `Δ = 2.2 rad` the axial groove widths are 12.50 ± 0.05 Å (minor) and 23.20 ± 0.05 Å (major), summing to the pitch, and the C1′ chord `2·5.9·sin(Δ/2)` is 10.5 ± 0.1 Å; both strands are right-handed, i.e. the triple product `(a × b)·c` of any three successive world chord vectors at 0.5 bp steps is > 0; `DNA-A2` rendering is not mirrored: through the scene's own projection at `yaw = 0`, `pitch = 0.18`, zoom 1, every parental backbone sample with `z_local ≥ 0.25R` lies on a segment with screen slope `dy/dx > 0` (a `\` slant with y down, as a right-handed helix along +x requires), world +y projects to a smaller row, and at equal x a near sample's level exceeds a far sample's; at 200×50 and 120×36 the near braille layer lights ≥ 1.5× as many dots as the far layer (bold near strands), and at 80×24 and 40×12 ≤ 1.3× (both 1 dot wide); `DNA-A3` Watson–Crick pairing: sequences use only A, C, G, T; `comp` is an involution with A↔T and G↔C; `Crick[i] = comp(Watson[i])`, so the duplex has #A = #T and #G = #C (Chargaff); for 64 seeds, every drawn sequence's realised GC fraction over all N_MAX = 512 bp lies in [0.35, 0.65]; once synthesis at bp `i` is complete, both daughter pairs at `i` equal the parental pair `(Watson[i], Crick[i])`, and each daughter holds exactly one parental strand (semiconservative); `DNA-A4` fork kinematics: `f(τ) = 3τ`, and the spin `ω = 2π·3/10.5 = 1.79520 rad/s` keeps the apex backbone angle constant through `unwind` and `hold` (`|Δθ| < 1e-9` rad); no pair with `i ≥ f(τ)` is ever separated; `Y(s)` is 0 for `s ≤ 0`, non-decreasing, and equal to `1.7R` for `s ≥ 6`; at 200 sampled times over a cycle, at 80×24 and 200×50, every projected backbone sample of every visible strand lies within rows `[1, h)`, and bp 0 projects left of column 0; views with `R·scale` < 1.75 rows (40×12, 40×13, 200×10) show no fork at any time (no τ, zoom 2.2, the story's fade and draws kept, no half-rungs or helicase in mid-`unwind`), while 50×14 and 80×24 show the story unchanged; `DNA-A5` synthesis is 5′→3′ only: leading base `j` is made at `τ = (j + 2)/3`, so leading synthesis times increase strictly with `j` and no leading base is within 2 bp of the apex; lagging bases come in 8 bp fragments, each primed only once `f ≥ 8k + 9`, with synthesis times strictly decreasing in `j` within a fragment; every fragment is complete before its successor is primed, and each nick is sealed exactly 0.5 s after completion; at 80×24 and 200×50 every visible base of both daughters is synthesised and every visible nick sealed before `reset` begins; `DNA-A6` the story is a pure function of time: driving the pure `Story` state (fork index, synthesised bases with their ages, sealed nicks, phase, zoom, fade) with 1/60 s steps and with a seeded irregular sequence of `dt ∈ [0, 0.25]` to the same time gives identical state and identical RNG position; the timeline is `helix` 4 → `unwind` 14 → `hold` 4 → `reset` 2 s and loops at 24 s; exactly one new sequence is drawn per cycle, at the `reset` midpoint, and for seed 42 consecutive cycles' sequences differ; the zoom changes discontinuously only at an instant where the fade level is 0.

### electron_orbitals  (States of Matter, M10)
- **Intent**: the hydrogen atom as its quantum states actually look. A Monte-Carlo cloud of electron positions is drawn from |ψ_nlm|² and turns slowly in 3-D. Lobes are coloured by the sign of the wavefunction, and the nucleus glints through the nodes. Between states the atom passes through a real superposition, and the charge cloud sloshes from lobe to lobe in a calm breath: the true Bohr beat, slowed by a factor the label states. That oscillating dipole is how the atom emits or absorbs Lyman-α, Hα or Paschen-α light. The cloud is centred. The r₉₀ surface of 4f spans 0.75 of the height, and smaller n is drawn smaller but never as a dot. Point density is matched to each orbital's on-screen size, so 1s is a speckled ball, not a solid disc, and 4f's lobes are not sparse. The eye reads lobe shape first, then the colour of the sign, then the corner label. Nothing else is on screen.
- **Model**: atomic units, a₀ = 1. `ψ = R_nl(r)·Y(r̂)` with `R_nl = √((2/n)³(n−l−1)!/(2n(n+l)!)) e^{−ρ/2} ρ^l L^{(2l+1)}_{n−l−1}(ρ)`, ρ = 2r/n (generalised Laguerre by its three-term recurrence). Real harmonics on the unit direction (x, y, z): `s = 1/√(4π)`, `p_z = √(3/4π) z`, `p_x = √(3/4π) x`, `d_z² = √(5/16π)(3z² − 1)`, `d_xz = √(15/4π) xz`, `d_xy = √(15/4π) xy`, `f_z³ = √(7/16π)(5z³ − 3z)`, `f_xyz = √(105/4π) xyz`. The gallery is a closed dipole-allowed loop (Δl = ±1): 1s → 2p_z → 3d_z² → 4f_z³ → 3d_xz → 4f_xyz → 3d_xy → 2p_x → 1s. Energies are `E_n = −R_H/n²` with `R_H = R∞hc·μ/mₑ = 13.5983 eV` (reduced mass). Lines are `λ = hc/ΔE` with hc = 1239.842 eV·nm: Lyα 121.57 nm, Hα 656.47 nm, Pα 1875.6 nm (vacuum). Sampling is exact and separable, because every real harmonic here is a product Θ(θ)·Φ(φ): `|ψ|² d³r = [r²R² dr]·[Θ² d cosθ]·[Φ² dφ]`. Each factor is tabulated once as a 1024-knot inverse CDF (r ∈ [0, 1.5·r₉₉.₉], cos θ ∈ [−1, 1], φ ∈ [0, 2π)). One sample is three linear-interpolated lookups driven by `ctx.rng` uniforms. Each point also stores fixed uniforms `u_i` (visibility) and `v_i` (mixture), plus ψ of its own orbital and of its partner (f32 storage). Superposition a → b: `Ψ = c_a ψ_a e^{−iE_a t} + c_b ψ_b e^{−iE_b t}` with `c_a = cos χ`, `c_b = sin χ`, so `|Ψ|² = c_a²ψ_a² + c_b²ψ_b² + 2c_a c_b ψ_a ψ_b cos ωt`. Display sampler (exact intensity, per-orbital density): orbital k uses a prefix of `M_k = clamp(round(0.8·π(4ρ_k)²), 256, 16384)` iid points, where ρ_k is its on-screen r₉₀ radius in rows (View), which gives ≈ 0.4 visible points per dot inside r₉₀. For a → b: `M(χ) = (c_a²/M_a + c_b²/M_b)⁻¹`, `p_a = M(χ)c_a²/M_a` and `p_b = M(χ)c_b²/M_b`, so p_a + p_b = 1. Point i < M_a of slot S_a is proposed iff `v_i < p_a`, and point i < M_b of S_b iff `v_i < p_b`, so proposals have intensity exactly `M(χ)·q` with `q = c_a²ψ_a² + c_b²ψ_b²`. A proposed point is visible iff `u_i < w/2`, where `w = |Ψ|²/q = 1 + 2c_a c_b ψ_a ψ_b cos ωt / q ∈ [0, 2]` (AM–GM), evaluated in f64 (q ≤ 0 or non-finite ⇒ hidden). The visible points therefore have expected density exactly `(M(χ)/2)·|Ψ(t)|²` at every instant, and their count glides from M_a/2 to M_b/2. Display fade: on screen a point is proposed iff `v_i < p_k·τ(r)`, where τ is 1 inside `r_in` and falls linearly to 0 at `r_out`. The window is (r₈₈, r₉₅) of the states on screen (the wider (r₉₀, r₉₇) left lone single-dot braille cells a few columns out from the lobes, which read as grain rather than cloud), blended geometrically (`exp(lerp(ln r_a, ln r_b, c_b²))` componentwise, exactly a's at c_b² = 0 and b's at 1) and shared by both slots, so the displayed density is exactly `τ(r)·(M(χ)/2)|Ψ|²`, and the far dust ends inside the frame. Given a proposal, `v_i/p_k` is a fixed uniform independent of `u_i`, so the fade is shimmer-free. Because u and v are fixed per point, the cloud changes only where thresholds are crossed: no shimmer, no pops. At the endpoints c² (and therefore p) is set to exactly 0 or 1. The centroid follows `⟨r⟩(t) = sin 2χ·d_ab cos ωt` with `d_ab = ⟨a|r|b⟩`: 0.7449, 2.452, 5.188, 2.995, 3.867, 3.867, 2.123 and 0.7449 a₀ around the loop. Time is slowed, and the label says by how much: the beat is the true Bohr frequency ΔE/h (h = 4.135667696×10⁻¹⁵ eV·s; Lyα 2.466 PHz, Hα 456.7 THz, Pα 159.8 THz) divided by a round per-line factor S = 4×10¹⁵ / 10¹⁵ / 5×10¹⁴, so `f_disp = ΔE/(h·S)` = 0.62 / 0.46 / 0.32 Hz for Lyα / Hα / Pα: a calm breath with 1.8 / 1.4 / 1.0 cycles per 3 s beat, still ordered by ΔE as the physics is. (The previous `2.0 Hz·√(ΔE/ΔE_Lyα)` compression made 1s–2p_z flip every 0.25 s, a vibrating blob 2–4× faster than anything else in the scene.) ω = 2π f_disp with `t` counted from the rise start; a pair that is not a gallery α line would have no line, no factor and no beat. Timeline per orbital: `hold` 6 s (pure; this is χ = π/2 of the (prev, a) pair and χ = 0 of the (a, next) pair, which give the same visible set), `rise` 2 s (χ 0 → π/4, smoothstep), `beat` 3 s (χ = π/4, full sloshing), `fall` 2 s (χ π/4 → π/2). That is 13 s per orbital and a 104 s loop, starting at a seeded orbital's hold. Two-slot ring: during a's hold the free slot is filled with S_next, and S_a's partner ψ is rewritten to ψ_next (≤ 256 points per 1/60 s of dt; any remainder completes at the rise boundary). View: orbital (x, y, z) maps to world (x, z, −y), so orbital z is screen-up. One world unit is the orbital's r₉₀ times `g_k = (r₉₀,k/r₉₀,4f)^{0.3}` (r₉₀ = 2.66 / 7.99 / 15.80 / 25.99 a₀ for 1s / 2p / 3d / 4f), so `ρ_k = R·g_k` with `R = fit_radius(stage, 0.75)`, which is 0.50 R, 0.70 R, 0.86 R and R. The stage is every row above the label (all rows when the label is hidden), and the nucleus sits in its middle cell with the projected origin at that cell's centre, so a nodal plane through the nucleus empties one whole row. During transitions ln r₉₀ and ln g are lerped with the weight `glide(c_b²)`, so the larger state of the pair sets the scale while it holds at least half the weight: `1 − (1 − 2w)₊²` into a larger state, `(2w − 1)₊²` out of one. A plain c_b² would draw the larger state bigger than in its own hold (× 1.47 for 1s → 2p_z at w = ½) and push its dust off the frame. Camera: `geom::Camera` with distance 6 and a per-state pose (α_k yaw offset, p_k pitch, rock amplitude, phase φ_k), lerped component-wise with weight c_b²: 1s, 2p_z, 3d_z² and 4f_z³ (0, 0.15, 0.6, 0); 2p_x and 3d_xz (0, 0.15, 0.25, π/2); 4f_xyz (π/4, 0, 0.8, 0); 3d_xy (0, 1.05, 0.6, 0). On the Timeline clock t, with θ = 2π(t − 3 s)/13 s + φ_k, `yaw = α_k + rock·cos θ` and `pitch = p_k − 0.10·sin θ`. One rock period is one orbital's 13 s visit, so the 104 s loop is exactly periodic and every hold sees the same part of the rock: the rock peaks at mid-hold (3 s in), and states with φ_k = π/2 pass through broadside there. The pitch wobble is in quadrature, so the camera always turns at ≥ 0.10·2π/13 s ≈ 2.8°/s and never stalls. Pose rationale: m = 0 states sit low, so their nodal planes stay open (at pitch 0.32 the 2p_z gap fills in). 2p_x and 3d_xz have a vertical nodal plane that is edge-on at yaw 0 and fills in when turned further (at ±0.6 the 2p_x node column holds up to 0.86 of the lobe column's dots, ≤ 0.26 at ±0.25). 4f_xyz is turned 45° and kept level, so its z = 0 nodal plane stays an open gap through the nucleus between two tiers of lobes; at mid-hold the rock turns it to yaw π/4 + 0.8 ≈ π/2, where its vertical nodal planes are edge-on and the lobes stand as four `+#` blocks (±0.6 never separates them). 3d_xy lies in the horizontal plane and is seen from above. The camera only rotates and scales the whole atom, so none of this touches the physics. References: Bethe & Salpeter (1957) for ⟨r⟩, ⟨r²⟩ and d(1s–2p).
- **Motion**: the camera rocks about each state's pose, never slower than 2.8°/s and at up to 0.6·2π/13 s ≈ 17°/s of yaw (0.8 rad for 4f_xyz: ≈ 22°/s), with the pitch nodding in quadrature. Even a 1s or m = 0 state, whose shape is invariant under yaw, visibly turns because the finite sample rotates, so the frame changes within the first second (GEN-R9). Otherwise a stationary state stays still, as it does in nature; there is no resampling shimmer. Every 13 s there is a transition. The second lobe grows in, the cloud breathes between lobes at f_disp (1s–2p_z swells up and down once every 1.6 s, the 3d–4f pairs roll over once in about 3 s), and then the new orbital settles and the framing eases to its size. Every 15 s visit sees at least one transition.
- **Resize**: samples live in atomic units and don't depend on the terminal size. They are drawn in the first resize (current and next slot) and then during holds. A resize rescales the view, resizes `Dots` and recomputes every M_k. When an M_k grows past the sampled count, the resize tops up that live slot (allocation is allowed there). Timeline, samples and uniforms are kept. Zero-area sizes make plotting a no-op. ADR-0012 needs no lattice.
- **Inks**: the cloud uses `Role::Div`. The sign is that of the dominant component at the point, `sign(c_kψ_k)` with `k = argmax_k |c_kψ_k|`; in holds this is exactly the orbital's phase sign, and it never blinks at ω. The level is `ink::div(±(0.44 + 0.56·near))`, i.e. `128 ± (56 + 71·near)`, so both signs stay clear of the dark zero and nearer dots are more saturated: cyan negative, orange positive. Each cell keeps its most intense dot by `|level − 128|` (CORE-R41), so a negative-lobe cell shows its nearest dot. The nucleus is `Accent` 180 (`Glyph::new('•', 'o')`), drawn before the cloud so it shows only through nodes (l ≥ 1). While a state with l ≥ 1 is on screen it is drawn again over the cloud when at most 2 of its cell's 8 dots are lit, since a lobe dot or two from the tilt would otherwise hide it; the 1s hold keeps it covered. Row h−1 takes no dots while the label shows. The label is `Text` 150.
- **ASCII**: `Dots` fallback by dot count (`.`, `:`, `+`, `#`). 1s is a speckled round ball, `+#` at the core fading through `:` to `.`. 2p_z is two stacked `+#` lobes with a sparse `.` equatorial band and the `o` nucleus between them. 3d_z² is two lobes plus a `.:` equatorial ring. 4f_z³ is two polar lobes and two conical `+` rings. 4f_xyz shows its eight cube-diagonal lobes as two tiers split by an open gap through the `o` nucleus, and at mid-hold as four `+#` blocks split by a cross. Labels use their ASCII variants.
- **Label**: right-aligned on row h−1 (GEN-R13), static strings only (GEN-R11). Hold: `3d_z²` (ASCII `3d_z2`). Transition: `2p_z → 3d_z²  Hα 656.5 nm  slowed 10¹⁵` (ASCII `2p_z -> 3d_z2  H-a 656.5 nm  slowed 1e15`), and likewise `Lyα 121.6 nm  slowed 4×10¹⁵` / `Ly-a … slowed 4e15` and `Pα 1876 nm  slowed 5×10¹⁴` / `P-a … slowed 5e14`: the one liberty the scene takes with time is stated on screen. Parts are dropped, never clipped, to fit `title::readout_room`: first the slow-down part, then the line part, then the arrow part (leaving the current state's name), and the whole label when `w < 14` or `h < 8`.
- **Perf**: estimated at 200×50 with M_k ≤ 16384. The v-test runs over ≤ 2 × 16384 f32 SoA points, weights are computed for about half of them, and ≤ 8 k visible points are projected and plotted: ≈ 0.15 ms. The camera rotation is hoisted out of the loop (`Camera::projector`). `Dots` clear and blit of 10 k cells ≈ 0.05 ms. Slot preparation of ≤ 256 samples plus ψ pairs per 1/60 s ≈ 0.08 ms. Total ≈ 0.3 ms (aim < 1 ms; measured 0.16–0.31 ms at 200×50 on a loaded machine). The worst frame is a resize late in a hold, which forces synchronous completion of a 16384-point slot (≈ 2 ms in release, still < 5 ms). Memory: two SoA slots of 7 × 16384 f32 (≈ 0.9 MB) and 192 KB of tables (8 samplers × 3 `InverseCdf<1024>`).
- **Acceptance**: `ORB-A1` every gallery orbital satisfies ∫R²r² dr = 1 (trapezoid, h = 0.01, r ≤ 90; 1e-6) and ∫Y² dΩ = 1 (48-point Gauss–Legendre × 96 φ, 1e-9), and distinct gallery orbitals are orthogonal (|⟨i|j⟩| < 1e-9); `ORB-A2` quadrature gives ⟨r⟩ = (3n² − l(l+1))/2 (1.5 / 5 / 10.5 / 18 a₀) and ⟨r²⟩ = n²(5n² + 1 − 3l(l+1))/2 (1e-6 relative), and 20 000 sampler draws per orbital give a mean r within 4σ of ⟨r⟩ and ⟨cos²θ⟩ within 4σ of 3/5 (2p_z) and 11/21 (3d_z²); `ORB-A3` ⟨1s|z|2p_z⟩ = 2^{15/2}/3⁵ = 0.74494 a₀ (1e-4), every consecutive gallery pair including the wrap has |⟨a|r|b⟩| equal to the Model's d_ab table (1e-3) and > 0.5 a₀, and the line wavelengths are 121.57, 656.47 and 1875.6 nm within 0.1 % and agree with the label strings as rounded, and every consecutive gallery pair (wrap included) is labelled with its own line (a pair that is not an α line shows no line part and has no beat rather than a wrong one); ΔE_Lyα/h = 2.466 PHz (0.1 %), and each line's beat is exactly ΔE/(h·S) with S the factor its label prints (ASCII `4e15` parses to S, Unicode `4×10¹⁵` / `10¹⁵` matches it): 0.6165 / 0.4567 / 0.3197 Hz (1e-3), each ≤ 0.7 Hz, decreasing with ΔE, and ≥ 0.9 cycles per 3 s beat; `ORB-A4` exact superposition: for 1s–2p_z at χ = π/4 with M_a = M_b = 40 000, the visible count is 20 000 within 4σ and the visible centroid z̄ equals 0.7449·cos ωt within 4 SE at cos ωt ∈ {1, 0, −1}, with x̄ and ȳ zero within 4 SE; with M_a = 10 000 and M_b = 40 000 the visible count is M(χ)/2 = 8 000 within 4σ; the display fade is exact: for 1s–2p_z at χ = π/4, cos ωt = 1 and M_a = M_b = 40 000 with the c_b² = ½ window, the displayed count matches `(M/2)∫τ|Ψ|²` = 10 000·(∫τR_1s²r² dr + ∫τR_2p²r² dr) within 4σ and z̄ matches `(∫τR_1sR_2p r³ dr)/√3` over half that bracket within 4 SE (both by quadrature; per-slot windows missed by 23σ and 4.5 SE); after a slot switch the live slot carries its new partner's ψ (at the 2p_z → 3d_z² beat z̄ = ±⟨2p_z|z|3d_z²⟩ = ±2.452 a₀ within 4 SE); `ORB-A5` no pops: the displayed set is identical at the end of each phase and the start of the next (hold→rise and fall→hold, including the slot switch), and over a full 1s → 2p_z transition at 200×50 (dt = 1/60) no step toggles more than 6 % of the sampler's visible count (the expected bound is sin 2χ·(ω dt/2)·∫|ψ_aψ_b| d³r ≤ 1.35 % of M(χ), i.e. 2.7 % of the visible count at Lyα's 0.62 Hz with ∫|ψ_1sψ_2p| = 0.419, plus ≤ 2 % from χ drift; measured 4.7 %), nor more than 7 % of the displayed count, which adds the drift of the fade window as it widens 3× (measured 5.1 %); `ORB-A6` the Timeline visits 1s, 2p_z, 3d_z², 4f_z³, 3d_xz, 4f_xyz, 3d_xy, 2p_x in that order with 6 / 2 / 3 / 2 s phases and a 104 s loop, and the label is pure ASCII in ASCII mode, complete at 80×24 (slow-down included), right-aligned on row h−1 within `title::readout_room` chars: at h = 12 the slow-down part needs w ≥ 48, the line part w ≥ 35, and 3d_xz → 4f_xyz w ≥ 23 (22 in Unicode), and it is absent when `w < 14` or `h < 8`; over one loop at dt = 0.25 no dot lands on the label row (80×24, 40×12, 14×8); `ORB-A7` composition at 80×24 (seed 42; the first three at yaw 0.3, pitch 0.15): in the 1s hold ≤ 5 % of lit cells are full 8-dot cells (measured ≈ 2 %); in the 4f_z³ hold ≥ 16 rows hold ≥ 3 lit cells; in the 2p_z hold the nucleus row (the nodal plane) has ≤ 60 % of the lit cells of the fullest row (measured ≈ 40 %). With each hold's own camera swept over a whole rock cycle: 3d_xy spans ≥ 0.6 of 4f_z³'s rows, and a ring at 0.5ρ_k about the nucleus meets ≥ 4 sign runs for 3d_xy and 3d_xz (clovers); the 2p_x nucleus column holds ≤ 40 % of the fullest column within ±3 (measured ≤ 26 %), and 2p_x is broadside (yaw 0) at mid-hold; the nucleus glyph shows through the 2p_z node in ≥ 80 % of frames; the 4f_xyz nucleus row holds ≤ 50 % of the fullest row's lit cells; its centre band (nucleus column ± 1) empties to ≤ 15 % of the fullest lobe column for ≥ 16 of 52 clocks; and at its mid-hold yaw is π/2 within 0.02 and the nucleus column holds ≤ 60 % of the fullest lobe column (four blocks, not two slabs), with the 104 s loop a whole number of rock cycles. Every pose's camera turns at ≥ 2°/s throughout. No hold leaves more than 1 lit cell per frame beyond 1.25ρ_k (the far dust fades out). In the 2p_z hold every Div cell has |div value| ≥ 0.43, and both signs light > 20 cells. Over one loop at dt = 0.1 (80×24 and 200×50) no dot reaches the outermost stage rows in a hold or beat, and the χ ramps leave ≤ 0.1 cells per frame on them.

### globular_cluster  (Deep Cosmos, M10)
- **Intent**: an ancient globular cluster seen through a slowly turning telescope. A blazing, unresolved core thins into a sparse halo of single stars, with a handful of orange giants glinting among them. Every visible halo star is a live gravitating body: the swarm breathes, and the camera's slow turn gives it depth through parallax. It is calm, dense and three-dimensional, the stellar counterpart to the flat `starfield` and the disk-shaped `galaxy_collision`.
- **Model**:
  - **Units and softening**: direct N-body in Hénon units (`G = M = 1`, virial radius 1, `E ≈ −1/4`), equal masses `m = 1/N`, Plummer softening `Φ_ij = −G m²/√(r² + ε²)` with `ε = 0.05` (0.085 of the Plummer scale radius `a = 3π/16`).
  - **Forces and integration**: an O(N²/2) Newton-3 symmetric pair loop, so `Σ m a = 0` to rounding. `ode::verlet_integrate` (KDK leapfrog, CORE-R44) runs with `h_max = 1/128` N-body units at a rate of `0.4` N-body units per simulated second: one force evaluation per frame at `dt = 1/60`, 13 at `dt = 0.25`. The crossing time `t_cr = 2√2` is 7.1 s.
  - **ICs**: Plummer sphere `ρ ∝ (1 + r²/a²)^{−5/2}`, sampled per Aarseth, Hénon & Wielen (1974) in `G = M = a = 1`:
    - Radius `r = (X₁^{−2/3} − 1)^{−1/2}` with mass cut `X₁ < 0.999` (so `r ≤ 38.7 a`).
    - Isotropic directions (`z = 1 − 2u`, `φ = 2πu`).
    - Speed `v = q·√2(1 + r²)^{−1/4}`, with `q` from `g(q) = q²(1 − q²)^{7/2}` by rejection under the bound 0.1 (the maximum is 0.0923).
    - COM position and velocity subtracted.
    - Positions scaled so the unsoftened `W = −1/2` (then `a ≈ 3π/16` and `r_h ≈ 0.769`).
    - The density centre moved to the origin: 4 passes that each subtract the mean position of the bodies inside the half-mass radius. The heavy tail (`r_rms ≈ 3.0 a` with the 0.999 cut) puts the COM ≈ `r_rms/√N` off the body core (0.08 units at N = 500, 0.22 at N = 64). The view and the glow are centred on the origin, so without this the core would sit off the glow and swing around it as the camera turns. It is a translation, so `W` and the dynamics are unchanged, and the momentum stays zero, so the cluster does not drift.
    - Velocities scaled so `2K = |W_ε|` (virial equilibrium in the softened potential), giving `E ≈ −0.248`.
  - **N**: the largest bucket in `{64, 96, 128, 192, 256, 384, 500}` that is `≤ max(area/5, 64)`. 80×24 gives 384, and 200×50 and anything larger gives 500 (the ADR-0012 cap).
  - **Unresolved light**: each body stands for ~10³ real stars, so 500 bodies cannot fill a braille core (at 200×50 they light only ≈ 1.5 of 8 dots per core cell). The core glow is drawn from the Plummer surface brightness `g(R) = (1 + R²/a_g²)^{−2}`, where `R` is the projected distance from the centre and `a_g = r_h/1.3048`. Here `r_h` is the body system's 3-D half-mass radius about the origin, smoothed with `τ = 4 s` and reset at every reseed, so the glow follows the real core contraction.
  - **Luminosity tags** (visual only): the first `g_n = clamp(N/50, 3, 10)` bodies (sample order is random) are red giants. The dynamics stay equal-mass, so there is no mass segregation.
- **Motion**:
  - Bodies orbit in the self-consistent potential: the core crosses in about 2 s and halo stars drift slowly.
  - `geom::Camera` yaws at `2π/90 s`, with pitch `0.30 + 0.15·sin(2πt/67 s)` and perspective `distance = 10` units, so near-side stars slide against far-side ones. The camera alone moves an `r = 1.5` star ≈ 4 dots/s at 200×50.
  - A looping `Timeline`: `evolve` 120 s (48 N-body units ≈ 17 t_cr). Spitzer's `t_rh ≈ 0.138 N r_h^{3/2}/ln(0.4N)` gives 8.8 units (22 s) at N = 500; softening at ε = 0.05 cuts ln Λ to ≈ ln(r_h/ε) ≈ 2.7, so a cycle spans ≈ 3–5.5 t_rh. Then a 1.5 s `fade`, a fresh Plummer sample from `ctx.rng`, and a 1.5 s `rise`: 123 s per loop.
  - Within a cycle the core contracts slightly and the odd star escapes. Core collapse needs ≈ 15 t_rh and is never reached. The reseed keeps the halo populated and bounds the accumulated energy error.
- **Resize** (ADR-0012 analogue, with N as the cap):
  - Within the same N bucket a resize only changes the view: `scale = draw::fit_radius(size, 0.92)/2.2` rows per unit, so `R = 2.2` units (≈ 93 % of the projected mass) fills the frame, and `Dots` is resized. Pos/vel are untouched.
  - On a bucket change, shrinking keeps the first N′ bodies (an unbiased subsample). Growing appends fresh AHW samples, scaled by the same length and velocity factors as the build sample. Then `m = 1/N′`, the momentum is re-zeroed (the COM position is not re-centred: that would break GC-A6's bit-identity and jump the view; the subsample's small COM offset, ≈ `r_rms/√N′`, persists until the next reseed), and velocities are re-virialised to `2K = |W_ε|` (one O(N²) pass). The cluster never visibly resets while you drag the window.
- **Inks**:
  - Everything except the giants is one `Dots` layer in `Role::Star`.
  - Glow: a dot at projected radius `R` is lit iff `p(R) > u(x, y)`, where `p = clamp(1.7·(g − 0.08)/0.92, 0, 1)` and `u` is a fixed per-dot hash in `[0, 1)` salted once from `ctx.rng`. So the core is solid for `R ≤ 0.518 a_g`, thins through a speckled ring, and ends at `R = 1.59 a_g`. A cell whose centre has `p = 1` is lit whole (all 8 dots), as a saturated detector pixel is, so the core reads as a solid knot. Glow level is `90 + 165·g`.
