  - Bodies: level `(100 + 140·√g(R) + 20·clamp(z/2, −1, 1))·f_eye(z)` (nearer is brighter), clamped to `[0, 255]`. The eye at distance 10 sits inside the far halo (`r ≤ 38.7 a ≈ 23` units), so a near-side halo star would be magnified without bound and streak across the screen like a meteor. `f_eye = 1 − smoothstep((z − 2.5)/(10/3 − 2.5))` therefore fades near-eye stars out between depth `z = 2.5` and `z = 10/3`, where perspective magnifies them 1.33–1.5×.
  - Giants: `Glyph::new('✦', '*')` in `Role::Hot`, drawn over the blit, each twinkling slowly at level `200 + 30·sin(2πt/P_k + φ_k)` with `P_k ∈ [5, 9]` s and `φ_k` drawn from the salt (visual only). Giants projected where the glow probability is `p ≥ 0.5` (`R < 0.83 a_g`) are drawn as ordinary `Star` dots, lost in the core's glare as in a real exposure.
  - The label is `Text` 140.
  - `Seq` was deliberately not used: a false-colour cluster reads as a heatmap, not a starfield.
- **ASCII**: the `Dots` count fallback gives the classic look. At 80×24 there is a `#` core block (≈ 3 × 6 cells), a `+`/`:` ring out to ≈ 5 rows, `.` halo stars, and giants as `*`. The label is pure ASCII.
- **Label**: top-left (row 0, column 1), e.g. `globular cluster  N=500  3 crossings`: the body count and the whole crossing times (`t_cr = 7.1 s`, `1 crossing` in the singular) since the last reseed, so it changes only every 7.1 s. Before the first crossing the clause is left out (`globular cluster  N=500`), so the label never shows a debug-like `0 crossings`. It is written into a stack `StackText` without allocating. The full form is used only when it takes at most half the width (`2·len ≤ w`), so it stays a quiet caption rather than a banner. Otherwise the label is `globular cluster` when that fits in `w − 2`, else nothing. Column 0, the label and one trailing cell of row 0 are kept clear of glow, bodies and giants, so no star reads as part of the text.
- **Perf**: at 200×50 with N = 500 a frame costs ≈ 0.4 ms.
  - Force: 124,750 pairs × (1 sqrt + 1 div + ~20 flops) ≈ 0.25 ms scalar.
  - Projection: 500 × `Camera::project` ≈ 30 µs.
  - Glow: ≈ 12k dots inside `1.59 a_g`, each one hash and compare, ≈ 25 µs.
  - `Dots` clear and blit ≈ 25 µs.
  - `dt = 0.25` gives 13 substeps (≈ 13× the force cost, a few ms). Only the GEN-R4 contract reaches it: the runtime feeds fixed 1/60 steps (ADR-0003), and GEN-R8 covers `update(1/60)`.
  - Pos/vel/acc `Vec<f64>`s have capacity 1500 from `build`, and the cluster's half-mass scratch has capacity 500. The reseed at 121.5 s refills these in place (GC-A6), and the glow needs no buffer, so update and render do not allocate.
  - Debug note: the contract suite's 600-step 200×50 run is ≈ 75 M pair evaluations, ≈ 1.5–2.5 s unoptimised (flagged, like `molecular_dynamics`).
- **Acceptance**: `GC-A1` the AHW sampler (`G = M = a = 1`, cut 0.999): a seeded 4000-star sample has KS distance < 0.03 from the truncated Plummer mass profile `M(r) = r³/(1 + r²)^{3/2}/0.999`, median radius within 4 % of `1.3048 a`, the speed fractions `q = v/v_esc(r)` (`v_esc = √2(1 + r²)^{−1/4}`) all in `[0, 1)` with KS distance < 0.03 from the CDF of `g(q) ∝ q²(1 − q²)^{7/2}` (the regularised incomplete beta `I_{q²}(3/2, 9/2)`, integrated numerically), while exponents 5/2 and 9/2 give > 0.03, and the raw N = 1500 virial ratio `2K/|W|` in `[0.9, 1.1]`; `GC-A2` after `plummer` for seeds 1–6 and N = 64, 256, 500, the density centre (mean position inside the half-mass radius) lies within 0.03 units of the origin and `|Σ m v| < 1e-14`; for N = 256 the unsoftened `W = −1/2` within 1e-12, `2K = |W_ε|` within 1e-12, and `E = −1/4` within 0.01; `GC-A3` for N = 64 seeded, the Newton-3 pair loop equals a naive per-star O(N²) sum within 1e-12 relative, `|Σ m a| < 1e-14`, each acceleration equals `−∇Φ_ε/m` by central differences within 1e-6 relative, and two coincident stars give a finite, zero mutual force; `GC-A4` for N = 128 seeded, `ε = 0.05`, `h = 1/128`, 10 N-body units (1280 steps, ≈ 10 M pair evaluations): `|Σ m v| < 1e-14` throughout and max `|ΔE/E₀| < 5e-4` (measured ≤ 4.6e-5); `GC-A5` in the same run, `r_h(t)/r_h(0) ∈ [0.7, 1.3]` at every 0.5-unit sample with time mean in `[0.9, 1.1]`, and the time-mean `2K/|W_ε|` is in `[0.9, 1.1]`; `GC-A6` N follows the bucket rule (80×24 ⇒ 384, 200×50 ⇒ 500, 1×1 and 0×0 ⇒ 64, never > 500), a resize within a bucket leaves pos/vel bit-identical, and a bucket change keeps the first `min(N, N′)` positions bit-identical while restoring `|Σ m v| < 1e-14` and `2K = |W_ε|` within 1e-12, and the reseed at 200×50 draws a fresh N = 500 sample into pos/vel/acc/scratch without changing their capacities; `GC-A7` at 200×50 (seed 42, t = 0, Unicode): every cell whose centre is within projected `R ≤ a_g/2` shows `⣿` or a giant glyph, no dot beyond `R = 1.6 a_g` is lit except at a body's projected position, the mean ink level of lit cells within `a_g` exceeds that of lit cells beyond `3 a_g` by ≥ 50, and at most `g_n` `Hot` giant glyphs are drawn (`g_n` = 10, 7, 3 for N = 500, 384, 64), none at a giant projected within `R < 0.83 a_g` (at least one such giant exists at this seed); `f_eye` is 1 up to `z = 2.5`, strictly decreasing, and 0 from `z = 10/3`; `GC-A8` `p(R)` is 1 for `R ≤ 0.518 a_g`, strictly decreasing between `0.519 a_g` and `1.59 a_g`, and 0 beyond, and the per-dot hash is a pure function of `(x, y, salt)`; `GC-A9` the label reads `globular cluster  N=384  4 crossings` at age 4.5 `t_cr` (80×24), `1 crossing` at 1.99 `t_cr` and `globular cluster  N=384` before the first crossing; the full form shows down to `w = 2·len` and falls back to `globular cluster` one column below (so at 40 columns) and down to `w = 18`, and the label is empty below; at 30×10, 40×12 and 80×24 (seed 42, t = 0, 5, 12 s) no dot is lit in row 0's cells `0..=len + 1` and no giant lies there; each giant slot's level stays in `[170, 230]`, swings by ≥ 40 over 9 s, with `P_k ∈ [5, 9]` s, and the slots differ.

### ising_model  (States of Matter, M10)
- **Intent**: the ferromagnetic phase transition as a living mosaic. A quench turns static into two colours of domain that swell, round off and straighten. Reheating melts them into a dim boil, and a slow cool through the critical point leaves clusters of every size breathing (critical opalescence). Order lights up: aligned regions glow and disorder sinks toward the background, so the eye goes to the shrinking bubbles and the straightening walls.
- **Model**: 2-D Ising `H = −J Σ⟨ij⟩ sᵢsⱼ`, `sᵢ = ±1`, `J = k_B = 1`, zero field, periodic (torus) boundaries. The lattice is capped: `L = grid::lattice_for(Size(w, 2h), 16 384, 1.25)` in square pixel units, so 200×50 → 178×91 (≈ 1.1 px per spin) and 80×24 → 80×48 (below the cap the lattice is the pixel grid itself). Kinetics are single-spin Glauber heat-bath (Glauber 1963), random-sequential, with one `u64` per attempt from a scene-local SplitMix64 seeded once from `ctx.rng` at the first resize (GEN-R7). Bits 32–47 and 48–63 pick `x = (r₁₆·Lw)>>16` and `y = (r₁₆·Lh)>>16` (≤ 0.3 % non-uniform, harmless because every heat-bath move leaves the Boltzmann distribution invariant). The local field is `h = Σ_nn sⱼ ∈ {−4,−2,0,2,4}`; set `sᵢ = +1` iff the low 32 bits are `< thr[h]`, where `thr = round(p₊·2³²)` as u64 (so `p = 0, 1` are exact) and `p₊(h) = 1/(1 + e^{−2h/T})`. The 5-entry table is rebuilt once per update with T clamped to `[0.05, 100]` (NaN ⇒ T_c). This is the same model-A equilibrium, universality and coarsening class as Metropolis; it replaces checkerboard Metropolis because deterministic sublattice sweeps flip every ΔE = 0 spin at high T (a period-2 strobe) and because random-sequential updates give smooth fractional sweeps per frame at any lattice size. The rate is `max(60·N/16 384, 1)` Monte Carlo sweeps (MCS) per simulated second (N = lattice sites; one MCS per 1/60 s step at the cap), realised as `⌊acc⌋` attempts from an accumulator gaining `rate·N·dt`. Domain size relative to the lattice scales as `√(MCS/N)`, so coarsening looks the same at every terminal size. Temperature is in units of J/k_B with `T_c = 2/ln(1+√2) = 2.269 185 314` (Onsager 1944). References: `m₀(T) = (1 − sinh(2/T)⁻⁴)^{1/8}` below T_c and 0 above (Yang 1952); exact energy per spin `e(T) = −coth 2β·[1 + (2/π)·k′·K(k)]` with `k′ = 2 tanh² 2β − 1` (signed complementary modulus, computed directly to avoid cancellation), `K = π/(2·AGM(1, |k′|))`, and the product `k′·K` taken as its limit 0 when `k′ = 0`, so `e(T_c) = −√2`. T/T_c follows a looping `Timeline` of 58 s: `coarsen` 20 s at 0.60 → `reheat` 8 s, smoothstep 0.60→1.25 → `hot` 5 s at 1.25 (ξ ≈ 2–3, a boil rather than a per-frame fizz) → `cool` 15 s, `1 + 0.25(1−p)²` (eases into T_c so most of the phase sits near criticality) → `critical` 8 s at 1.00 → `quench` 2 s, smoothstep 1.00→0.60; it is continuous at every boundary. The first resize seeds a T = ∞ state (i.i.d. ±1), so t = 0 is the textbook quench. Magnetisation `M = Σs` is tracked incrementally and recomputed on resize.
- **Motion**: within 1 s (≈ 60 MCS at the cap) static condenses into domains 10–20 spins across; coarsening follows `ℓ ∝ t^{1/2}` (Allen & Cahn 1979; Bray 1994). Over `coarsen`, about 1200 MCS grow domains to lattice scale: bubbles shrink and vanish, walls straighten, and the phase often ends in a few domains or one stripe (≈ 1/3 of torus quenches end in stripes). In `reheat`, walls roughen and thermal clusters eat the domains as T crosses T_c. `hot` is a dim boil of small clusters. In `cool`, clusters grow fractal and slow down (critical slowing, z ≈ 2.17; Nightingale & Blöte 1996). `critical` breathes at all scales up to `t^{1/z}`. `quench` feeds straight into `coarsen`, so later cycles coarsen from critical clusters and the first from noise.
- **Resize**: if `lattice_for` returns the same lattice, only the display maps are rebuilt (cell-centre nearest-index tables px→lattice, `Vec<u16>`, allocated in `resize`) and the spins are untouched. A different lattice (bucket change, or any change below the cap) triggers `grid::resample_nearest` into a scratch grid and a swap (ADR-0012); spins stay exactly ±1 and M is recomputed. Above the cap the lattice-to-pixel ratio is not an integer (200×50: 178×91 → 200×100 px), so about every 8th pixel column and 11th pixel row repeats a lattice line; cell-centre sampling spreads the repeats evenly, and the resulting ±1 px wall jitter is accepted (an integer ratio would halve the lattice). `resize` never draws from `ctx.rng` after the first seeding. T comes from scene time, so a resize never desyncs the schedule.
- **Inks**: every pixel is `Role::Div` with `v = s·A[n_al]`, `A = [0.06, 0.12, 0.22, 0.38, 0.72]`, where `n_al ∈ 0..4` counts aligned lattice neighbours; the ten possible inks are built once per scene from A and indexed by `(s > 0)·5 + n_al`, so render does no float work. The table is convex because even at 1.25 T_c most spins still have 3–4 aligned neighbours (⟨sᵢsⱼ⟩ ≈ 0.45), so a linear map could not dim the hot phase: only n_al = 4 is bright. Domain interiors are ±0.72 (astro: orange up, blue down). Walls are ±0.22–0.38, a darker seam that outlines domains without drawing lines. Thermal or isolated spins are ±0.06–0.12, so the hot phase sinks toward the background and ordering visibly brightens the screen. The label is `Role::Text` 200.
- **ASCII**: the `Pixels::blit` closure decodes each pixel's signed value with the exact inverse of `ink::div` (`(level − 128)/128` below 128, `/127` above), takes the cell mean m and applies `draw::ramp((m/0.72 + 1)/2, [. , : + #])` (so interiors reach the ramp ends) with ink `ink::div(m)`. Up interiors are `#`, down interiors `.`, up walls `+`, down walls `,`, and cells split across a wall `:`. The 80×24 snapshots show five-glyph noise at t = 0, blobs about 5 cells wide at t = 1 (≈ 14 MCS) and `#` islands with `+` rims in a `.` sea at t = 5 (≈ 70 MCS).
- **Label**: top-left (col 1, row 0) `T/Tc 0.93  |m| 0.71`. T/T_c comes from the schedule and `|m| = |M|/N` is measured live; after a quench it visibly lags the equilibrium m₀ (for example 0.71 vs 0.86 at 0.93). `put` resets bg, so the label sits on a small dark chip. It is written from a stack buffer; the `  |m| …` part is dropped when w < 24, and the whole label when w < 12 or h < 4.
- **Perf** (200×50): update is 16.2 k heat-bath attempts × ≈ 4–5 ns (one u64 draw, table-driven wrapped neighbours, 4 L1 loads, compare, store) ≈ 75 µs. Render is 20 k pixels × (2 map lookups, 5 lattice loads, 1 ink-table lookup) ≈ 50 µs plus blit ≈ 40 µs. Total ≈ 0.17 ms. Attempts per step scale as `N²/16 384` below the cap (80×24 ≈ 0.03 ms), and `dt = 0.25` at the cap costs ≈ 1.2 ms of update.
- **Acceptance**: `ISING-A1` for h ∈ {−4,−2,0,2,4} and T ∈ {0.5, 1, T_c, 3, 10}, `p₊(h)/p₊(−h) = e^{2h/T}` within 1e-12 relative (detailed balance against Boltzmann), `p₊(h) + p₊(−h) = 1` within 1e-15, `p₊(0) = ½` and `thr(0) = 2³¹` exactly, thresholds are non-decreasing in h, and T ∈ {0, −1, NaN, ±∞} gives probabilities in [0, 1] with no NaN; `ISING-A2` `T_c = 2.269185314` (1e-9), `m₀(2.0) = 0.911319`, `m₀(1.5) = 0.986500`, `e(2.0) = −1.745565`, `e(3.0) = −0.817310` (all 1e-6), `e(T_c) = −√2` (1e-9) and `|e(T_c ± 1e-6) + √2| < 1e-3`, m₀ = 0 for T ≥ T_c, m₀ is non-increasing on (0, T_c) and strictly decreasing on [1, T_c); `ISING-A3` on a seeded 32² torus, from a cold start at T = 2.0 after 1000 MCS the means over the next 3000 MCS (one sample per MCS) are ⟨|m|⟩ = 0.9113 ± 0.015 and ⟨e⟩ = −1.7456 ± 0.015, and from a random start at T = 3.0 they are ⟨|m|⟩ < 0.2 and ⟨e⟩ = −0.8173 ± 0.015; `ISING-A4` on a seeded 192² random start at T = 0.6·T_c the excess wall density `ρ − ρ_eq` (ρ = fraction of antiparallel bonds, `ρ_eq = (1 + e/2)/2 = 0.006678`) strictly decreases across 24, 48, 96 and 192 MCS and its least-squares log–log slope over those four points lies in [−0.62, −0.32] (Allen–Cahn −½); `ISING-A5` spins stay in {−1, +1} and the tracked M equals Σs after any sequence of updates (dt ∈ [0, 0.25]) and resizes including 1×1, a resize between two displays in the same `lattice_for` bucket at or above the cap leaves the lattice bit-identical, a bucket change makes every new site equal to its `resample_nearest` source, and 60 updates of 1/60 s perform `rate·N` attempts within ±1 with `rate = max(60·N/16 384, 1)`; `ISING-A6` T/T_c(t) is 0.60 at t = 0 and 10 s, 1.25 at 30 s and 1.00 at 50 s, is periodic with period 58 s and changes by ≤ 0.01 per 1/60 s step over a whole loop, and the label for (0.93, 0.71) is exactly `T/Tc 0.93  |m| 0.71`, is `T/Tc 0.93` for widths 12–23, and is absent below 12 columns or 4 rows.

### liesegang_rings  (Reactions, M10)
- **Intent**: the rhythm of periodic precipitation. A rack of gel columns stands side by side, each with its outer electrolyte poured on top. In each gel a faint supersaturation haze sinks and, every few seconds, snaps into a bright precipitate band. Bands grow wider, brighter and farther apart with depth (Jablczynski's spacing law). Each tube keeps its own tempo: the concentrated one packs bands tight (forming its first band later and deeper), the dilute one wide and slow, so the Matalon–Packter law reads as a visual polyrhythm. Then a redissolution front wipes the bands top-down and the rack regrows. ("Liesegang rings" names the phenomenon; in tubes it forms bands.)
- **Model**: per tube, a 1-D reaction–diffusion system with nucleation-and-growth precipitation. The ingredients are Ostwald's (1897) supersaturation theory, the nucleation threshold of Keller & Rubinow (1981) and the aggregation threshold of Chopard, Luthi & Droz (1994). The reactions are A + B → C (soluble product, idealised 1:1) and C → D (immobile precipitate), as in Liesegang's 1896 experiment of AgNO₃ diffusing into K₂Cr₂O₇-loaded gelatin to precipitate silver dichromate. The equations are `∂a/∂t = a'' − k·a·b`, `∂b/∂t = b'' − k·a·b`, `∂c/∂t = D_c·c'' + k·a·b − P`, `∂d/∂t = P`, `P = λ·max(c − c_s, 0)·[nᵢ]`. After each Euler step the per-node nucleation flag nᵢ is set irreversibly when `cᵢ ≥ c*` (homogeneous nucleation at supersaturation) or when `cᵢ ≥ c_g` and a neighbour was nucleated before the step (growth on adjacent precipitate; a Jacobi update, so the result is sweep-order-free). The step time is stored in `t_nuc[i]`. Parameters (dimensionless) are `D_a = D_b = 1`, `D_c = 0.3`, `k = 1`, `λ = 0.05`, `c_s = 0.01`, `c_g = 0.037`, `c* = 0.04`, `b₀ = 0.05`, and five presets `a₀ ∈ {0.7, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0}` (a₀/b₀ = 14–60). One unit ≈ 0.2 mm and one time unit ≈ ℓ²/D_A ≈ 27 s (D_A ≈ 1.5·10⁻⁹ m²/s), so the displayed 100 units are 2 cm grown in about 9 h, which are real Liesegang scales. The discretisation is part of the model: band positions shift about 10 % with τ while p changes by ≤ 0.02 between τ = 0.15 and 0.075. It uses a 3-point Laplacian and explicit Euler with `τ = 0.15`; positivity holds exactly since `τ(2 + k·a₀,max) = 0.75 ≤ 1` and `τ(2D_c + λ) ≤ 1`. Fields are f64 on 160-node columns, and node i sits at depth `x = i + 1` (the reservoir ghost is x = 0). The top ghost is `a = a₀` (reservoir) with no flux for b and c; the bottom ghost is `a = 0, b = b₀,c, c = 0` (semi-infinite gel; a closed bottom starves the front of b). All five tubes are always simulated, whatever is shown. Sim time is a warp of scene time s: `t_sim = T_END·(s/36 s)^{1.5}` with `T_END = 1200` through `grow`, then continuing at its final rate of 50 units/s, so `t_sim = 1500` when `hold` ends. Euler steps are taken whole from an accumulator, so the scheme always uses exactly τ. `scan_bands(d)` runs every 0.25 s: nodes with `d ≥ 5 %` of the column max form contiguous runs, each run is a band with d-weighted centroid x, width = run length and birth time `t_n = min t_nuc` over its nodes, and the first run is the junction zone, excluded from every law. Reference behaviour (a₀ = 2, t = 1500): bands at x = 8.9 (junction zone, 17 wide), 21.3, 28.3, 36.9, 48.6, 63.9, 83.6 and 106.8, ratios 1.33, 1.31, 1.32, 1.32, 1.31, 1.28 (last five mean 1.304), widths 5 → 12 (width law), and x_n/√t_n rising to 2.9 (last four mean 2.84; time law, Morse & Pierce 1903). Across the presets at t = 1400, p = 0.507, 0.406, 0.341, 0.299, 0.264, which fits `p = F + G·b₀/a₀` with F = 0.190, G = 4.41, R² = 0.999 (Matalon & Packter 1955; Antal et al. 1998). The looping `Timeline` (50 s) is `grow` 36 s → `hold` 6 s (bands thicken and the fronts leave the view) → `dissolve` 8 s. Dissolve is a scripted redissolution with the reaction frozen, like an ammonia front complexing the silver salt to [Ag(NH₃)₂]⁺ (or Co(OH)₂ in excess NH₃). The front descends as `y_e = 120·√progress` nodes (it passes the view bottom at 69 % of the phase), and above it `c, d ← ·e^{−3·dt}`; a, b and the flags do not change. At the loop, state resets to `(a, b, c, d, n, t_nuc) = (0, b₀,c, 0, 0, false, ∞)` with `b₀,c = b₀·(1 + 0.03ξ)`, where ξ ∈ [−1, 1] is drawn from `ctx.rng` at the loop (ξ = 0 in the first cycle). `regrow` is simply the next cycle, with slightly different bands.
- **Motion**: in the first second (at s ≈ 0.7 s) each junction zone precipitates as a dim, cloudy cap under the meniscus, and a dim-red haze starts to sink. Bands are then born 2–5 s apart, staggered across tubes (a₀ = 2: s ≈ 6, 8, 11, 15, 20, 28 s). At each birth a thin faint line appears just ahead of the haze, then brightens and thickens over about 1 s as it drains the haze, leaving a dark depletion gap between the band and the sinking haze. During `dissolve` a clear front sweeps down and erases the bands top-down; the tubes rest clear for about 2.5 s before the next cycle.
- **Resize**: the chemistry never depends on the terminal. The five 160-node columns are the capped lattice, decoupled from the display (ADR-0012). 1-D columns have no aspect, so there is one bucket and nothing to resample; no resize touches state or draws from `ctx.rng`. Only the layout is rebuilt: `n = clamp(⌊(w + 4)/24⌋, 1, 5)` visible tubes, showing presets {2}, {1,3}, {0,2,4}, {0,1,3,4} or {0..4} (always dilute → concentrated). The interior width is `clamp(round(w/(2.6n)), 1, 16)` columns with even gaps. Row 0 is the header, row 1 the liquid, and the gel runs to the bottom edge (tubes run off-screen, no base). The gel's `D_px = 2·max(h − 2, 0)` pixel rows show nodes [0, V) with `V = clamp(2.4·D_px, 50, 100)` through linear box-filter weights built in `resize`: V = 100 from h = 23 (≈ 2.3 nodes/px at 80×24, ≈ 1.1 at 200×50), and short terminals zoom in on the top of the gel (V = 50 at 40×12) rather than merge bands and gaps into one striped column; the chemistry and the ratios always use the full column. A concave meniscus gives each pixel the depth `x = y − y₀ − sag·(1 − u²)`, with u ∈ [−1, 1] across the tube and sag = 0 below h = 22 (fewer than 40 gel rows, where a 1 px sag would only notch single pixels), 1 px for 22 ≤ h < 40 and 2 px from h = 40; pixels with x < 0 are liquid, so the interface and the bands curve gently. Cylinder shading is `× (0.8 + 0.2·√(1 − u²))`.
- **Inks**: gel pixels are `Role::Seq` at the highest of three levels, times the cylinder shading. Precipitate is `(0.30 + 0.70·min(d/0.2, 1)^{0.75})·clamp(d/0.02, 0, 1)` from the box-filtered d, a continuous fade-in, so a newborn band is a thin faint line that brightens and a dissolving band fades fully out; it is not drawn below d = 0.002. Early bands are ≈ 0.6 and deep mature bands → 1.0 (pale yellow). The junction zone is scaled by 0.45 (keeping its cloudy gradient) so the eye goes to the bands. Haze is `0.25·clamp((c/c* − 0.5)/0.5, 0, 1)` (dim red, only above half the nucleation threshold, c ≥ c*/2 = 2·c_s). Clear gel is not drawn (terminal background), so depletion gaps and virgin gel are both dark. The liquid row and meniscus pixels are `Role::Wave` level 60, the glass walls are cell glyphs in `Role::Structure` level 100, and the header is `Role::Text` (ratios 170, legend 110).
- **ASCII**: walls are `|` (Unicode `▕` left and `▏` right, hugging the gel) and liquid cells are `~`. The `Pixels::blit` closure classes each gel pixel from the fields, not the shaded ink: band iff the box-filtered `d ≥ 0.04` outside the junction zone; faint iff otherwise `d > 0.002` (faint precipitate and the whole junction zone); haze iff otherwise haze > 0 (clear gel carrying only the supersaturation haze); otherwise empty. band/band ⇒ `=`, band on top only ⇒ `-`, band on bottom only ⇒ `_`, faint/faint ⇒ `:`, one faint or any haze ⇒ `.`, empty ⇒ space, and the ink carries the level. The haze is thus a light stipple: its large sinking cloud never outweighs the `-`/`=` bands or the `:` junction cap. Wavy bands read at half-cell resolution. The 80×24 snapshots show three `|` tubes under a `~` row; at t = 5 each tube shows a `:` junction cloud over a sinking `.` haze; the first `-`/`=` bands appear from t ≈ 8 (the dilute tube first, the concentrated one at t ≈ 12).
- **Label**: the header is on row 0. Centred over each tube is its live ratio of its two deepest complete bands (a band is complete once a deeper band has nucleated), for example `1.31`, blank until that exists. The legend sits at column 1 while any ratio is shown, keeping a 2-column gap before the first tube's ratio column r₀ (the walls start below the header, so nothing else can collide): `x(n+1)/x(n)` when r₀ ≥ 14 (80×24, 200×50 and 40×12), `xn+1/xn` when 10 ≤ r₀ < 14, otherwise none. During `dissolve` a tube's ratio leaves once the front `y_e` passes the end of the deeper band it measures (or the view bottom), so the numbers follow the wipe and the rest shows clear tubes under an empty header; the legend leaves with the last ratio. Numbers are dropped when the tube interior is < 4 columns, and the header when h < 6. Written from a stack buffer.
- **Perf**: update is ≤ 5 tubes × 160 nodes × ≤ 6 Euler sub-steps per 1/60 s (the peak comes in `hold`; 1–2 early) ≈ 4.8 k node updates ≈ 25 µs, plus a band scan of 5 × 160 nodes every 0.25 s. `dt = 0.25` costs ≤ 84 sub-steps ≈ 0.35 ms. Render at 200×50 is ≈ 7 k gel pixels ≈ 25 µs plus blit ≈ 30 µs. Total ≈ 0.1 ms, independent of terminal size; all state is fixed-size arrays, so nothing allocates after `resize`.
- **Acceptance**: `LIES-A1` for any sequence of `update(dt ∈ [0, 0.25])` over two full loops all fields stay finite with `0 ≤ a ≤ a₀`, `0 ≤ b ≤ b₀,c`, `c ≥ 0`, `d ≥ 0`, and during grow and hold every node's d is non-decreasing and its nucleation flag and `t_nuc` never change once set; `LIES-A2` on a closed 96-node column (no flux at both ends, a = 1 on the top 40 nodes, b = b₀ everywhere), after 10⁴ Euler steps `Σ(b + c + d) = 96·b₀` and `Σ(a − b) = 40 − 96·b₀` within 1e-9 relative (A + B → C → D stoichiometry); `LIES-A3` preset a₀ = 2 on a 160-node column run to t = 1500 gives ≥ 7 bands (junction zone included) with x ≤ 112, and excluding the junction zone the last five ratios `x_{n+1}/x_n` lie within ±4 % of their mean, the mean is in [1.27, 1.35] (reference 1.304), and the deepest band is wider than the first band after the junction zone (reference 12 vs 5 nodes; spacing and width laws); `LIES-A4` in the same run `x_n/√t_n` for the four deepest bands lies within ±8 % of their mean and the mean is in [2.65, 3.0] (reference 2.84; time law); `LIES-A5` across the five presets (160 nodes, t = 1400), p = mean of the last three ratios − 1 is strictly decreasing in a₀, and the least-squares fit `p = F + G·b₀/a₀` has R² ≥ 0.98, G ∈ [3.5, 5.5] and F ∈ [0.12, 0.26] (reference 0.190, 4.41, 0.999; Matalon–Packter); `LIES-A6` the phases grow 36 / hold 6 / dissolve 8 s loop at 50 s, `t_sim(36 s) = 1200` and `t_sim(42 s) = 1500` (1e-9) and t_sim strictly increases through grow and hold, during dissolve a, b and the flags are unchanged and Σd over nodes [0, 100) is non-increasing in every tube and ends below 2 % of its starting value, and at the loop every field equals the reset state exactly with `|b₀,c/b₀ − 1| ≤ 0.03`; `LIES-A7` two seeded scenes driven by the same update sequence across a loop boundary, one of them resized through 1×1, 7×3, 80×24 and 200×50 in between, have bit-identical tube states, and the visible presets follow the n(w) table (w = 20 ⇒ {2}, 80 ⇒ {0,2,4}, 200 ⇒ all five); `LIES-A8` the legend is `x(n+1)/x(n)` for r₀ ≥ 14, `xn+1/xn` for 10 ≤ r₀ < 14 and absent below, and the long form shows at 80×24, 200×50 and 40×12; at 80×24 (seed 42) the header shows the legend and three ratios at s = 41.9 (hold) and is blank at s = 49.9 (end of dissolve); a pixel with `d ≤ 0.002` and haze > 0 is class haze and any cell with haze but no faint, band or liquid pixel is `.`, while faint/faint stays `:`; and V = 100 at 80×24 and 200×50, 50 at 40×12 and `2.4·D_px` at 80×16, with the deepest window of 40×12 ending at node 50.

### molecule_viewer  (Reactions, M10)
- **Intent**: a slowly tumbling ball-and-stick model of a real molecule, lit like a physical model on a dark bench. Near atoms are bright and far atoms recede. The gallery runs water, methane, ethanol, benzene, caffeine, then buckminsterfullerene. Each molecule grows out of a point, turns, and collapses back into the point before the next one grows.
- **Model**: static tables in the module. Each holds element, position (Å) and bonds with an order of `Single`, `Double` or `Aromatic` (1.5). Benzene uses `Aromatic`; caffeine uses Kekulé single and double bonds. Planar molecules are stored with their plane in world xy (normal along z), so yaw takes them through face-on and edge-on views. Geometries:
  - water: r(OH) 0.9572 Å, ∠HOH 104.52° (Benedict, Gailar & Plyler 1956).
  - methane: T_d, r(CH) 1.087 Å.
  - ethanol: trans (anti H–O–C–C) conformer, r(CC) 1.512, r(CO) 1.431, r(OH) 0.971 Å (NIST CCCBDB experimental); the remaining C–H bonds and angles come from CCCBDB, or are tetrahedral with r(CH) 1.09 Å.
  - benzene: D₆h, r(CC) 1.397 Å, r(CH) 1.084 Å (Herzberg 1966).
  - caffeine C8H10N4O2: the 3-D conformer of PubChem CID 2519 (24 atoms, 25 bonds, planar purine), with double bonds C2=O, C6=O, C4=C5 and C8=N9.
  - C60: generated at build, not typed in. Each of the 30 edges of an icosahedron carries two atoms at fractions `t` and `1 − t`. The icosahedron's vertices are the cyclic permutations of (0, ±1, ±φ); its edge is 2, so it is scaled by `a/2`. With `a = 2·r₅₆ + r₆₆` and `t = r₅₆/a`, the pentagon (5:6) bonds are r₅₆ = 1.458 Å and the hexagon-fusing (6:6) bonds, which lie along the old edges, are r₆₆ = 1.401 Å (Hedberg et al. 1991, gas-phase electron diffraction). Every atom then sits 3.562 Å from the centre.

  Every table is recentred on its centre of mass (H 1.008, C 12.011, N 14.007, O 15.999 u).

  Balls and bonds:
  - Ball radius is 0.20 × the Bondi (1964) van der Waals radius (H 1.20, C 1.70, N 1.55, O 1.52 Å), which keeps ≥ 0.5 Å of visible stick on C60's 1.40–1.46 Å bonds, so the cage reads as rings of balls rather than a heap of blobs.
  - Bodies are chosen once per molecule and size, per class (heavy atoms, hydrogens), from the class's largest unmagnified ball at full size: *Dot* (the centre cell only) below 0.95 rows, *Compact* (a 3-cell lozenge on the centre row) below 1.2 rows, *Round* (a shaded `draw::disc`) above; a body is floored at 0.8·0.95·s rows so small far atoms keep a few lit cells. At 80×24 only caffeine and C60 are dot-and-stick.
  - Bonds are drawn only while the gallery scale `s ≥ 0.5` (below it the atoms crowd the centroid and sticks only thicken the clump). A bond is a Bresenham stroke between the two projected centres. It skips every cell that `draw::disc` would paint for either end ball, so sticks run from ball edge to ball edge.
  - A double bond is drawn as two parallel strokes, one cell either side of the axis, when its projected length is ≥ 6 cells and neither end is a Dot body. Otherwise, and for aromatic bonds, it is one stroke.

  View:
  - The camera is `geom::Camera { yaw, pitch, distance: 5·r_eff }`, with `r_eff = max(maxᵢ(|rᵢ| + r_ball,ᵢ), 1.25 Å)` so water (raw ≈ 1.16 Å) is framed but not ballooned.
  - The canvas below the label row (height `h − 1`, floored at 1) is centred at row `1 + (h − 1)/2`.
  - Scale (rows/Å) is `s(t) · draw::fit_radius(w × (h − 1), 0.86) / (1.05·r_eff)`. A sphere of radius `r_eff` seen from `5·r_eff` projects to at most `5/√24` ≈ 1.021 of its radius, and the ≈ 3 % on top covers floored bodies and rounded cells, so no orientation leaves the frame; `s(t)` is the gallery scale (Motion). (The on-axis bound 5/(5 − 1) = 1.25 would leave water and ethanol at ≈ 75 % of the framing radius.)

  Painter's order: in every `render`, all atoms and bonds (≤ 150 items; a bond's depth is the mean of its end depths) are sorted far → near. They sit in fixed-size stack arrays (`[u16; 150]`, `[f32; 150]`), sorted with `sort_unstable_by` on `(depth.total_cmp, index)`, which is deterministic and uses no heap.

  Shading: the sphere normal is `n = (nx, −ny, √(1 − nx² − ny²))` from `draw::disc`, where screen y points down. Light comes from `L = normalise(−0.45, 0.55, 0.70)` (upper left, front). Intensity is `I = 0.35 + 0.65·max(0, n·L)`, and cells with `n·H > 0.95` are specular (Blinn–Phong, `H = normalise(L + V)` for a viewer on +z, so the highlight sits ≈ 0.39 of the radius up-left of centre).
- **Motion**: everything is a closed-form function of scene time `t`.
  - The gallery is a `Timeline` of `grow` 0.6 s → `hold` 8.8 s → `shrink` 0.6 s (10 s per molecule). The molecule is `(start + timeline.cycle(t)) mod 6`, where `start` is seeded.
  - Scene time begins at the start of the first `hold`, so the first frame shows a full molecule, and successive 15 s `sim` visits show different molecules.
  - The gallery scale `s` is `ease::smoothstep(progress)` in `grow`, 1 in `hold` and `1 − smoothstep(progress)` in `shrink`. Atoms converge on the centroid and their levels dim with `s²`. The molecule and label change only at the slot boundary, where `s = 0`.
  - Yaw is `ω·τ − 0.78 rad` with `ω = 2π/10 s`, where τ is the time since the slot began, so each molecule reaches full size in a three-quarter view (≈ −0.4 rad). Pitch is `0.30 + 0.30·sin(2πt/17 s)` rad.
  - C60's equator sweeps up to ≈ 9 columns per second at 80×24 (≈ 19 at 200×50).
- **Resize**: view only; there is no simulation state (ADR-0012). The centre, scale and label tier are recomputed. Scene time, slot and orientation are kept, and the seeded `start` is drawn only on the first resize.
- **Inks**: CPK colours mapped to roles: H `Text` (white), C `Structure` (grey), N `Cold` (blue), O `Hot` (red). Each bond is split at its projected midpoint, and each half takes its atom's role at 0.8× that atom's level.
  - The depth cue is `level = s²·(100 + 155·ν)·I`, with `ν = (depth + r_eff)/(2·r_eff) ∈ [0, 1]`.
  - The projected ball radius is `r_ball · scale · d/(d − depth)`.
  - Specular cells are `█` at level 255. The label is `Text`.
- **ASCII**: every atom's centre cell shows its element symbol, upper-case in the near half (`ν ≥ 0.5`) and lower-case in the far half (`C`/`c`, `H`/`h`). Ball bodies (Compact and Round) are `: + * # @` by intensity `I`, dim to bright (the dimmest tier is `:`, so a ball's shadow rim stays distinct from rear bonds). Bonds use `- | / \` by projected slope; a bond whose ends are both in the far half (ν < 0.5) is dotted `.` (Unicode `·`), so C60's rear lattice recedes. At 80×24 the frame reads like a textbook structure drawn in letters.

  Unicode uses `█▓▒░` bodies by `I`, `●` for small heavy atoms and `•` for small H, with `─ │ ╱ ╲` bonds.
- **Label**: top-left (row 0, col 1): `caffeine  C8H10N4O2  194.19 g/mol`, all in ASCII. The Hill formula (C first, then H, then the rest alphabetically; H2O for water) and the molar mass (2 decimals) are checked against the table. Tiers, used in order when the label does not fit `width − 2`: drop the mass, then the name, then show nothing. The label is never clipped and switches only at the slot boundary.
- **Perf**: `update` only advances `t` (O(1)). The worst case is `render` of C60 at 200×50 (≈ 5.1 rows/Å, C ball ≈ 1.75 rows):
  - 60 `Camera::project` calls;
  - a 150-item stack sort (≈ 3 µs);
  - ≈ 60 discs × 20 cells + 90 bonds × 15 cells ≈ 2.5k cell writes.

  That is ≈ 0.05 ms, with no heap use in `update` or `render`.
- **Acceptance**: `MOL-A1` every table is chemically consistent: with the covalent radii of Cordero et al. (2008) (H 0.31, C 0.76, N 0.71, O 0.66 Å), bonded pairs satisfy `0.80 ≤ d/(rᵢ + rⱼ) ≤ 1.12` and every non-bonded pair has `d/(rᵢ + rⱼ) ≥ 1.35`, so the bond list equals the distance-derived connectivity; each atom's summed bond order equals its valence (H 1, C 4, N 3, O 2; aromatic = 1.5); the Hill formula derived from the table equals the label's (H2O, CH4, C2H6O, C6H6, C8H10N4O2, C60), the derived molar mass matches 18.015, 16.043, 46.069, 78.114, 194.194 and 720.66 g/mol within 0.001, and the label's 2-decimal mass is within 0.005 + 1e-9 of it; and the centre of mass is at the origin within 1e-6 Å; `MOL-A2` reference geometry: water r(OH) = 0.9572 ± 0.0005 Å and ∠HOH = 104.52 ± 0.05°; methane r(CH) = 1.087 ± 0.001 Å with all six ∠HCH = 109.47 ± 0.05°; ethanol has 9 atoms and 8 bonds with r(CC) = 1.512, r(CO) = 1.431 and r(OH) = 0.971 (each ± 0.001 Å) and an H–O–C–C dihedral of 180 ± 1°; benzene's 12 atoms are coplanar within 0.001 Å in the xy plane with r(CC) = 1.397 ± 0.001 and r(CH) = 1.084 ± 0.001 Å; caffeine's 14 heavy atoms are coplanar within 0.05 Å; C60 has 60 atoms and 90 bonds, 3 bonds and exactly one 6:6 bond per atom, 30 bonds of 1.401 ± 0.001 Å and 60 of 1.458 ± 0.001 Å, every atom at 3.562 ± 0.002 Å from the centre, and its 5:6 bonds form exactly 12 disjoint 5-cycles; `MOL-A3` the motion is rigid and fits the frame: at 500 sampled `hold` times over a 60 s cycle, the rotated coordinates (recovered from an orthographic `Camera` at scale 1 as `(x/ASPECT, −y, depth)`) keep every pairwise distance of the table within 1e-9 Å; at 80×24, 200×50, 40×12, 120×36, 60×20, 100×30, 30×10 and 160×40 every atom projects (`Some`), and every cell of its ball lies inside columns `[0, w)` and rows `[1, h)`; at 80×24 and 200×50 every molecule's balls reach ≥ 0.85 of the framing radius (centre distance, aspect-corrected, plus projected ball radius) at some point of its hold turn, so small molecules are not left timid in the frame; the depth-cue factor `100 + 155·ν` and the projected ball radius (for a fixed element) are strictly increasing in depth; `MOL-A4` painter's order: the render order is a permutation of all atoms and bonds, sorted by non-decreasing depth with ties broken by index; when the pure paint helper draws two overlapping atoms, the shared cells carry the nearer atom's ink; and no bond cell is one that `draw::disc` paints for either end ball; `MOL-A5` gallery timeline: from the seeded start, one 60 s cycle shows the six molecules in gallery order, 10 s each; `s` is exactly 1 throughout `hold`, never outside `[0, 1]`, and continuous (`|Δs| ≤ 0.05` per 1/60 s step); the molecule index and label change only at slot boundaries, where `s = 0`; seeds 1..=32 yield all six starting molecules (six uniform draws would cover all six only 6!/6⁶ ≈ 1.5 % of the time); `MOL-A6` label and fallback: the chosen label tier is the longest one that fits `width − 2` (empty when none fits), so the label is never clipped; in ASCII mode at 80×24 the frame is pure ASCII, and every atom whose centre cell is not covered by a later-drawn item (checked by repainting the later items onto a blank canvas) shows its element symbol, upper-case when `ν ≥ 0.5` and lower-case otherwise.

### physarum  (Living Systems, M10)
- **Intent**: a slime mould solving a transport problem in time-lapse. A glowing inoculum spills outward in a lace of veins, finds the scattered oat flakes, and thickens into a few pulsing tubes that link them like a rail map (Nakagaki et al. 2000; Tero et al. 2010). The network then starves and dissolves tube by tube into darkness. Fresh flakes drop onto the empty plate, and a new colony starts somewhere else.
- **Model**: the agent-based approximation of Jones (2010). The plate is the screen above the label row: `h_p = h − 1` rows when the label is drawn and `h_p = h` otherwise, so the plate's absorbing edge keeps every tube off that row and the label sits under the organism, not inside it. The chemoattractant `T` is a `Grid<f32>` on a capped square lattice `L = grid::lattice_for(Size(w, 2h_p), 20_000, 1.25)` (CORE-R48). That gives 197×98 at 200×50 and 80×46 at 80×24; below the cap the lattice equals the pixel display, so the view is exact. There are `n = max(1, round(p·lw·lh))` agents with `p = 5 %` (965 and 184), within the population range Jones explored. Each agent has a continuous position and a heading in `Z₁₆` (22.5° units) with a 16-entry `(cos, sin)` table, so no trig runs per step; turns are addition in Z₁₆ (`rotate(h, t)`, + = clockwise on screen). Its site is its rounded position. Each Jones step is two passes over a fresh Fisher–Yates shuffle of the live agents (rand's `SliceRandom::shuffle` on a `SplitMix64` drawn from `ctx.rng`). **Motor pass**: step `SS = 1` px along the heading. If the target site is on the lattice and unoccupied (particle exclusion, one agent per site), or is the agent's own site (a sub-site move, possible after a diagonal step from a non-integer position), the agent moves and deposits `depT = 5` at its new site. Otherwise it stays, deposits nothing and takes a uniformly random new heading. **Sensory pass**: sample `T` at three sensors at distance `SO = clamp(round(0.09·min(lw, lh)), 3, 9)` px (4 at 80×24, 9 at 200×50) and angles `h − SA, h, h + SA` with `SA = 22.5°`. Off-lattice sensors read −1, so walls repel. The agent then rotates by `RA = 45°` according to Jones's rule: F strictly greatest ⇒ keep the heading; F strictly least ⇒ ±RA by coin; otherwise turn toward the strictly stronger side (FL < FR ⇒ toward R, FR < FL ⇒ toward L); any remaining tie ⇒ keep. **Chemoattractant**: after both passes, `T ← (1 − δ)·mean₃ₓ₃(T)` as a separable 3-tap box filter with `δ = 0.1`. The plate edge absorbs: sites off the lattice count as 0 in the mean, so attractant leaks out there and the network sits on the plate instead of tracing the frame. **Drawn field**: a second grid `V` receives the same deposits (at inoculation and in the motor pass) and the same diffusion and decay, but no food pinning. It is what is drawn (through the display EMA `S`, see Inks); the agents sense `T` only. **Food**: `n_f = clamp(round(lw·lh/800), 4, 9)` flakes (5 at 80×24, 9 at 200×50) at seeded positions in the central 80 %. They are at least `0.25·min(lw, lh)` apart and at least `max(2·SO, 0.15·min(lw, lh))` beyond the inoculum's rim, so the colony has to spread before it touches one. Placement uses at most 200 rejection tries and accepts fewer flakes on a crowded plate, including 0. Food discs of radius 1.5 px are pinned to `T = max(T, 20)` (4·depT) during explore and consolidate, which anchors trunk lines between sources as in Tero et al. (2010). **Inoculum**: a disc of radius `√(2n/π)` px (50 % occupancy) centred at a seeded point in the central 40 %, with random headings. Each agent deposits once at seeding, and `V` is then blurred twice by the same filter with δ = 0, so the colony shows at t = 0 as a soft drop rather than a checkerboard of single deposits. **Rate**: one Jones step per `SIM_DT` (60 per simulated second), counted by the core `FixedStep(SIM_DT, 15)` (CORE-R9..R11). This is a time-lapse: the network in Tero et al. took about a day to grow. **Timeline** (48 s, loops): explore 10 s → consolidate 30 s (the physics is unchanged; the network coarsens under its own dynamics while the food holds the trunk lines) → fade 6 s (food unpinned; each agent dies at a seeded uniform time in the phase, and a dead agent stops moving and depositing and frees its site, so tubes thin and snap one by one) → rest 2 s (the trail decays to black, `0.9^120 ≈ 3·10⁻⁶`). At rest start, the next cycle's inoculum centre and food are drawn from `ctx.rng`, and the new flakes fade in. The inoculum is seeded at the cycle boundary. A step spans `[t₀, t₁]`: starvation and the Jones step are decided at `t₀`, and these two events at `t₁`, so the state already holds them when the scene time reads `t₁` and the drawing never runs ahead of the colony (PHY-A5). Every cycle differs, and each seed is deterministic.
- **Motion**: agents move 60 px/s and the trail changes every step. Within 1 s the inoculum grows filaments that reach visibly past its rim; because the rim streams outward at 60 px/s, at 80×24 the nearest flakes are touched within about a second and tied in by `+` tubes by ≈ 3 s. By 10 s the sources are joined by a web with loops. During consolidation, brightness pulses along the stable tubes as agents stream through them, while their edges hold still (PHY-A7). The fade reads as a slow disintegration, followed by a dark plate where new flakes appear, and then a new bloom.
- **Resize** (ADR-0012): the lattice comes from `lattice_for`. If it is unchanged (same aspect bucket, with both sizes at or above the cap in both axes), only the display map and `Pixels` are rebuilt, no `ctx.rng` is consumed, and the simulation stays bit-identical. If it changes, `T` and `V` are resampled with `grid::resample_bilinear`. Agent positions are scaled by `(lw'/lw, lh'/lh)` and re-inserted into the occupancy grid in index order, and any collisions are dropped. Outside fade and rest, the population is then brought to `n' = round(p·lw'·lh')`: trimmed evenly in index order (agent i of n is kept when `⌊(i + 1)·n'/n⌋ > ⌊i·n'/n⌋`, with no randomness), or topped up with agents placed on free sites where `T` is above the mean, using at most 16 `ctx.rng` tries per agent before skipping it. Food is scaled, SO is recomputed, and the timeline is untouched. Agent, order and occupancy buffers have capacity 1000 (= p·20 000) from `build`.
- **Inks**: the drawn field `S` is `V` through a display-only EMA, `S += (V − S)·(1 − e^(−h/0.15 s))` after every step, reset to `V` at inoculation and on a lattice change. The discrete agents' per-step deposits make tube edges flip grade on almost every frame (≈ 30 % of lit ASCII cells at 80×24); the filter keeps the pulsing and the growth but stills the edges. The agents sense `T`, so no model result depends on it. `S` uses `Role::Seq` via `ink::seq(0.2 + 0.8·u)`, with `u = min(1, √(S / (2.5·T_ref)))`. `T_ref` is reset at every inoculation (the first and each cycle boundary) to the bright-half mean of the new inoculum's `V`, so every bloom opens on the same soft drop, and is otherwise an EMA (τ = 1 s) of the mean `V` over sites above `V`'s global mean (the global total comes out of the diffusion pass). During the first 1.5 s of explore it follows `max(bright-half mean, 0.45·max V)` with τ = 0.25 s instead: a young inoculum is a flat stack of deposits about 2.5× its bright-half mean, which that reference alone paints as one saturated `#` disc (≈ 44 % of lit cells), and exposing for its peak leaves `#` to the hottest ≈ 10 % of the drop. It is frozen during fade and rest, so the dying network dims instead of being re-exposed. (Inheriting the old network's frozen reference instead opened every later bloom as an underexposed `.`/`:` haze with holes, ≈ 25 % fewer lit cells than the first and no `+`/`#`.) Starting the ramp at 0.2 skips the colormap's violet floor. In astro the organism shows crimson halos, amber tubes and pale-yellow junctions (Physarum is yellow), which reads differently from the full-range Seq fields of gray_scott, belousov_zhabotinsky and liesegang_rings. Pixels with `u < 0.45` (`V` below ≈ `T_ref/2`) stay `Ink::BLANK`, so the plate is the terminal's own dark; a lower floor lights the diffuse attractant around every tube and fattens the veins into blobs. Food is `Role::Structure` 255, pale cool oat flakes against the warm organism; because the pinned `T` is not drawn, a flake has no glow until the organism reaches it. The colony's flakes fade out over the last 1 s of the fade (`255·(1 − smoothstep)`), so rest opens on an empty plate, and the next colony's fade in from 0 to 255 over rest (`255·smoothstep(progress)`); a flake at level 0 is not drawn. Label `Role::Text` 110. Agents are not drawn: the trail is the organism.
- **ASCII**: half-block `Pixels` (CORE-R42) over the plate, `w × 2h_p`; each display pixel samples `S` through `grid::SampleMap` (CORE-R52) and is tone-mapped in `update`; the floor test runs on `S/(2.5·T_ref) < 0.45²`, so dark pixels skip the square root. The `blit` closure works per cell: food pixels count as blank; both blank ⇒ untouched; otherwise `m = max(u_top, u_bottom)` maps to `.` (< 0.60), `:` (< 0.75), `+` (< 0.90) or `#`, using the brighter pixel's Seq ink. In ASCII, `render` then puts a single `@` in the flake's Structure ink at each flake's centre cell; in Unicode a flake stays a 5-pixel plus. At 80×24 the snapshots show a soft `.`/`:`/`+` drop and five `@` at t = 0, filaments already reaching the two nearest flakes at t = 1 s, and a `.`/`:` lace with `+`/`#` trunks through the `@`s at t = 5 s.
- **Label**: `SA 22.5 RA 45 SO 9` (ASCII, rebuilt in `resize` when SO changes) right-aligned in the row below the plate (GEN-R13), drawn only when `w ≥ 40` and `h ≥ 12` and it fits right of the title, with a blank cell either side, so it never looks glued to a tube.
- **Perf**: at 200×50 (lattice 197×98, 965 agents, 1 Jones step per update): the shuffle, the motor and sensory passes, two separable diffuse-decay passes (`T` and `V`; the `V` pass also returns its total), one more pass over `V` for the bright-half mean and peak, food pinning (< 1 µs) and the display EMA of `S` (one pass), then the `SampleMap` tone map of 200×98 pixels, all in `update`; `render` blits 9.8 k cells. Criterion on a loaded machine (load average ≈ 15–26): 0.28–0.39 ms at 200×50 (0.64 ms at load ≈ 80–99 with the 197×98 plate) and 0.71–1.05 ms at 400×100, against the < 1 ms aim (GEN-R8 is measured at 200×50). At 400×100 the 80 k-pixel tone map is ≈ 0.45 ms of that; testing the floor on the square saves ≈ 5–10 % of it. The simulation cost is independent of terminal size above the cap; the tone map and the blit scale with the display. Steady state is allocation-free.
- **Acceptance**: `PHY-A1` `steer(f, fl, fr, coin)` returns exactly 0 / ±2 (by coin) / +2 / −2 / 0 in Z₁₆ units (+ = toward FR) for the five cases of Jones's rule, listed literally and then exhaustively over all orderings of three values with ties; every entry of the 16-direction table has unit length to 1e-6; `rotate` is addition in Z₁₆ (sixteen +1 rotations return to every start, `rotate(0, −RA) = 14`, `rotate(15, RA) = 1`) and + is clockwise on screen; and an agent heading east with attractant only at its FR sensor turns by +RA in the sensory pass; `PHY-A2` `diffuse_decay`: a constant field `c` maps to `(1 − δ)c` at interior sites, `(1 − δ)c·6/9` at edge sites and `(1 − δ)c·4/9` at corners (absorbing edge; relative error 1e-6), and the returned total matches the field's sum; with δ = 0 an interior impulse spreads to exactly its 3×3 block at 1/9 each and conserves the total to 1e-6; for any seeded non-negative field, `max(out) ≤ (1 − δ)·max(in)`, no output is negative, and `min(out) ≥ (1 − δ)·min(in)` at interior sites; `PHY-A3` over a seeded 600-step run on an 80×48 lattice followed by a full fade, after every step the occupancy count equals the live agent count, no two agents share a site and every agent is on the lattice; the live count is constant outside fade, non-increasing within it and 0 at its end; `PHY-A4` network forms (thresholds from a Jones-model prototype): from a seeded inoculum on a 96×48 lattice with 5 food sources, every flake lies at least `√(2n/π) + max(2·SO, 0.15·48)` from the inoculum centre, at step 0 no site within Chebyshev distance 2 of a flake centre has trail (no flake's 3×3 disc is 8-adjacent to the inoculum); after 600 steps (10 s) the trail's coefficient of variation is ≥ 1.2, the fraction of sites above the mean is in [0.2, 0.45], and the largest 8-connected component of {T > mean} holds ≥ 85 % of those sites; the largest component of the unpinned drawn field {V > mean} holds ≥ 4 of the 5 food sources (this holds for about 90 % of seeds; seed 42 is used); as a control, the same seeded colony stepped without food pinning, judged on the same unpinned field, links ≤ 3 of the 5 sources (seed 42: 4 fed against 0), so the pinning is what ties them in; on `T` the pinning itself would hold every fed flake centre above the mean (~0.5 s in debug); `PHY-A5` on a 40×24 display (lattice 40×46) the beats are explore [0, 10) / consolidate [10, 40) / fade [40, 46) / rest [46, 48) s and repeat at 48 s, the total trail at 47.9 s is < 1e-3 of its mean over consolidate, the second cycle's food layout and inoculum centre differ from the first cycle's, and both are identical across two runs with the same seed (< 1.5 s in debug); and across the loop seams, over every step of two cycles plus 1 s (seed 42): during explore and consolidate the drawn flakes are exactly the colony's own layout at level 255, the flake level never changes by more than 12 between consecutive steps, and the drawn layout changes (≥ 4 times) only between frames whose levels are both ≤ 12, so no seam flashes or cuts a layout at full brightness (< 2 s in debug); `PHY-A6` resizing 220×60 → 240×64 (both lattice 197×101) leaves the lattice and `T` bit-identical, and after 60 more steps `T` is bit-identical to a run that never resized; resizing 200×50 → 80×24 (197×98 → 80×46) during consolidate gives a new max ≤ the old max, a new mean within 20 % of the old, every agent on the lattice with exclusion intact, an unchanged food count and an unchanged beat, and a population trimmed to at most `round(p·80·46) = 184` and at least 90 % of it; resizing 80×24 → 200×50 during consolidate tops the population up to ≥ 90 % of `round(p·197·98)`; `PHY-A7` drawing: on the seeded 80×24 display at t = 0, no Seq pixel lies within 3 pixels of a flake's centre (the pinned `T` is not drawn), the ASCII frame holds exactly one `@` per flake, at the flake's own cell, and in both glyph modes the cells at x = 0 and just after the label are blank; at 1 s samples over the first 100 s (two cycles) the bottom row holds exactly the label and nothing else in both glyph modes; in ASCII frames at 30 FPS on the seeded 80×24 display, the first frame of the second bloom (t = 48 s) has a lit-cell count within ±20 % of the t = 0 frame and a nonzero `+`/`#` share (inheriting the old reference gives ≈ 25 % fewer and no `+`/`#`), and `#` is ≤ 20 % of the lit cells in every frame of the first 1.5 s of the first and the second bloom (≈ 44 % without the bloom exposure), and over 2 s of consolidation (from t = 77 s) ≤ 15 % of lit cells change glyph from one frame to the next (≈ 30 % without the display EMA; ≈ 8 % with it) (~1 s in debug).

### solar_surface  (Sun & Planets, M10)
- **Intent**: the Sun up close as a slowly turning ball. The granulated photosphere darkens and reddens toward the limb. A few sunspot groups with dark umbrae and striated penumbrae ride differential rotation from east (left) to west (right). Cool loops, with knots of coronal rain sliding along them, stand bright above the limb as their active regions rotate into view, and cross the disc as dark filaments. The disc fills the height, and the eye goes to the loops at the limb and to the spots. The black sky around it stays empty. At 200×50 the disc is 92 cols × 46 rows with 54 empty cols on each side; at 80×24 it is 44 × 22 with 18 on each side.
- **Model**: the view is a composite: the photosphere in white light, loops and rim as in H-α. The disc is centred with radius `R = min(0.46·P, 0.36·W)` px on `Pixels` (P = 2h), with solar north up and east left. The viewer is fixed in inertial space, so rates are sidereal (from Earth the synodic rate is 0.986 °/day slower). B0, the heliographic latitude of the disc centre, is seeded in [−7.25°, 7.25°]. Each disc pixel gets an observer-frame unit normal n = (dx/R, −dy/R, √(1 − ·)) with μ = n_z, tabulated per resize. *Limb darkening*: `I(μ) = 1 − u(1 − μ)` with u = 0.6 (linear law, visible continuum). *Differential rotation*: `ω(φ) = A + B sin²φ + C sin⁴φ` with A = 14.713, B = −2.396, C = −1.787 °/day sidereal (Snodgrass & Ulrich 1990). Time runs at 0.3 day per simulated second, so the equator turns in 81.6 s and latitude 60° in 100.7 s. Every surface feature advances its longitude by exactly `ω(φ)·0.3·dt` (closed form). *Granulation*: multiplicatively weighted Worley cells from `N_g = clamp(round(4πR²/s_g²), 64, 2400)` feature points on the sphere. The spacing at disc centre is `s_g = clamp(0.085 R, 3.6, 5)` px (3.91 px at R = 46, ≈ 45× the real ≈ 1.3 Mm spacing, which would be sub-pixel), so a cell spans 2–3 terminal cells and lanes read as polygons rather than a blocky maze. Each point carries (φ, λ), a lifetime of 6–10 s and a weight w that fades in and out over 1.5 s by smoothstep; a dead point respawns uniformly on the sphere. Per frame, points with μ > 0 are projected and counting-sorted into a screen bucket grid of cell size s_g. Each disc pixel takes F1 and F2 from the sites within s_g (its 3×3 buckets), widening the query to 1.5 s_g for the few pixels whose F2 reaches s_g, with squared distances scaled by m² where `m = 1 + 1.5(1 − w)`. Newborn cells therefore grow from a point and dying ones shrink, with no popping. The pattern is `g = 0.55·smoothstep(0, 0.3 s_g, F2 − F1) + 0.45·w₁·(1 − min(F1/0.9 s_g, 1))²`, giving bright granule cores and dark intergranular lanes. It is applied as `I *= 1 + (G − 1)·gate` with the granulation factor `G = 1 + C(μ)(2g − 1)`, `C = 0.12·√μ·smoothstep(0.1, 0.2, μ)`, and `gate` the product of the spots' photosphere weights (0 inside a spot, 1 outside, see *Spots*): convection is suppressed in umbrae and penumbrae, so a spot is a clean dark ring on the mottle rather than a dot on tiles. The real rms contrast is ≈ 0.13 at 500 nm and falls toward the limb; 0.12 keeps granule cores below the ramp's clip, so the mottle is a fine texture under the spots and loops, and the smoothstep avoids a hard ring. Worley is skipped for μ < 0.1. Granules evolve on display time, because real 5–10 min lifetimes would flicker at 0.3 d/s, but they rotate with the surface, so the texture itself shows the sphere turning and shearing. *Active regions* (3): mean latitude seeded in 10–25° (Spörer), ±5° per region, either hemisphere, ~120° apart in longitude. The first region starts at −78° (just inside the east limb) with its tallest loop at h = 0.32 R, so loops show at t = 0. Each region is a bipolar pair tilted by Joy's law, γ = 0.5|φ|, with the leading spot equatorward and the spots 2δ = 13–15° apart, so the pair's blended penumbrae (out to 1.07 ρ_p each) never touch: no δ-spots, which are a few % of real groups. Penumbral radius ρ_p is 4–6° for the leading spot and 2.5–4° for the following one, which is absent 40 % of the time. The region rotates rigidly at ω(φ_AR). *Spots*: angular distance from a spot centre is β ≈ √(2(1 − n·s)) (chord, no trig), with q = β/ρ_p. The umbra is 0.22 for q ≤ 0.375 and blends by smoothstep to the penumbra over q ∈ [0.375, 0.525]. Its radius of 0.45 ρ_p gives U/(U+P) ≈ 0.20 and U/P ≈ 0.25, as observed. The penumbra is `0.70 + 0.08 cos(nψ + ψ₀)` (a little darker than the observed ≈ 0.75–0.85, so that with the granulation gated out the ring stands about one ramp step below the intergranular lanes, 0.88, at 80×24) with `n = clamp(round(2π ρ_px / 3), 6, 24)` radial filaments (one per 3 px of penumbral circumference, so they never alias below a pixel; real penumbrae hold hundreds) and blends to 1 over q ∈ [0.93, 1.07]. The umbra is displaced toward disc centre by 0.10 ρ_p sin θ in the sky plane: the Wilson depression, ≈ 2.5× the real d/ρ_p ≈ 0.04 (≈ 10× in absolute depth, since spots are drawn 2–3× oversize). Lifted onto the sphere it grows as 0.10 tan θ, so the centre-side penumbra narrows toward the limb and is covered below μ ≈ 0.2 (≈ 0.08 at the real depth). Faculae brighten outside the spots as `I *= 1 + 0.35(1 − μ)·exp(−(β/3ρ_p)²)`, which shows only near the limb, as observed. Each spot acts only within its own facular cap `cos β ≥ cos 6.5 ρ_p` (where the Gaussian is < 1 %), and faculae never brighten a spot pixel, whatever the spot order. The only per-pixel trig is ψ inside penumbrae. Small discs floor the drawn spot size: each spot is drawn at `ρ_eff = max(ρ_p, min(2.0 px/R, sep/2.2))`, with sep the pair's angular separation, so at 80×24 (R ≈ 22 px, where a 2.5° following spot is ≈ 1 px in penumbral radius and its umbra under half a pixel) every spot is ≥ 2 px with an umbra ≥ 0.9 px and reads as umbra and penumbra. A floored pair's penumbrae still never touch: at 80×24 2δ ≥ 13° > 1.07·(6° + 5.2°), and on smaller discs, where both spots may reach sep/2.2, 2·1.07/2.2 < 1. At 200×50 the floor (2.49°) is under every ρ_p, so no spot is enlarged. This is a display exaggeration like the granule spacing, and it applies to the umbra, filaments, Wilson shift and faculae alike. *Loops*: each region has 3 nested field lines of a horizontal point dipole buried at depth `d_b = 2δ` (in R) below its midpoint, so B_z peaks under the spots. A field line is `p(θ') = c_d + L sin²θ'(cos θ' â + sin θ' ẑ)` with `L = d_b + h`; the apex heights are `h_max·{1, 0.72, 0.48}` (floored at 0.04 R) with `h_max` = 0.20–0.32 R (first region 0.32 R): 139–223 Mm, ≈ 1–1.5× the tallest observed cool H-α and coronal-rain loops, a stated display exaggeration like the granules and the Wilson shift, so the arcade is the focal point at 80×24 (≈ 7 px) and still fits the empty columns beside the disc. The three lines lie in one vertical plane through the region midpoint, turned 45–65° (either sense) from the spot axis: a sheared arcade over the inversion line. That is a display compromise, less sheared than real filament channels (≳ 70°), so the plane stays inclined to the line of sight at either limb and the loops show as nested arches rather than edge-on spikes. Loops with an apex below 2.5 px are not drawn, and on discs with R < 30 px (80×24 is 22) the middle arch is dropped so the outer and inner arches keep a black gap. Footpoints (|p| = R) are found by bisection at spawn. The 56 samples between them are stored in region-local coordinates and rotated each frame through an orthographic `geom::Camera`. Each loop carries 2 knots of coronal rain (Gaussian width 0.05 in σ) on display time (like the granules). A knot condenses within 0.1 σ of the apex and falls down one leg, its distance `a = |σ − ½|` from the apex growing as `ȧ = (0.3 + 1.4a)/T` (T = 5–9 s; closed form `rain_fall`, exact over any step split): slow near the top, accelerating down the leg. It fades in after condensing and out before the footpoint, over 0.05 σ, then condenses again. A sample is *Emission* if its projected radius is ≥ R, and *Filament* if it lies inside the disc with z ≥ 0. Otherwise the sample is *Hidden*. A Filament sample standing higher than 0.35 of its loop's apex darkens its pixel idempotently to `min(level, seq(0.12 + 0.78·0.6·I_disc))`, except inside a spot (photosphere weight < ½), so the thread bridges a group's spots without slashing across their penumbrae: the low legs would trace a ring around the spots, while the elevated arches overlap as one short dark thread along the loop plane, still well above the umbrae (0.22). Cool H-α loops are thus bright against the sky and absorb against the disc. A region that has reached +150° and is fully hidden (all spots μ < 0, no Emission or Filament sample) respawns 0–60° further on with new parameters, so nothing pops on the visible disc. *Chromosphere*: a 1 px rim at R < r ≤ R + 0.8, plus spicules of height `s_h·max(0, ½sin(n₁ϑ + 1.3t + φ₁) + ½sin(n₂ϑ − 0.9t + φ₂))` px with `s_h = min(0.035 R, 1.6)` (real spicules stand ≈ 0.014 R☉; fixed in pixels they would break a small disc's limb into stray pixels and notches). Here n₁ = round(2πR/3) and n₂ = round(2πR/2) are whole numbers of lobes (so the pattern closes around the limb), and ϑ is the limb angle.
- **Motion**: granules boil with 6–10 s lives while the surface turns at ≈ 3.5 px/s at disc centre (200×50). Features foreshorten toward the limb, so the sphere reads in 3-D. An equatorial spot crosses the disc in ≈ 41 s while high-latitude features visibly lag. Loops rise over the east limb, cross the disc as dark filaments and set over the west limb. Rain knots condense near the loop tops and fall, accelerating, down one leg, and spicules flicker. The label's day counter ticks 0.3 per second. For GEN-R9, rim, spicule and knot pixels change half-block occupancy within 1 s; interior colour changes alone do not change `to_lines` in Unicode mode.
- **Resize**: there is no simulation lattice. Heliographic state survives any resize untouched: regions, knots, granule (φ, λ, age), clock and B0. Resize rebuilds only the view: disc normals, the rim annulus list (pixel, r, ϑ), the bucket grid, the spicule lobe counts and `Pixels`. The granule pool changes only when N_g moves by > 10 % (hysteresis). It is then truncated, or topped up with points that fade in (capacity 2400 is reserved at build). For R < 4 px, granulation and loops are skipped, and nothing panics at 1×1.
- **Inks**: the disc, rim, spicules and loops use `Seq` with `v = clamp(0.12 + 0.78·I, 0, 1)` via `ink::seq`. On the astro inferno ramp that gives: disc centre ≈ 0.90 (pale yellow); limb 0.43 (crimson, so the ramp shows true limb reddening); umbra ≈ 0.29 (violet); penumbra ≈ 0.67; rim 0.55; spicules 0.42–0.50; loops 0.60 (orange, above the rim and spicules but under the penumbra), with rain knots adding up to 0.28 (≈ 0.88, pale yellow) as the moving highlight; filaments 0.6× the disc behind them. The label is `Text` 150. No other roles are used.
- **ASCII**: the `Pixels::blit` closure averages the lit `Seq` levels of the pair. On the disc (cell centre inside R) the glyph is `#` for ≥ 0.92, `*` for ≥ 0.84, `+` for ≥ 0.72, `=` for ≥ 0.58, `:` for ≥ 0.46, `.` for ≥ 0.36, and otherwise a written space. Granules read as `#*` mottling, the limb as `:.`, a spot as a `=` ring around a blank umbra, and filaments one step darker. Off the disc the cell takes the brighter pixel: a knot core (≥ 0.60 + 0.9·0.28 ≈ 0.85) is `o`; a cell holding a pixel within 1 px of the limb is the one-cell rim `'`; a loop (≥ the rim/loop midpoint 0.575) is `.`; anything dimmer, including spicule-only cells, stays blank so loops stand clear of the rim. Empty cells stay untouched.
- **Label**: `day 12.4  Teff 5772 K` (`Text` 150), right-aligned on the last row (GEN-R13). The elapsed days (0.3·t, one decimal) are written into a stack buffer, and 5772 K is the IAU nominal effective temperature. The Teff part is dropped first when it would reach the title (`title::readout_room`), then the whole label; there is none when w < 30 or h < 10.
- **Perf** at 200×50: update ≈ 0.37 ms. Ages and longitudes cost ≈ 0.02 ms (1739 granules, ω precomputed per point; respawns; 3 regions and 18 knots); the rest is the rasterisation, which runs in `update`: projecting and bucketing ~870 visible granules ≈ 0.05 ms; disc ≈ 6.6k px × ~12 weighted-distance candidates ≈ 0.25 ms; spots and faculae ≈ 0.03 ms (3 dot products per pixel with early reject); loops (504 samples) and rim ≈ 0.02 ms. Render (blit and label) ≈ 0.03 ms. Total ≈ 0.4 ms (measured 0.42 ms in criterion at load ≈ 14, 2026-09-27), bounded at any size by the 2400-point cap, with no allocation after resize.
- **Acceptance**: `SUN-A1` `limb_intensity(μ)` is 1 at μ = 1, 0.4 at μ = 0 and 0.7 at μ = 0.5, and strictly increasing; in a 200×50 render at t = 0 with regions off (test hook), the mean `Seq` level of disc pixels with μ > 0.8 exceeds that of pixels with μ < 0.3 by ≥ 60 levels (≈ 84 expected); `SUN-A2` `omega_deg_per_day(φ)` is 14.713 at 0°, 14.002 ± 0.001 at ±30° and 11.911 ± 0.001 at ±60°, even in φ and strictly decreasing in |φ|; the equatorial period is 81.56 ± 0.01 s at 0.3 d/s; over 10 s of updates split three ways (40 × 0.25, 600 × 1/60, mixed), a region and a granule that do not respawn in the window each advance by ω(φ)·3 days within 1e-9°; `SUN-A3` `spot_intensity(q, ψ)` is 0.22 for q ≤ 0.375, has azimuthal mean 0.70 ± 0.005 on [0.525, 0.93] with |modulation| ≤ 0.08, and is 1 for q ≥ 1.07; its azimuthal mean is non-decreasing in q, crosses the umbra–penumbra midpoint 0.46 at q = 0.45 ± 0.005 (U/(U+P) = 0.2025) and the penumbra–photosphere midpoint 0.85 at q = 1.00 ± 0.005; `wilson_offset(ρ_p, θ)` points toward disc centre with magnitude 0.10 ρ_p sin θ (1e-9); the filament count is `clamp(round(2π ρ_px/3), 6, 24)` and the azimuthal mean stays 0.70 ± 0.005 for every count in [6, 24], and a rendered 200×50 spot's filaments stand ≥ 2 px apart at mid-penumbra unless held at 6; faculae never brighten a spot pixel (and the result is independent of spot order), and a spot changes no pixel outside its facular cap `cos(6.5 ρ_p)`; a pixel within q ≤ 0.9 of a spot shades identically under any granulation factor; over seeds 0–199 at 40×12, 80×24 and 200×50, every shown spot is drawn at its own `ρ_eff` (above), which at 200×50 is its own ρ_p and at 80×24 gives an umbra of ≥ 0.85 px radius, and every pair with both spots present is strictly more than 1.07(ρ_eff,lead + ρ_eff,follow) apart; `SUN-A4` every loop sample satisfies `|p − c_d| = L sin²θ'` (relative 1e-9) in its vertical plane; both footpoints lie on the photosphere (||p| − R| < 1e-6 R) and every interior sample lies above it; a field line's apex height is L − d_b (1e-9 R); the two footpoints lie on opposite sides of the region midpoint along â; each region's 3 loops lie in one vertical plane through the region midpoint (1e-9), turned 45–65° from the spot axis, with strictly decreasing apex heights and footpoint spans; at 80×24 the first region's tallest apex is ≥ 7 px, every drawn loop clears 2.5 px, the middle arch is dropped and the outer and inner arches stay ≥ 2 px apart, and loop pixels clear of the limb lie in [rim + 0.03, 0.60 + 0.28 + 0.01] with the loop body under the penumbra; in ASCII at 80×24 off-disc cells are only `'` (on the one-cell rim), `.`, `o` or blank, with ≥ 8 loop cells clear of the rim; rain: `rain_fall` composes over split steps (1e-12) and moves a knot > 2.5× faster at a = 0.4 than at the apex, a condensing knot starts within 0.1 σ of the apex at weight 0, and a falling knot moves monotonically toward its footpoint, where its weight is 0; `SUN-A5` `classify(p)` returns Emission iff the projected radius is ≥ R, Filament iff the point is inside the disc with z ≥ 0, and Hidden otherwise; in a 200×50 render with loops on versus off (test hook), off-disc pixels differ only within 1 px of a projected Emission sample, and no on-disc pixel is brighter with loops on; over 80 s at 200×50 (seeds 11 and 42), every on-disc pixel darkened by loops lies within 1 px of a Filament sample standing higher than 0.35 of its loop's apex and outside every spot (photosphere weight ≥ ½), and some filament crosses the disc; `SUN-A6` over 2000 seeded spawns the two spots of a region are 13–15° apart and more than 1.07(ρ_lead + ρ_follow) apart; at 80×24 over 1200 × `update(0.25)` with no renders (< 2 s in debug), exactly 3 regions exist at every step, all with |latitude| in [5°, 30°]; at t = 0 the first region is at −78° with ≥ 1 Emission sample east of the disc; every respawn happens at relative longitude ≥ 150° with all spots μ < 0 and no Emission or Filament sample; `SUN-A7` after warm-up at 200×50 (R = 46 px) `granule_spacing` is 3.6 px at R = 10, 3.91 at R = 46 and 5 at R = 200, and the pool holds exactly N_g = 1739 points, never grows, and every lifetime is in [6, 10] s; with regions off, the rms of I/limb_intensity(μ) about its mean (from `Seq` levels mapped back to I) is in [0.06, 0.18] for μ > 0.8 and < 0.6× that value for 0.15 < μ < 0.3; `granule_pattern` is < 0.26 whenever F2 − F1 ≤ 0.1 s_g and F1 ≥ 0.45 s_g (w₁ ≤ 1), and ≥ 0.55 whenever F2 − F1 ≥ 0.3 s_g; the bucketed Worley query returns exact F1/F2 within its 1.5 s_g reach (a site two buckets over is found) and F2 = the reach for a lone site.

### strange_attractors  (Forces & Waves, M10)
- **Intent**: a slowly turning gallery of five classic chaotic flows: Lorenz's butterfly, Aizawa's shell, Thomas's labyrinth, Halvorsen's propeller and Rössler's band. Each is drawn by a swarm of particles leaving short glowing threads on the braille lattice, so the flow itself builds the shape instead of a plotted curve. Every 12 s the swarm flows into the next attractor, with no cut and no fade to black. Each attractor is framed to its own projected extent, so it fills about 0.88 of the height (or of the width, if that is tighter). Each is also seen from its own best elevation: Halvorsen from above its 3-fold axis, so the three blades read, with the frame following the blades so the figure stays centred as they turn. Threads are continuous at every size and the particle count grows only linearly with the lattice, so 200×50 gains resolution but not clutter. The eye goes first to the bright dense cores (the Lorenz wing eyes, the Aizawa tube), then follows the threads outward. A single corner label is the only text.
- **Model**: model time in *tu*. Gallery: Lorenz (1963) `ẋ = σ(y−x), ẏ = x(ρ−z) − y, ż = xy − βz`, σ = 10, ρ = 28, β = 8/3. Aizawa (1982, Langford-type torus flow) `ẋ = (z−b)x − dy, ẏ = dx + (z−b)y, ż = c + az − z³/3 − (x²+y²)(1+ez) + fzx³`, (a,b,c,d,e,f) = (0.95, 0.7, 0.6, 3.5, 0.25, 0.1). Thomas (1999) `ẋ = sin y − bx` (cyclic), b = 0.18 (λ₁ ≈ 0.038). The popular b = 0.19 is a limit cycle, and at the often-quoted b = 0.1998 and 0.208186 orbits stay on one of several coexisting off-centre attractors. Halvorsen (Sprott 2003) `ẋ = −ax − 4y − 4z − y²` (cyclic), a = 1.27 (λ₁ ≈ 0.82); the popular a = 1.89 is periodic (λ₁ ≈ 0). Rössler (1976) `ẋ = −y − z, ẏ = x + ay, ż = b + z(x − c)`, a = b = 0.2, c = 5.7 (λ₁ ≈ 0.071). Starts are fixed in-basin constants, never random: Lorenz (1, 1, 1), Aizawa (0.1, 0, 0), Thomas (0.1, 0, 0), Halvorsen (−1, 0, 0), Rössler (1, 1, 0). Aizawa has a coexisting non-chaotic attractor: from (0.2, 0.1, 0.5), λ₁ ≈ 0 and the z extent is only 82 % of the chaotic one. Each particle is advanced in f64 with `ode::integrate` (RK4) at `h_max` = 0.01 / 0.02 / 0.1 / 0.005 / 0.02 tu, at a model rate `r_k` = 0.33 / 0.69 / 6.5 / 0.31 / 1.6 tu per simulated second. These rates are measured so the mean normalised speed is 1.2 box half-widths/s for every attractor (the peak is ≈ 9/s in Rössler's fold). That is ≤ 2 substeps per particle per 1/60 s. Normalisation: `x̃ = Q_k(x − c_k)/s_k`, where `c_k` is the reference box centre and `s_k` its largest half-extent: Lorenz (0, 0, 24.5)/26.0, Aizawa (0, 0, 0.74)/1.49, Thomas 0/4.2, Halvorsen −3.54·(1,1,1)/9.8, Rössler (1.17, −1.47, 11.36)/11.35. World position `p = x̃/ρ_k` with `ρ_k = max|x̃|` = 1.24 / 1.02 / 1.13 / 1.32 / 1.38, so `|p| ≤ 1` and the perspective stays bounded. `Q_k` maps native z (Lorenz, Aizawa, Rössler) or the (1,1,1) axis (Thomas, Halvorsen) onto the camera's up axis +y, so yaw spins the last two about their 3-fold axis. Ensemble: N = 1024 particles per attractor, sampled from one scout trajectory. The scout starts at the fixed start plus a seeded 10⁻³ Gaussian jitter, burns in for 40 s·r_k, and takes a sample every 0.15 s·r_k. Samples are stored at bit-reversed indices, so every power-of-two prefix is spread along the scout. Two slots hold the current and next ensemble. The next slot is filled during the first 2 s of each hold (≤ 400 scout steps per update, with any remainder finished at the morph boundary); a scout that escapes restarts from the fixed start offset by 10⁻³·i on every axis at its i-th restart, burn-in included, so no transient sample is kept; after 8 restarts it fills the rest of its slot by repeating the samples it has (the start if none), so filling always terminates (unreachable with today's in-basin starts, but `run` is called with an unbounded budget). Morph: at morph start each prefix is put in pairing order: 8 equal-count azimuth sectors about the vertical axis, each cut into 4 equal-count height bands, each band ordered by cylindrical radius (in place with `sort_unstable_by`, ties broken by index). Equal counts keep sector j of one attractor paired with sector j of the other however unevenly their mass is spread (Lorenz's wings). The next slot's prefix is then permuted onto the current slot's indices, so the points on screen are never re-indexed, and point i pairs a_i ↔ b_i. Both slots integrate during the morph, so the pairs decorrelate within about a second, and a plain lerp of two uncorrelated clouds is narrower (its spread falls to `√((1−w)² + w²)` ≈ 0.71 at w = ½): the swarm knotted to 0.65–0.83 of its height mid-morph. So the displayed point is `p_i,j = m_j + g_j·(lerp(a_i,j, b_i,j, w) − m_j)` per world axis j, with `w = smoothstep(s)`, `m_j = lerp(mean a_j, mean b_j, w)`, and `g_j` clamped to [1, √2] so that the blend's spread `(⟨|p_j − m_j|^16⟩)^{1/16}` equals the lerp of the two ensembles' spreads. The factor is measured after every morph step (O(N_eff), allocation-free), and the endpoints w = 0 and 1 are exact. A variance match (power 2) overshot: the extremes decorrelate last (pairs at the top of both stay there while the bulk mixes) and a blend has thinner tails than the shapes it averages, so Aizawa → Thomas spilled past the frame (80×24: 5.6 edge-row cells per morph frame, against 0.07 in holds, and 0.7 at power 16). One gain for all axes turns uncorrelated extremes into corners the shapes never have (4.8), and an order statistic jumped 0.3 world units in one update when a fast particle overtook. At s = 1 the next slot becomes current. Blow-up guard: a particle that is non-finite or has `|x̃|∞ > 3` is replaced by a clone of particle `(i + N_eff/2) mod N_eff` of the simulated prefix, or, if that has escaped too, of the next finite particle (the fixed start only if all have escaped, since it lies off the attractor, e.g. at Lorenz's origin saddle), plus a seeded 10⁻⁶·s_k jitter, so the clone separates by chaos instead of shadowing its source forever. Camera: `geom::Camera` with yaw `2πt/36 s`, pitch `p_k + 0.10 sin(2πt/29 s)` with `p_k` = 0.20 / 0.48 / 0.55 / 1.35 / 0.50 (Halvorsen from nearly above its 3-fold axis, so the three blades read; Aizawa, the seed-42 opening, from far enough above that its shell's loops open into rings around the tube instead of a tangle, which at 0.35 made it the least legible member at 80×24), and distance 4.5. Framing uses each attractor's 99.5th-percentile projected box over the camera loop (yaw every 5°, pitch p_k ± 0.10), perspective included, in world units with screen y down: `Frame_k = (x, top, bottom, swing)` with `|x|/ASPECT ≤ x` and `top ≤ y − swing·cos 3ψ ≤ bottom` at camera yaw ψ: Lorenz (0.90, −0.66, 0.66, 0), Aizawa (1.01, −0.82, 0.94, 0), Thomas (0.87, −1.01, 0.98, 0), Halvorsen (0.94, −0.80, 0.92, 0.12), Rössler (0.65, −0.74, 1.02, 0). The swing follows Halvorsen's blades: seen from above they turn like a triangle, and every 120° of yaw a tip points up, then down, swinging the box midpoint by ±0.17 half-heights while the centroid stays put. A fixed centre left 4 blank rows on one side and none on the other within one hold at 80×24 (9 of 49 at 200×50), and following it also narrows the frame from 1.89 to 1.72. The others swing ≤ 0.06 half-heights with no 3-fold harmonic. The stage is the rows above the label (all rows when it is hidden). The fit is `scale_k = min(0.88·rows/(bottom − top), 0.92·w/(2·ASPECT·x))` rows per world unit, and the frame's midpoint `(top + bottom)/2 + swing·cos 3ψ` sits on the stage's middle row, `rows/2`. Rare excursions beyond the 99.5 % box may be clipped at the frame edge. During a morph `p_k` and the frame midpoint are lerped by w and `scale_k` log-lerped. The horizontal centre is `w/2`, and dot coordinates are cell coordinates × (2, 4). Density: `Grid<f32>` at `Dots::dot_size()`. Each update multiplies it by `e^{−dt/τ}` (τ = 0.1 s), then deposits `w = (0.6 + 0.4·near)·(1 − 0.2·sin πw_morph)` (near = (depth + 1)/2; the second factor dims a morph lightly at its middle, where the lerped velocity already shortens and fades the blended threads) on every dot of the segment from each particle's previous projected dot to its current one (`geom::line`, start dot excluded). A segment longer than 16 dots deposits only its end dot; this happens after a morph start, a blow-up replacement, a resize or a large dt. A dot is lit when `D ≥ D_min = 0.08`, so threads are ≈ 0.25 s of flow long and unbroken at any size. Because thread length in dots grows with the lattice, the prefix grows only linearly: `N_eff = clamp(pow2_floor(6·min(h, w/2)), 64, 1024)`, which is 128 at 80×24 and 256 at 200×50 and keeps coverage near 30 % of the silhouette. Chaos reference: Lorenz λ₁ = 0.9056 (Viswanath 1998; Sprott 2003), with Σλ = −(σ + 1 + β) = −41/3.
- **Motion**: particles stream at ≈ 1.2 half-widths/s and the camera turns 10°/s, so the frame changes from the first step (GEN-R9). The Timeline is `hold` 8 s plus `morph` 4 s per attractor, in the order Lorenz → Aizawa → Thomas → Halvorsen → Rössler, a 60 s loop. The run starts at the beginning of a seeded attractor's hold, so every 15 s scene visit (the default `interval`) includes a morph. During a morph the cloud flows as one body, keeping its size: the old silhouette dissolves while the new one condenses, framing and elevation glide to the new attractor's, and the label switches at s = 0.5.
- **Resize**: no simulation state depends on the display (ADR-0012 needs no lattice here). Ensembles, timeline, time and camera phase are kept. A resize reallocates only the density grid and `Dots` (zeroed), recomputes centre, scale and `N_eff`, and forgets each particle's previous dot.
